# HapieCoin · decision log (ADR)

One entry per decision. Newest last. Each entry: context, decision, consequences. This file is indexed by graphify together with `spec/`, `GAPS.md` and the mock sources so every agent can find why things are the way they are.

## ADR-001 · Product name and reference (2026-09-07)
- Context: the product being built is the user's own site; coingreeks.com is only the reference/competitor that was cloned for parity.
- Decision: the product is **HapieCoin** (hapiecoin.com). The v1 clone keeps the CoinGreeks name deliberately as the reference copy; everything else uses HapieCoin.
- Consequences: package scope `@hapiecoin/*`, all copy and artifacts use HapieCoin.

## ADR-002 · Two mocks kept side by side (2026-09-06)
- Context: user wants to compare the faithful clone with the improved version and be able to fall back.
- Decision: `mockup-clone` (v1, faithful) and `mockup-v2` (improved, Obsidian Desk design) are separate builds with separate URLs; neither is edited to become the other.
- Consequences: v1 answers "what did the original do"; v2 answers "what should HapieCoin look and feel like".

## ADR-003 · Design system: Obsidian Desk, dark-first (2026-09-06)
- Context: three directions were built (Obsidian Desk, Quant Paper, Mission Control); research on options tools favoured a dark instrument with amber "market is here" accent.
- Decision: Obsidian Desk tokens app-wide; light theme is a first-class variant; amber only for spot/ATM/primary action; green/red only for P&L and side.
- Consequences: tokens live in one file; switching to Quant Paper later is a token change, not a redesign. Launch default theme may flip to light if usability sessions show daytime retail users.

## ADR-004 · Stack (2026-09-04, confirmed 2026-09-07)
- Decision: pnpm + Turborepo monorepo; Next.js 16 / React 19 / TypeScript / Tailwind v4 / shadcn; TanStack Query + Zustand + TanStack Table/Virtual; uPlot canvas charts; shared pricing package (Black-76) in a Web Worker; Hono API on Node 22 with Zod + OpenAPI + Drizzle; dedicated market-data gateway on uWebSockets.js; PostgreSQL 17 + TimescaleDB; Redis 7; BullMQ; Better Auth; Razorpay; Docker behind Cloudflare.
- Alternatives considered: Vite SPA + Astro marketing site (acceptable if the team prefers no server components); Bun (rejected for a system that places real orders); microservices beyond web/API/gateway (rejected, adds latency and ops cost).

## ADR-005 · Monorepo with independently deployable apps (2026-09-07)
- Decision: one repository; apps/web, apps/api, apps/gateway, apps/workers; packages/pricing, schema, venues, ui, config.
- Consequences: one PR changes pricing + API + UI atomically; shared Zod schemas prevent contract drift; one CI with budgets. Split only if a separate team owns a product on its own cadence.

## ADR-006 · Chain driven by the exchange instrument list (2026-09-07)
- Context: review found v2 showed only fixed-step strikes; real Delta expiries have irregular ladders (200/400/600 daily, 500/1000 weekly, 1000 monthly; 27–52 strikes).
- Decision: strikes always come from the venue's instrument list per expiry; never from a step constant. Calls and puts render as one table with a fixed centre strike column so vertical and horizontal scroll are shared.
- Consequences: first acceptance tests of Phase 2 (see GAPS.md #1, #2).

## ADR-007 · Traceability as the definition of done (2026-09-07)
- Decision: every feature has an ID in `spec/traceability.json`; a phase closes only when all its IDs have passing tests and visual diffs within tolerance; inferred items are confirmed with the product owner before implementation; review gaps become new IDs.

## ADR-008 · Knowledge graph for project memory (2026-09-07)
- Decision: graphify indexes this repository (spec, docs, GAPS.md, mock sources, and later the code) so agents query structure instead of re-reading files. Decisions are recorded here, not only in chat, so the graph can hold them.

## ADR-009 · Claude Code environment (2026-09-07)
- Context: development will run through Claude Code; the agent must not hallucinate symbols, claim untested success, touch the reference clone, or leak secrets. Four public setups were compared (router CLAUDE.md + memory + rules + commands; four anti-hallucination layers; six real-project skills; Anthropic's four-part playbook).
- Decision: project-scoped setup in the repo: a 39-line router `CLAUDE.md` whose first sections are honesty rules and a verification protocol; `.claude/memory/` (product, system, active context); `.claude/rules/` with path-scoped rules; `fact-checker` and `code-reviewer` subagents; skills `/plan`, `/review`, `/commit`, `/checkpoint`, `/frontend-design`, `/new-skill`; Node hooks that inject state at session start, deny destructive commands and protected paths, feed type/lint/syntax errors back after every edit, and run tests before the agent may finish. Documented in `docs/CLAUDE-SETUP.md`.
- Consequences: guidance lives in rules, enforcement lives in hooks and `permissions.deny`; `mockup-clone/` and `.env*` cannot be edited by the agent; decisions and gaps are file-based so graphify and future sessions see them. MCP servers and CI are added with the monorepo scaffold.

## ADR-010 · The 11 inferred behaviours, decided (2026-09-07)
- Context: 11 behaviours were reconstructed from the original's bundle (screens behind login). The product owner delegated the decisions ("apply the best").
- Decisions (full acceptance text in `spec/decisions-overrides.json`, applied to `spec/traceability.json`):
  - HC-TR-089 Trade All → Live becomes an explicit batch selector with per-strategy margin checks and one preview; nothing converts silently.
  - HC-TR-025 Builder state is kept per asset; switching assets never clears legs, so no confirmation dialog.
  - HC-TR-047 Drafts can be archived from the list with Undo; archive is a soft state with Restore.
  - HC-TR-078 Draft details: Load in Builder / Activate / Duplicate; archived: Load in Builder / Restore / Duplicate; "Final" performance only for archived.
  - HC-AD-049 Per-user lot sizes live in the user drawer's Limits tab, validated and audit-logged.
  - HC-AD-058 Bulk pay requires a payment reference, shows total and count, exports the batch CSV, audit-logged.
  - HC-AD-090 Quick-view drawer stays, plus a full page /admin/users/:id for deep edits and history.
  - HC-SH-074 Tour step 12 wording approved; steps 10–12 switch the left tab to Paper.
  - HC-AC-055 Referrals load error: inline panel with Retry plus toast; stale data kept visible with a note.
  - HC-AD-067 Banner image required in production (PNG/JPG/WebP ≤ 5 MB, 1200×630 recommended) with live preview; no gradient fallback.
  - HC-AD-070 Banners error state with Retry and "last loaded data" notice.
- Consequences: three new build items (per-asset builder state, batch selector, full user page) added to GAPS.md; the matrix has 0 open inferred items.

## ADR-011 · Toolchain pins for Phase 1 (2026-09-07)
- Context: `pnpm add` resolved TypeScript 7.0.2, which typescript-eslint 8.69 rejects (`>=4.8.4 <6.1.0`); the shared tsconfig extended the repo root by a relative path that breaks through pnpm symlinks; ESLint 10 removed the `unix`/`compact` formatters the post-edit hook used.
- Decision: pin `typescript` to 6.0.3 (exact) until typescript-eslint supports 7.x; make `packages/config/tsconfig/base.json` self-contained and let each package set `rootDir`/`outDir`; lint plain JS files without type information; the hook uses ESLint's default formatter; the token guard applies to `.ts/.tsx` only (CSS hex is checked by the ui package's tokens test).
- Consequences: revisit the TypeScript pin when typescript-eslint publishes TS 7 support; Node 24 + pnpm 12 via corepack are the supported toolchain.

## ADR-012 · Pricing model matches Delta India marks (2026-09-07)
- Context: `packages/pricing` was validated against 307 live Delta tickers (all BTC expiries).
- Decision: options are priced with Black-76 on `spot_price` with zero rate and no futures basis (the forward implied by every mark equals spot within 0.007 %); Greek units follow Delta: vega per 1 vol point, theta per calendar day; settlement 12:00 UTC for BTC/ETH and 16:00 UTC for XAUT, always taken from the instrument's `settlementTime`; POP uses the unshifted ATM IV; time is a 365-day year.
- Consequences: marks reproduce to 3.3e-11 USD at the implied forward; residuals in the fixture (max 3.4 USD) come from mixed spot snapshots, not the model. Unit tests assert price ≤ max(1.5 %, 0.5 USD), IV ≤ 0.005 where vega ≥ 10, delta ≤ 0.02, gamma ≤ 10 %, vega ≤ 5 %, theta ≤ 10 % (T ≥ 1 day).

## ADR-013 · Local development database without Docker (2026-09-07)
- Context: Docker Desktop and WSL 2.7.13 were installed on 07 Sep 2026 but need a Windows restart; Phase 1 must not block on it.
- Decision: `apps/api` runs on PGlite (embedded Postgres via Drizzle) when `DATABASE_URL` is absent and on PostgreSQL 17 + Timescale when it is set; the gateway uses in-process pub/sub when `REDIS_URL` is absent and Redis otherwise. `docker-compose.yml` provides both services; the same migrations run on both.
- Consequences: unit and contract tests run everywhere without services; the Docker path is exercised in CI and before Phase 1 closes.

## ADR-014 · Market-data protocol and venue adapters (2026-09-07)
- Context: Delta labels `v2/ticker` on `socket.india.delta.exchange` as legacy and offers a compact `ticker` channel on `public-socket.india.delta.exchange`; both answered live on 07 Sep 2026. The venues package and the schema package chose different field names for Instrument/Quote.
- Decision: the venues client defaults to `v2/ticker` and parses both frame formats, so the migration is a config change; the gateway speaks `@hapiecoin/schema` messages (JSON frames in Phase 1, encoder kept pluggable for msgpack later) and converts venue shapes through `packages/venues/src/schema-adapter.ts`, which is the only place the two vocabularies meet.
- Consequences: chain strikes always originate from the instrument list (ADR-006) and are validated by `ChainSnapshot.parse` before leaving the gateway.

## ADR-015 · API security and account conventions in apps/api (2026-09-07)
- Context: Phase 1 `apps/api` (Hono, Better Auth, Drizzle) had to fix several details the spec leaves open.
- Decisions:
  - Exchange credentials: API key and secret are sealed separately with AES-256-GCM, each record carrying its own IV and auth tag (columns `api_key_iv/tag`, `api_secret_iv/tag`) because GCM must never reuse an IV under one key; only `api_key_masked` is ever serialised (`BrokerCredentialPublic` is strict).
  - Referral codes are `REF` + 7 RFC 4648 base32 characters (10 chars, no underscore) so they satisfy the shared `ReferralCode` schema (`^[A-Z0-9]{6,12}$`) and survive being typed from a screenshot; the inviter's code is accepted as `ref` at sign-up and stored in `users.referred_by` only when it matches an existing user.
  - Better Auth is mounted at `/v1/auth/*`; sign-up is email + password with mandatory OTP verification (`/email-otp/verify-email` signs the user in), OTP sign-in and OTP password reset use the `emailOTP` plugin (6 digits, 10 minutes); `role` is server-assigned (`input: false`).
  - Delta credential failures map to the SCREAMING_SNAKE envelope codes required by `ApiError` (`INVALID_API_KEY`, `IP_NOT_WHITELISTED_FOR_API_KEY`, 400) with the venue's original code in `details.deltaCode`; an unreachable venue is 502 `DELTA_UNAVAILABLE`.
  - Rate limits: 300 req/min per IP globally, 5 OTP sends / 15 min per email and per IP, 10 failed verifications / 15 min lock the email; counters live in one `RateStore` (memory, or Redis sorted sets when `REDIS_URL` is set) shared with Better Auth's `rateLimit.customStorage`.
  - `audit_log` is append-only at the database level (rules discard UPDATE/DELETE) and every snapshot passes through the log scrubber.
- Consequences: the web app must send `ref` (not `referralCode`) at sign-up and read `apiKeyMasked`; rotating `CREDENTIALS_ENC_KEY` requires re-sealing rows (no key-versioning yet, see GAPS #10).

## ADR-016 · Gateway transport: `ws` now, uWebSockets.js when a compatible build exists (2026-09-07)
- Context: ADR-004 named uWebSockets.js for `apps/gateway`. `uNetworking/uWebSockets.js#v20.51.0` installs from GitHub but ships prebuilt binaries for Node ABIs 108/115/127/131 only; the repo toolchain is Node 24 (ABI 137) and the loader refuses ("supports only Node.js versions 18, 20, 22 and 23"). uWS.js also requires glibc while the gateway image is `node:24-alpine` (musl).
- Decision: the gateway runs on the `ws` package behind a transport interface (`apps/gateway/src/transport/types.ts`: `SocketServerFactory`, `Connection` with `bufferedAmount()` for backpressure). Server logic, origin check, snapshot/delta protocol, coalescing, backpressure and shutdown are transport-agnostic and tested through a fake transport; the `ws` transport has its own socket-level tests. Frames are JSON text; the encoder is pluggable (`src/encoder.ts`) so msgpack can be negotiated per connection later without touching the schema.
- Consequences: revisit when uWS.js publishes ABI 137 binaries and the image moves to a glibc base (or the gateway is pinned to Node 22 in its own image); the swap is one `SocketServerFactory` implementation. Redis fan-out (`RedisPubSub`) assumes a single feed process per Redis until a feed-leader election exists (Phase 2).

## ADR-017 · Web app boundaries: same-origin API, client boundary for the design system, nonce CSP (2026-09-07)
- Context: `apps/web` (Next.js 16 App Router) talks to `apps/api` (Better Auth cookies, `/v1`) and `apps/gateway` (WebSocket). `@hapiecoin/ui` ships no `"use client"` directives and creates React contexts at import time, which server components reject. The brief asks for a nonce CSP and a 350 KB gzipped first-load budget on `/analyse`.
- Decisions:
  - Same-origin API: `next.config.ts` rewrites `/api/auth/*` and `/v1/*` to `API_URL`, so the Better Auth session cookie is first-party and the browser needs no CORS; server components forward the cookie to `/v1/me` for the protected-route check (`src/lib/auth/server.ts`).
  - Design system through one client boundary: server components import UI parts from `src/components/ui.ts` (`"use client"` re-exports); the root layout duplicates `themeInitScript("hapiecoin.theme", "dark")` as a string pinned by a unit test instead of importing the package.
  - CSP with a per-request nonce from `src/proxy.ts` (`script-src 'self' 'nonce-…' 'strict-dynamic'`, `frame-ancestors 'none'`); every route is therefore server-rendered on demand. `style-src 'unsafe-inline'` stays because Tailwind/Radix set inline styles; documented in `next.config.ts`.
  - Budget: the workspace (chain, virtualiser) and the settings dialogs load through `next/dynamic` after first paint; `scripts/check-budget.mjs` reads Turbopack's per-route client-reference manifest and fails above 350 KB gzipped (measured 326.7 KB on 07 Sep; the `nomodule` polyfill is reported separately, as Next does).
  - Phase 1 Delta sign-in (`/auth/delta`) shows the spinner, then explains that Delta login arrives with live trading and links back, rather than faking a login.
- Consequences: adding `"use client"` to `@hapiecoin/ui` entry points would remove the boundary file (GAPS #18). The app codes against the contracts that landed on 07 Sep in `apps/api/src/routes/*` and `apps/gateway/src/server.ts` (plan `state`, PATCH updates, `{ items }` credentials, `feed.expiries`); the mock API and fake gateway under `apps/web/test/` mirror them.

## ADR-018 · Auth mount path and browser access to gateway health (2026-09-07)
- Context: the first real web→api→gateway run failed on sign-up: the browser posted to `/api/auth/*` on the web origin while `apps/api` mounts Better Auth at `/v1/auth/*`; and the browser could not read the gateway's `/healthz` (no CORS), so the expiry list fell back to the env default.
- Decision: the browser always talks to its own origin (`/api/auth/*`, `/v1/*`) so the session cookie stays first-party; Next.js rewrites `/api/auth/:path*` → `${API_URL}/v1/auth/:path*` and `/v1/:path*` → `${API_URL}/v1/:path*`. The web test mock serves both prefixes so unit tests (direct) and e2e (through the rewrite) agree. The gateway answers CORS (`access-control-allow-origin` = the allowed web origin, `vary: origin`, OPTIONS 204) on its HTTP endpoints only for origins that pass the same check as the WebSocket upgrade.
- Consequences: one auth base path on the API (`/v1/auth`); any client other than the web app must use it directly. A real integration run is part of the Phase 1 exit gate from now on, because the unit/e2e mirrors could not catch this class of mismatch.

## ADR-019 · Client IP trust and the public auth origin (2026-09-07)
- Context: the Phase 1 review (GAPS #23–#27) found four defaults that were safe on a laptop and wrong in production: the client IP for rate limits was read from proxy headers anyone can set (or fell back to one shared "unknown" bucket), `BETTER_AUTH_URL` defaulted to the API origin so the Google OAuth callback set the session cookie where the web app never reads it, production could boot on in-memory PGlite or with the OTP-logging mailer, and the web referral input used a format the API never issues.
- Decisions:
  - Client IP = the socket peer address (`@hono/node-server` conninfo). Forwarded headers (`cf-connecting-ip`, `x-forwarded-for`, `x-real-ip`) are honoured only when the peer is listed in `TRUSTED_PROXY_IPS` (`*` trusts every peer, for local development and the test harness only). Better Auth's own limiter reads a single internal header, `x-hapiecoin-client-ip`, that the app sets after that check and strips from incoming requests.
  - `BETTER_AUTH_URL` defaults to `WEB_URL`: auth URLs are public URLs on the web origin, and the `/v1/:path*` rewrite carries the OAuth callback to the API, so the session cookie is first-party (extends ADR-018). Set it explicitly only when the API is exposed on its own public host.
  - Production (`NODE_ENV=production`) requires `DATABASE_URL` and `RESEND_API_KEY` next to the existing secrets and warns without `REDIS_URL`; `createMailer` refuses the capture mailer in production regardless of config.
  - Referral input is validated with the shared `ReferralCode` schema (`REF` + 7 base32, case-insensitive on entry); no client-side format of its own.
  - The gateway re-watches every held chain topic after each instrument refresh so strikes listed intraday receive quotes (the venue socket deduplicates subscriptions).
- Consequences: deployments behind Cloudflare or a load balancer must list the proxy's address (or use the platform's own client-IP header via a trusted hop) or every user shares the proxy's bucket, which is the safe failure. Local development keeps working through `env.example` (`TRUSTED_PROXY_IPS=127.0.0.1,::1`). Google sign-in has an end-to-end test against the mock provider; the real provider still needs the client id and secret.

## ADR-020 · Chain layout mechanics: one virtualiser, three aligned row lists, mirrored horizontal offset (2026-09-07)
- Context: GAPS #1 and #2 (every venue strike; calls and puts as one table with a fixed centre strike column and shared scrolling) are the first Phase 2 acceptance tests. A single wide grid cannot give the two sides independent-but-mirrored horizontal scroll, and two separate scrollers cannot share one vertical scroll without drift. Design pass: docs/design/options-chain.md.
- Decisions:
  - One vertical scroller (`data-testid=chain-scroll`) runs one TanStack virtualiser; each virtual row is rendered three times, into the calls track, the fixed 92 px strike column and the puts track, all positioned from the same `start`/`size`, so the rows can never misalign.
  - Horizontal position is one number, `inner` (distance scrolled away from the strike column). The calls track is translated by `max − inner`, the puts track by `inner`; the header parts use the same values, so a scroll on one side moves the other by the mirrored amount and a resize keeps the columns nearest the strike in view. Wheel (and Shift+wheel) on either track, ← / → keys, and two detached scrollbars in the footer drive it; the tracks themselves are `overflow: hidden` and use `translateX`, so nothing re-lays out per tick.
  - The header lives outside the vertical scroller (two rows: side bands with their basis, then column labels) and is therefore always pinned; columns are fixed at 68 px so the header and body share one measure.
  - The range control (±6 / ±12 / all, persisted as `chainRange`) slices the gateway snapshot around the ATM index; it never generates rows (ADR-006). The footer shows "n of N strikes", Σ call OI, Σ put OI and PCR from the visible rows.
  - Below 560 px the table shows one side at a time (calls | puts toggle) with the strike column pinned at the inner edge; the same virtualiser and offset model apply.
  - Keyboard inside the grid: J / K / ↑ / ↓ move the highlighted strike, Home / End jump, A recentres on ATM, E / Shift+E step the expiry, ← / → shift columns; the palette exposes "Chain: recentre on ATM" and "Chain: show all strikes". These keys match the v2 mock's shortcut list; B / S (legs) arrive with item 3.
- Consequences: optional columns (HC-WS-021) only change `CALL_COLUMNS` and the track width; hover controls (HC-WS-023) and leg pills (HC-WS-027) attach to the existing row parts. The stale state dims the whole table and shows "as of" in the footer; the ATM band remains the only amber element (ADR-003).

## ADR-021 · Chain column layout: order from the strike outward, persisted and normalised (2026-09-07)
- Context: HC-WS-010..014, 021 and 073 add optional columns and a Column Settings dialog to the item 1 layout (ADR-020). The mock keeps one ordered list and mirrors it; the gateway quote carries prices, IVs, OI, volume, sizes, Greeks, last and 24 h change but no candle fields.
- Decisions:
  - One persisted layout `{ v: 2, order, visible }` (`chainColumns` in the UI store). `order` is read from the strike outward: the first visible column touches the strike on the puts side and the same list is mirrored on the calls side. Every read goes through `normaliseLayout`, which drops unknown ids, appends new ones in default order and falls back to the default for other versions, so a stored layout never breaks a newer build.
  - Column definitions (id, label, header, group, unit title, fixed width) live in one file; the track width, header labels and body cells all derive from the visible list, so adding a column is one entry plus one cell renderer. Essentials = Ask · Mark · Bid · OI · Δ; the Greek trio Γ Θ ν toggles together (palette and dialog).
  - The dialog applies every change immediately (no Save) and persists through the store; reorder offers ▲ ▼ buttons for keyboard users and HTML5 drag as a convenience.
  - Open / High / Low are deferred (GAPS #31) and shown muted in the dialog rather than as dead switches.
- Consequences: hover controls and leg pills (item 3) attach to rows, not columns, so they are unaffected by the layout; the 24 h change cell is the only new coloured figure (signed change, green/red per ADR-003).

## ADR-022 · Strategy legs from the chain: local per-asset state, added at mark (2026-09-07)
- Context: HC-WS-023..028, HC-TR-017 and HC-TR-018 need legs before the Builder tab and the Phase 3 strategy API exist. ADR-010 already fixed that legs are kept per asset and never cleared by an asset switch.
- Decisions:
  - Legs live in the web UI store as `legs: Record<Underlying, StrategyLeg[]>`, persisted in the browser and normalised on read (unknown shapes dropped). The Phase 3 API replaces the storage, not the shape: a leg is `{ id, asset, kind, side, strike, expiry, lots, price, iv?, symbol, status, createdAt }` with price and IV copied from the live quote at the moment of adding; quantities are integer lots and the underlying amount is derived for display only, at the lot size's precision.
  - The chain adds legs at the mark price with the row control (hover or keyboard highlight) or the keys B / S (calls) and Shift+B / Shift+S (puts); the lot count is one persisted setting (`chainLots`, presets 1..1000, default 10) shared by the control, the keys and the details dialog.
  - The active-leg limit is 10 per asset strategy (HC-TR-017); the control disables at the limit and the keyboard path toasts the reason. The builder's "8 legs for a new strategy" rule arrives with the Builder tab.
  - The automatic centring on load or expiry change only scrolls; only the A key, the ATM button and the palette command highlight the ATM row, so no row control appears before the trader asks for one.
  - The details dialog omits the mock's 24 h sparkline until price history exists (GAPS #32).
- Consequences: the Builder tab (item 4) reads and edits the same list; legs added here already carry everything the pricing package needs (`kind`, `side`, `strike`, `expiry`, `price`, `iv`, lots × lot size). Nothing here talks to a venue; no order can be created from this state.

## ADR-023 · Builder, templates and the analysis pane: drafts stay in the browser until Phase 3, pricing runs in a worker, margin is estimated as the worst defined loss (2026-09-08)
- Context: Phase 2 item 4 closes the workspace: Builder tab (HC-TR-001..049 Phase 2 rows), 28 templates, the Payoff / Greeks / Ladder pane (HC-WS-032..064 Phase 2 rows) and the workspace chrome (HC-WS-001..007). The strategy API, paper and live trading and the broker margin call arrive in Phase 3, so nothing here may depend on a server that does not exist yet. Design pass: docs/design/workspace-builder.md.
- Decisions:
  - Saved strategies ("drafts", `drafts` in the UI store) are local to the browser and normalised on read, exactly like legs (ADR-022). Save draft / Update, Load, Archive / Restore and Delete work on that list; "Save & trade" saves the draft and says that paper / live trading arrives in Phase 3. The Phase 3 API replaces the storage, not the shape (`{ id, name, asset, status, templateName, legs, createdAt, updatedAt, archivedAt? }`), and "Refresh strategies" (HC-TR-043) and "Activate" (HC-TR-046) arrive with it.
  - Every figure in the Builder ticket and the pane comes from `@hapiecoin/pricing` (`analyze`, Black-76 on spot with r = 0, settlement 12:00 UTC for BTC / ETH and 16:00 UTC for XAUT). The engine runs in a Web Worker (`lib/pricing/worker.ts`) behind the package's `PricingClient`; requests are batched per animation frame and re-issued on a 30 s clock so time-to-expiry moves without re-pricing on every tick; the inline transport is the fallback (SSR, tests, no `Worker`). Pricing legs are `lots × lot size` units at the live mark in "Live prices" mode or the stored price in "Custom prices" mode; IV is the live mark IV, falling back to the IV copied when the leg was added, then 50 %.
  - Templates are placed on the venue ladder: strike offsets `k` are row offsets from the ATM index of the chosen expiry's chain (ADR-006), never a fixed step; a template that would leave the listed strikes, or whose leg has no quote, is refused with the reason. Calendars use the next listed expiry.
  - "Margin est." is the worst defined loss at expiry (null for undefined risk) until the broker margin API exists; the tile says so. ROI-on-margin rows (HC-WS-050, 051, 058) wait for Phase 3.
  - Money formatting is one helper (`lib/money.ts`): USD by default; INR converts at the stored settings rate and formats with en-IN grouping. Strikes and prices per unit stay in USD (HC-WS-031). Position theta is per calendar day and vega per vol point, straight from the engine.
  - Builder table columns are Side · Instrument (strike + C/P or the perpetual symbol, expiry, days, moneyness) · Lots · IV · Price · Δ · Θ/day · delete. The mock's separate Type / Strike / Expiry / Unit columns collapse into Instrument and Lots because the venue has one unit (lots × lot size) and the two-line instrument cell reads faster; lots follow the chain presets on the stepper and accept any integer when typed. New strategies stop at 8 legs, editing an active one allows 10 (HC-TR-017).
  - The workspace split is one number (left-pane share 35–70 %, default 55 %, remembered per browser under `hapiecoin.split`); double-click or Home on the divider resets it. Below 1000 px the panes stack behind a Chain & Builder | Analysis toggle. The right tabs Scenarios, Vol and Structure show what arrives in Phase 5 instead of an empty box.
- Consequences: Phase 3 wires `drafts` and the ticket buttons to the strategy API and trading modes without touching the pane; the payoff canvas renderer (`components/analysis/payoffDraw.ts`) is a pure function of a frame, so the Phase 5 scenario matrix can reuse the scales. The only amber on the pane is the spot hairline and the ladder's spot row (ADR-003). No path here can create an order.

## ADR-024 · Phase 3 trading: strategies move server-side, paper is validated bookkeeping, live goes through the testnet first (2026-09-08)
- Context: Phase 3 turns Builder strategies into tracked positions (spec rows HC-TR-050..089, plus ADR-010 on HC-TR-078 / 089). ADR-023 kept drafts in the browser until the strategy API existed. The spec's "Trading safeguards" list is the acceptance test for anything that places a real order. Plan and design: docs/design/trading.md.
- Decisions:
  - Strategies and their legs live in the API (`strategies`, `strategy_legs`, `strategy_pnl`), owned per user, with status `draft | paper | live | archived`; the browser's local drafts are imported once on the first sign-in after this ships and then dropped from persistence.
  - Paper trading is bookkeeping: entry and exit premiums come from the client at the live mark (or the custom price), the server validates and records them, computes nothing from market data itself, and writes the audit log on every mutation. Unrealised P&L is priced on the client from the same live marks the pane uses; realised P&L is stored when a leg closes. Fees are estimates from the user's broker profile (notional × fee %, options capped at cap % of premium, plus GST).
  - Live trading (item 2) is a separate PR gated on the Delta India **testnet**: venue calls only behind mode + preview + confirm, server-side mark band (±5 %), per-user max notional and max legs, idempotency keys on every order, an account kill switch, and the audit log. Tests use the recording fake client; the live venue is never called by a test.
  - The Phase 4 plan quota (HC-TR-055) reads `subscriptions.featureLimits` and treats a missing limit as unlimited until plans exist.
- Consequences: the Builder's "Save & trade" becomes Paper Trade / Live Trade; "My templates" reads the API; the payoff pane is unchanged. Item 2 needs the user's testnet API key in the API's environment only (never in the repo or chat).

## ADR-025 · Live trading on Delta India: market orders through one executor, testnet URL by env, server-side safeguards (2026-09-08)
- Context: Phase 3 item 2 places real orders for the first time (HC-TR-023, 029, 055, 063, 070, 082..089). Trading-safety rules: only the live executor in `packages/venues` calls order endpoints; tests never touch live endpoints; idempotent, rate-limited, timed-out calls; paper and live visibly different. The user holds a Delta **demo / testnet** key (https://demo.delta.exchange, REST base https://cdn-ind.testnet.deltaex.org); production keys do not work there and vice versa.
- Decisions:
  - `DeltaTradingClient` in `packages/venues/src/delta/trading.ts` is the only code that calls `POST /v2/orders`, `DELETE /v2/orders`, `PUT /v2/orders` (a resting limit re-priced in place, ADR-083), `GET /v2/orders`, `GET /v2/positions/margined` and `GET /v2/wallet/balances`; it signs requests (HMAC-SHA256 over method + timestamp + path + query + body), times out at 5 s, spaces calls with a token bucket, refuses to run under `NODE_ENV=test` without an injected fetch, and resolves `product_id` from `GET /v2/products/{symbol}` (cached). The API's credential verifier reuses its signing.
  - Phase 3 places **market orders only**, sized in contracts: `size = lots × lotSize ÷ contract_value`, refused unless a whole number ≥ 1. Exits are `reduce_only` market orders on the opposite side. Limit orders and order editing are later work.
  - `DELTA_TRADING_REST_URL` (default = `DELTA_REST_URL`) is the base for every private call, including credential verification, so a testnet key connects through Settings → API Settings against the testnet while market data keeps streaming from production. The env `DELTA_API_KEY` / `DELTA_API_SECRET` are not used by the app: orders use the caller's own vault credential.
  - Safeguards enforced on the server before any order: global kill switch `TRADING_DISABLED`, per-user `users.trading_disabled`, `TRADING_MAX_LEGS` (10) and `TRADING_MAX_NOTIONAL_USD` per placement, mark band `TRADING_MARK_BAND_PCT` (5 %) between the previewed price and the venue's current mark, and an available-balance check against the worst-loss estimate when the wallet exposes the settling asset. Every placement carries an `Idempotency-Key`; a repeat with the same key returns the stored result; each order's `client_order_id` is `hc-<legId>-<attempt>` and a retry reuses the attempt id. Every action writes the audit log.
  - Order state lives in `strategy_orders` (client id, venue id, state pending | filled | failed | cancelled | closed, fill price, size, error, attempts). Market fills set the leg's entry premium from `average_fill_price`; partial fills stay `pending` until `sync` reconciles them from `GET /v2/orders/{id}`.
  - Trade All → Live (HC-TR-089, ADR-010) is a batch selector: every ticked paper strategy is previewed and placed one strategy at a time; a failure stops the batch and reports the strategy; a repeat with the same batch key reports the strategies that key already placed (matched on `strategies.order_batch_id`) and never places them again.
- Consequences: paper routes are unchanged; live routes add `/v1/strategies/{id}/live/preview`, `/place`, `/retry`, `/sync`, `/v1/strategies/live/batch` and `/v1/strategies/live/positions`; the web Live tab, Confirm Adjustment Order and the batch selector read them. Tests use the recording `FakeDeltaTradingClient`; the Playwright suite runs against the mock API only.

## ADR-026 · The analysis pane follows what the trader looks at (2026-09-08)
- Status: accepted
- Context: after Phase 3 item 1 the right pane only analysed the Builder's legs, so the Paper and Live tabs showed "No strategy yet" next to a live position. The reference site has the same limitation on its paper tab but analyses ticked net positions on its live tab; the user asked for the pane to always show what is selected (GAPS #39).
- Decision: the UI store carries a `paneSource`: `null` (Builder legs), a paper / live strategy id, or a list of ticked exchange product ids. Clicking a card follows it, the first card of the Paper / Live tab is followed by default, trading from the Builder follows the new position, and opening the Builder or Chain tab returns to the Builder legs. Followed legs are priced at their entry premiums (custom-price semantics) with current marks from the feed. The Builder ticket and the trade flow always read the Builder legs (`useStrategyAnalysis("builder")`) so a followed strategy can never leak into an order. A source bar above the pane names what is followed with a "Back to Builder" action.
- Consequences: `useStrategyAnalysis` takes a scope; `PaperPanel` cards are focusable buttons; the Live tab gains a net-positions table (HC-TR-144) whose ticked rows become legs through `lib/strategy/positions.ts`; the positions payload carries `contractValue` and `mark` from the venue.

## ADR-027 · Strategy templates one click from the Builder legs (2026-09-08)
- Status: accepted; amends ADR-023 (Builder | Templates sub-tabs)
- Context: with templates behind a sub-tab, comparing strategies costs three clicks each and hides the payoff while choosing. The reference site keeps a collapsible template strip under the legs; the user asked for that speed.
- Decision: a collapsible `TemplatesStrip` under the Builder actions with the four outlook tabs, the expiry select and the template cards in a scroll row; one click replaces the Builder legs (a template never merges into an existing position; basket mode is unaffected). The open / collapsed state is persisted per browser (`templatesStrip`). The Templates tab stays for the full gallery and My templates. Loading logic is shared through `useTemplateLoader`.
- Consequences: HC-TR-040 has two entry points; the Builder screenshot changes; the strip is disabled until the chain is loaded so a click can never place a template on an empty ladder (ADR-006).

## ADR-028 · Builder legs editable in place, switchable off, 100 lots by default (2026-09-08)
- Status: accepted; amends ADR-022 / ADR-023 (v2 legs table)
- Context: comparing the Builder against the reference site's current legs table (screenshots from the user, 08 Sep) showed three things ours lacked: a checkbox to leave a leg out of the analysis without deleting it, per-leg expiry / strike / type selects, and a 100-lot default. Our 10-lot default came from the inventory captured on 06 Sep; the reference now defaults to 100.
- Decision: `StrategyLeg.enabled` (undefined = on) is the only new state; the analysis, ticket, templates and trade flows read `activeLegs`, the table shows every leg (switched-off rows dimmed) with a master checkbox. Option legs get a CE / PE toggle, a strike select fed by that expiry's venue ladder (ADR-006 unchanged) and an expiry select fed by the listed expiries; a change rewrites the symbol and takes the ladder's quote when it has one. `DEFAULT_LOTS` is 100 in the Builder and the chain row control; presets unchanged. Payoff gains −2σ..+2σ price labels, break-evens as % from spot and the target calendar date; the header shows the exchange wallet's available balance and a one-click USD / INR toggle (rate still in the Currency dialog).
- Consequences: persisted legs from before keep working (`enabled` absent = on); drafts and trades never carry a switched-off leg; the visual captures of the Builder and payoff change.

## ADR-029 · Phase 3 close-out: polling reconciler, margin from positions, browser P&L writer (2026-09-08)
- Status: accepted
- Context: three follow-ups stayed open after item 2 (GAPS #34, #38, #40): the preview's margin figure, reconciliation of pending orders, and daily P&L points. Delta documents no pre-trade margin estimate endpoint (checked 08 Sep on docs.delta.exchange: orders, positions, add/remove position margin, and private WebSocket channels Margins / Positions / Orders / UserTrades / PortfolioMargins only).
- Decision: (1) the live preview shows the margin the exchange holds right now (sum of position margins from `GET /v2/positions/margined`) next to the available balance, and keeps the worst defined loss as the labelled estimate; no invented pre-trade number. (2) The API runs `startReconciler` every `TRADING_RECONCILE_MS` (default 15 s): one pass over live strategies with pending orders, the same `syncOrders` the Sync button runs, one signed GET per pending order through the token bucket, nothing while nothing is pending, never under NODE_ENV=test; the Live tab polls its list every 10 s while it shows a pending chip. A private WebSocket (Orders / UserTrades) stays a later optimisation. (3) The browser that already prices every strategy at the feed's marks upserts today's P&L point 20 s after the workspace mounts and every 5 minutes when the total moved; a server-side writer that needs no open browser belongs to the Phase 5 snapshotter.
- Consequences: `LivePreview.marginUsed`; `apps/api/src/live-reconcile.ts`; `usePnlWriter` in the Workspace; the harness exposes `deps` so tests drive `reconcilePending` directly.

## ADR-030 · Plans, entitlements and the admin masters (2026-09-08)
- Status: accepted
- Context: Phase 4 item 1 needs a plan catalogue an admin can edit, feature limits the app enforces, and a My Subscription page, before any payment exists. The reference site stores per-interval prices with per-interval limits and uses 0 for unlimited (HC-AD-014); its My Subscription page shows "Not included" for features a plan lacks (HC-AC-006).
- Decision: one `plans` row per plan with a JSON `intervals` column (monthly / quarterly / yearly, each with list price, optional discount price and a `limits` map); a limit key absent = not included, 0 = unlimited, n = n per UTC calendar month. A user's entitlements come from the plan behind their active subscription at its interval, or from the Free plan (the active plan priced ₹0 monthly) when they have none, with `users.limit_overrides` (> 0) replacing the plan number (HC-AD-048). Usage counts strategies started this month per mode and saved drafts. Enforcement happens in one place (`assertEntitled`) at paper start and live placement and answers 403 `UPGRADE_REQUIRED` with the reason the Upgrade Required dialog shows (HC-SH-054); a deactivated account (HC-AD-047) answers 403 `ACCOUNT_DEACTIVATED`. `POST /v1/subscription/activate` accepts only ₹0 totals; paid plans answer 402 until Razorpay checkout (item 2). Admin CRUD for plans, menu items and user subscriptions lives behind `requireAdmin` and writes the audit log; the bulk account toggle never switches off the acting admin. Test users sign up on Elite so trading tests stay about trading; billing tests ask for Free.
- Consequences: migration 0003 (plans, menu_items, subscription plan columns, users.active / limit_overrides / commission_pct); `PlanState` moved into the schema package; the web mock mirrors the routes; the admin rail lists later items with placeholder pages so navigation never dead-ends.

## ADR-031 · Referral commissions: recorded on subscription, settled per referrer (2026-09-08)
- Status: accepted
- Context: Phase 4 item 3 needs the trader's My Referrals page (link, code, earned / paid / pending, the referred users) and the admin's Commissions tab (owed per referrer, Mark Paid / Not Paid, bulk pay). Razorpay checkout (item 2) does not exist yet, so the only subscription writer is the ₹0 activation; the reference site shows every referred sign-up, even those who never paid.
- Decision: one `referral_commissions` row per referred subscription, written by `recordReferralCommission` wherever a subscription is created (activation now, checkout later through the same function); the amount is `commissionFor(paidInr, referrer.commission_pct)` at that moment (2 dp), so a later change of the referrer's percentage never restates old rows. A ₹0 payment writes a `not_paid` row with no amount so the referrer still sees the sign-up ("No Purchase"); a paid one is `pending` until an admin settles it. Re-recording the same subscription refreshes a not-paid or pending row and never reopens a paid one; self-referral and unknown codes write nothing. The referrer's view lists every user who signed up with their code (newest first) with the latest commission row per user; stats sum all rows. Admin `mark` settles every pending row of one referrer together (`paid` sets `paid_at`; `not_paid` needs a reason; note and proof stay when a later mark omits them; nothing pending answers 409) and `bulk-pay` settles all pending rows or those of the given referrers with the note "bulk pay". The month filter buckets by the commission's creation month (UTC), and bulk pay ignores it (the dialog says so). The referral link is `${WEB_URL}/auth?tab=signup&ref=CODE`.
- Consequences: migration 0004 (`referral_commissions`); `GET /v1/referrals`, admin `GET /v1/admin/commissions[?q&month]`, `GET /v1/admin/commissions/{id}`, `POST …/{id}/mark`, `POST …/bulk-pay`; web `/referrals` page, admin Commissions tab, palette "Referrals: copy link"; the web mock mirrors the routes and seeds referred users (`/__test/seed { referrals: n }`), and each mock account now has its own referral code so a referrer resolves to one user. Payout itself stays manual (bank transfer outside the app) until a payouts integration is chosen.

## ADR-032 · Admin User Management: comped plans, invitations by OTP, role rules, no impersonation (2026-09-08)
- Status: accepted
- Context: Phase 4 item 4a needs the admin's user list and a per-user drawer with plan, account, role, limits and lot-size actions (HC-AD-086..109). The reference site's "Set plan" moves users between plans without payment, its "Invite user" and "Impersonate" only toast, and its admin guard lives in the browser.
- Decision: every admin action is a server route behind `requireAdmin` and writes the audit log. "Set plan" (`POST /v1/admin/users/{id}/plan`, `POST /v1/admin/users/bulk-plan`) cancels the active subscription and starts a new one now at the plan's list price with `paid_inr` 0 (a comp, never a charge) and records the referrer's commission row through the same function as checkout. "Invite" (`POST /v1/admin/users/invite`) inserts the user row (role user, generated referral code, unverified email) and emails a sign-in link; the invitee signs in by OTP and no password is ever set by an admin; a known email answers 409. Role rules: an admin cannot change their own role or deactivate their own account, the last active admin cannot be demoted, a deactivated account cannot be promoted (409 with the reason; the drawer hides those buttons for the acting admin). The list (`GET /v1/admin/users`) gains `plan`, `sort` and `dir`; the row gains mobile, referral code, amount paid (sum of every subscription's `paid_inr`) and last login (newest session). The drawer's detail (`GET /v1/admin/users/{id}`) returns the row, every subscription, the plan's default limits, the referral summary and the last 20 audit lines targeting the user. Impersonation is not built: a real one needs a session-swap design with its own audit trail and consent rules (GAPS #47); a toast-only mock would mislead.
- Consequences: `Mailer.sendInvite` and `mailer` on `AppDeps`; `admin-users.ts` builds the row for both billing.ts and the new routes; the web mock mirrors the routes and keeps per-account history and invites; shared admin table tools (sortable headers, Columns menu persisted in the UI store's `adminCols`, Copy CSV, floating bulk bar) start on User Management and reach the other admin tables as they are touched; palette "Admin: …" commands and the settings menu's Admin section appear for admins only.

## ADR-033 · Banners: images in Postgres, frequency rules in the browser, schedule decided by the server (2026-09-08)
- Status: accepted
- Context: Phase 4 item 4b needs admin-scheduled promotional popups (HC-AD-059..070, 121, 122) and the flyer carousel traders see on /analyse (HC-SH-055, 056). The reference site keeps images as data URLs in the browser mock and lets the client decide what is live; HapieCoin has no object store yet.
- Decision: banner images live in Postgres (`banners.image` bytea, PNG / JPEG / WebP / GIF, ≤ 5 MB, checked on the client before upload and again on the server) and are served from `GET /v1/banners/{id}/image?v=<updatedAt>` with a private one-day cache; JSON never carries bytes. Writes take a data URL in JSON under a 7 MB body limit, so no multipart handling is needed. The server decides the schedule (showing / scheduled / ended / hidden) and `GET /v1/banners` returns only banners showing now. The frequency rule (every visit, once per session, once a day) and "Don't show again" are per browser: sessionStorage and localStorage under `hapiecoin.flyers`, because a per-user server record would cost a write on every page view for a cosmetic preference. The palette's "Show announcements" reopens every live banner regardless of rules. hapiecoin.com links navigate in place, others open a new tab. Admin create / update / delete are audited. The reference site's gradient fallback is not built (HC-AD-067 stays confirmed): production banners need a real image.
- Consequences: migration 0005 (`banners`); `hono/body-limit` on the two write routes; the web mock mirrors the routes and seeds banners with `/__test/seed { banners: n }`; the admin table lists every banner without paging (GAPS #48 when the count grows); an object store can replace the bytea column later behind the same image URL.

## ADR-034 · Razorpay checkout, coupons, payments and invoices: the API owns every amount (2026-09-08)
- Status: accepted
- Context: Phase 4 item 2 needs paid plans (HC-AC-016..036, 060..065) and the admin's coupons (HC-AD-029..041, 115, 116). The reference site runs a browser mock of Razorpay and lets the client compute totals; HapieCoin must never let a client set a price, and tests must never touch Razorpay.
- Decision: the API creates every Razorpay order from the catalogue price after the plan discount and the coupon (`POST /v1/checkout`, amount in paise, basic-auth REST over fetch, no SDK) and writes a pending payment row; the browser opens Razorpay's checkout.js with the order id and key id only. The subscription changes in exactly one function (`settlePaid`) after either the checkout handler's payload passes HMAC-SHA256(order_id|payment_id, key secret) or a webhook passes HMAC-SHA256(raw body, webhook secret); settlement is idempotent by Razorpay payment id, a late `payment.failed` never undoes a paid row, and a closed tab is repaired by the webhook. Coupons are validated by one function (`couponReason`) at listing, quote, checkout and activation (unknown, inactive, window, max uses, community assignment, plan, interval, minimum, per-user limit); `usedCount` moves only on a paid payment. A ₹0 total (Free plan or a 100 % coupon) goes through `POST /v1/subscription/activate` and leaves a paid ₹0 payment row so history and invoices are complete. Invoices (INV-YYYY-NNNN, GST split CGST 9 % + SGST 9 %) render from the stored payment row; the seller block comes from `INVOICE_SELLER_*` env. Razorpay keys come from `RAZORPAY_KEY_ID` / `RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET` (`RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET` for the webhook); without keys checkout answers 503 and the live client is refused outright under NODE_ENV=test (the harness uses `FakeRazorpay`). The web mock server answers orders in "mock" mode and the client shows an in-app mock checkout that says no money moves; the real client never does.
- Consequences: migration 0006 (`coupons`, `payments`); `razorpay` on `AppDeps`; the Subscribe dialog gains the coupon column and Payment History with the invoice dialog; admin Coupon Code Master with bulk actions; GAPS #49 (webhook secret and a public URL are needed before relying on webhooks). Refunds and proration are out of scope.

## ADR-035 · Promotional emails: server-rendered placeholders, sequential delivery, one row per recipient (2026-09-08)
- Status: accepted
- Context: Phase 4 item 4c needs admin campaigns to user segments with placeholders, a preview, a test send and a delivery history (HC-AD-071..085, 124..128). The reference site simulates delivery in the browser and never records who received what.
- Decision: `POST /v1/admin/emails/send` takes the recipient ids, subject, message and segment; the API renders `{{name}} {{email}} {{plan}} {{expiry}}` per recipient from the same user rows as User Management, sends one address at a time through the configured mailer (`Mailer.sendPromo`, plain text), and records a `campaigns` row plus a `campaign_recipients` row per address with sent / failed and the transport's error text; the call is audited. Recipient lists (`GET /v1/admin/emails/recipients`) exclude deactivated accounts, carry a status (active / free / expired) and are capped at 200 rows with a "refine the search" note. Segments: paid = active on a paid plan, free = no plan or the Free plan, expired = the newest subscription ran out. `POST /v1/admin/emails/test` sends only to the acting admin with their own values and records nothing. No HTML editor: plain text keeps the mail out of the HTML-injection class and renders everywhere. The admin row now falls back to the newest expired subscription when there is no active one, so "Expired" is a real status in User Management and in the email segments (it was unreachable before).
- Consequences: migration 0007 (`campaigns`, `campaign_recipients`); the capture mailer records promos and can bounce addresses for tests; the web mock mirrors the routes, seeds campaigns and fails every seventh recipient of a send; GAPS #50 notes a queue for large campaigns.
## ADR-036 · Assistant and product tour are client-side, deterministic and platform-only (2026-09-09)
**Context.** Phase 4 item 5a needs the reference site's chat bubble (HC-SH-057..063) and its 15-step guided tour (HC-SH-064..076). The reference answers with canned text matched by keyword and drives the tour from `data-tour` anchors and custom events. HapieCoin's trade flow differs (mode dialog → optional name dialog → preview), and a hosted model would add cost, latency and a new secret before it adds value.
**Decision.** (1) The assistant is a keyword-matched answer bank in `apps/web/src/lib/assistant.ts` plus a strategy explainer built from the same `useStrategyAnalysis` result the payoff pane uses; it answers platform questions only and every explainer ends "not financial advice". No network call, no API route, no secret. Signed-out visitors get the same answers with a sign-in note ("limited visitor mode"). (2) The tour is data (`TOUR_STEPS` in `lib/tour.ts`) rendered by one overlay; steps that ask the trader to act advance on app events (`hc-tour:*`) and each event names how many steps to jump so a skipped dialog (a named strategy has no name step) cannot strand the tour. (3) "Tour done" lives in its own storage key (`hapiecoin.tour`) rather than the UI store, because tests and traders clear the UI store to reset layouts and the tour must not replay on every reset. Auto-start happens once on /analyse after the flyer popup has closed; replay from Settings → Take a tour or the palette.
**Consequences.** Answers are only as good as the bank; questions outside it get the fallback and the support email. A hosted model later means an API route behind the same panel and the same "platform help only" guard (GAPS #52). The tour's anchors are a contract: renaming a `data-tour` value needs `tour.test.ts` and the e2e tour updated. The Playwright fixture marks the tour done for every test except those opting in with `test.use({ tour: true })`.

## ADR-037 · The public payoff preview runs the workspace engine on fixed presets, with no market data (2026-09-09)
**Context.** The reference site's /payoff-preview shows a styled payoff chart with four presets, legend/layer toggles, zoom, SD bands, target sliders and a summary, driven by its mock pricing. HapieCoin's landing visitors have no session and the gateway offers the public pages only tickers, not a chain.
**Decision.** `/payoff-preview` is a public route that reuses `PayoffChart` and calls `analyze` from `@hapiecoin/pricing` synchronously in the browser on four fixed presets (`lib/payoff-preview.ts`): one contract = 1 BTC, expiry 30 days from page load, spot fixed per preset. Open interest bars are a deterministic bell around spot (a shape, labelled "faint bars"), never live OI. SD bands use the legs' average IV over the slider horizon. Nothing is persisted and no API is called.
**Consequences.** The page is a faithful demo of the chart, not of the market: the copy says "sample legs at a fixed spot" and points to sign-in for live strikes. A future version could subscribe to the gateway's chain for real OI once the public gateway exposes instrument lists (GAPS #53).

## ADR-038 · Market Analytics data comes from free public feeds through one ingest service; paid on-chain sources are deferred (2026-09-09)
**Context.** Phase 5's Market Analytics block (294 rows, `docs/research/market-analytics-data-sources.md`) needs derivatives, spot, index and on-chain data that Delta alone cannot supply. The user chose the hybrid direction with a zero budget: free feeds first, "coming soon" for screens that need paid providers, buy only when users ask.
**Decision.** (1) A new `apps/ingest` service owns every upstream call: one adapter per source (Binance USDⓈ-M, Bybit v5, OKX v5 public endpoints for funding / open interest / long-short / taker volume / liquidations; CoinGecko Demo for spot markets; alternative.me for Fear & Greed), a scheduler that never overlaps a job with itself, and a snapshot store in Redis (`hapiecoin:an:{dataset}:{symbol|-}`, TTL = 20 × refresh interval). Browsers never call a provider. (2) Every snapshot is the `AnalyticsSnapshot` envelope from `packages/schema/src/analytics.ts` with `source`, `asOf`, `ttlMs` and `stale`; a failed refresh re-writes the previous snapshot with `stale: true` instead of blanking it. A venue that fails is left out of that refresh and out of `source` ("Binance · OKX"), so an outage narrows a chart's provenance rather than hiding it. (3) The API serves them read-only and publicly at `GET /v1/analytics/{dataset}` with `Cache-Control` and `X-As-Of`; it re-validates the JSON and answers 503 UNAVAILABLE until the ingest has written a key. (4) Long/short and taker volume come from Binance only (the one venue publishing all three long/short series); liquidations combine Binance's `!forceOrder@arr` stream with OKX's polled fills, deduplicated by (venue, time, symbol, price, size). (5) The CoinGecko key is optional: without it the markets dataset is skipped and logged, nothing else changes. (6) ETF flows, exchange reserves, token unlocks, Puell and gold / DXY ship as "coming soon" panels (GAPS #55).
**Consequences.** Binance's statistics endpoints keep 30 days, so 1Y timeframe chips need our own history table later (GAPS #56). The CoinGecko Demo cap (10k calls/month) sets the spot refresh at 10 minutes; a paid plan lifts it without code changes. Aggregation is always stated as the venues present, never "all exchanges". Provider shapes are validated with Zod in each adapter, so a silent upstream change fails one job loudly instead of drawing wrong numbers.

## ADR-039 · Analytics pages: own route names, one inline-SVG chart, "coming soon" panels for deferred datasets (2026-09-09)
**Context.** PR 5.2 builds the analytics shell, Markets Hub and Futures overview on the ingest snapshots (ADR-038). The reference site's hub path carries a third-party data brand and its charts are hand-built SVG; several hub tiles need data we deliberately deferred (GAPS #55).
**Decision.** (1) Routes are ours: `/analytics` redirects to `/analytics/hub` (the spec's hub row is satisfied by the hub itself; no provider name in a URL). (2) One `Chart` component (`lib/analytics/chart.ts` for the pure layout, `components/analytics/Chart.tsx` for rendering) draws every analytics chart as inline SVG with a toggleable legend, timeframe chips, crosshair tooltip, last-value tags and a skeleton; the workspace's canvas payoff chart stays separate because its interaction model differs. (3) Every chart and table prints a `SourceLine` (venues · as of · stale badge). (4) Tiles and panels whose data is deferred render a `ComingSoon` block that names the gap, never a blank or a fake number; hub tabs Stock and Token Unlock do the same as in the reference. (5) Sections that arrive in later PRs (`/analytics/markets`, `/derivatives`, `/options`, `/etf`, `/liquidations`, `/whales`, `/sentiment`, `/terminal/*`) render a "arrives in PR 5.x" page inside the shell so navigation never dead-ends (HC-SH-016). (6) An `overview` dataset folded by the ingest service feeds both pages in one read; the hub's Derivatives table merges it with the CoinGecko markets rows; without a CoinGecko key the table shows why it is empty.
**Consequences.** Coin pages in this PR show tiles only; charts follow in PR 5.3. Watchlist and column choices live in the UI store. The palette gains "Analytics: Markets Hub" and "Analytics: Futures overview".

## ADR-040 · Derivatives, liquidations, screener and coin pages derive from the eight datasets; no new feeds until the venue block is understood (2026-09-09)
**Context.** PR 5.3 builds `/analytics/markets`, `/analytics/derivatives`, `/analytics/liquidations` and the coin-page charts on the datasets the ingest already writes (ADR-038: funding, open-interest, long-short, taker-volume per symbol; liquidations, markets, fear-greed, overview). The reference site's versions of these screens also draw a close-price chart, a perp/spot basis chart, a per-coin liquidation history and a liquidation "heatmap" from data we do not ingest. While testing on the user's machine, `fapi.binance.com` and `www.okx.com` reset every TLS handshake (curl and Node alike, inside and outside the tool sandbox); only Bybit answers, so long-short, taker-volume and the liquidation stream have no data there (GAPS #57).
**Decision.** (1) No new ingest jobs in this PR: every chart and table is a pure derivation of existing snapshots (`lib/analytics/derive.ts`), so the pages work wherever the ingest works and the feed gap is fixed once, in the ingest. (2) Price history comes from CoinGecko's 7-day hourly sparkline already in the markets rows (`sparklineSeries`), aligned to the OI grid by nearest timestamp (`alignTo`); charts say "spot · CoinGecko sparkline" and offer 1D/7D only. (3) Liquidation bars for 12 h/24 h use the ingest's hourly buckets; 1 h/4 h re-bin the newest events at 5/15 minutes and the page says when the 100-event ring buffer does not reach the window start. The coin page's liquidation chart and the heatmap are built from the same captured events (price band ±5 % × hour), labelled "from the captured events", never from an estimated-leverage model. (4) Basis stays a "coming soon" panel (GAPS #57) because it needs mark and index prices per venue; per-venue 24 h volume is replaced by OI 24 h % in the coin-page markets table for the same reason. (5) Funding-rate arbitrage rows are computed client-side from the per-symbol funding snapshots of every tracked coin (long the lowest-paying venue, short the highest; APR = spread × 3 × 365) with a `useQueries` fan-out; only coins with two or more venues qualify. (6) Compare mode is a checkbox column in `DataTable` (max 3) plus `?compare=BTC,ETH`; category chips drive `?category=`; the watchlist chip filters on the persisted watchlist. (7) The remaining "coming soon" sections are options, ETF, whales and sentiment (PR 5.4) and the terminal (PR 5.5).
**Consequences.** Long/short, taker volume and the live liquidation feed are empty on networks that block Binance and OKX until Bybit fallbacks land in the ingest (GAPS #57); the pages state which venue they are waiting for instead of showing zeros. The palette gains "Analytics: Markets screener", "Analytics: Derivatives" and "Analytics: Liquidations".

## ADR-041 · Venue fallbacks in the ingest: Bybit for long/short and liquidations, Binance stays primary (2026-09-09)
**Context.** On the user's network Binance and OKX reset every TLS handshake (GAPS #57), so the Binance-only datasets (long-short, taker-volume) fail and the liquidation stream never connects. Bybit answers. Bybit publishes a global account ratio (`/v5/market/account-ratio`) and an `allLiquidation.{symbol}` public stream (verified in the docs on 09 Sep 2026: `S` is the liquidated position side, "Buy" = a long was liquidated; `p` is the bankruptcy price; push every 500 ms; a ping every 20 s keeps the socket). Bybit has no top-trader ratios and no taker buy/sell endpoint; OKX has a taker-volume endpoint but its docs were unreachable today, so its units are unverified.
**Decision.** (1) Fallback order per dataset, not per venue: `long-short` tries Binance (three series) and falls back to Bybit's global ratio with empty top-trader series and `source: "Bybit"`; the job fails only when both fail. (2) A second liquidation stream: one generic `LiquidationStream` with per-venue specs (path, subscribe frames, parser, heartbeat); Binance keeps `!forceOrder@arr`, Bybit subscribes to `allLiquidation.{SYMBOL}USDT` with one frame per tracked symbol (Bybit rejects a whole request when any topic is unknown; PEPE trades there as 1000PEPEUSDT) and pings every 20 s. Both feed the same buffer; `source` lists every connected venue and `/healthz` reports `stream: { binance, bybit }`. (3) Bybit's `usd` uses the bankruptcy price, an approximation the source line makes visible. (4) Taker volume stays Binance-only and basis stays deferred until the OKX taker units and a mark/index tickers job are verified (GAPS #57 remains open for those two).
**Consequences.** On Bybit-only networks the long/short charts and the liquidation pages fill; the top-trader series and the taker chart stay empty there and say so. No schema, API or web change beyond two empty-state labels.

## ADR-042 · Options, sentiment and cycle datasets: Deribit + Delta options, our own maths on Bybit closes, premium via CoinGecko (2026-09-09)
**Context.** PR 5.4a builds `/analytics/options`, `/analytics/sentiment` and the `/analytics/etf` placeholder. Verified today: Deribit's `get_book_summary_by_currency` lists every option with open interest in underlying units and the underlying price (no documented public rate limit); Delta India's `/v2/tickers` lists BTC/ETH options with `oi_value` in underlying units; Bybit spot klines return 1000 daily candles; Hyperliquid has no documented leaderboard; Coinbase and Binance are unreachable from the user's network, while CoinGecko's exchange-ticker endpoint (Demo plan, 2-minute cache) carries both. AHR999's published constants and Puell's miner-revenue source could not be verified, and the OKX docs were unreachable.
**Decision.** (1) New datasets, generic through the API: `options` per underlying with per-venue expiries (call/put OI in underlying units, max pain by the Deribit Insights method, put/call, 24 h volume), `cycle` (1000 daily closes from Bybit spot with 111DMA, 2×350DMA, 2-year MA and ×5, and a log-linear regression fit over that window for the rainbow bands), `rsi` (Wilder RSI(14) per timeframe from Bybit perpetual candles for every tracked symbol) and `premium` (Coinbase BTC-USD minus Binance BTC-USDT through CoinGecko, hourly, with the history kept by the ingest itself: the store gains `appendSeries`, a capped JSON array, its first self-kept history). (2) The rainbow is labelled as a fit over the shown window, not the genesis-anchored curve; the 2-year averages appear only once 730 closes exist. (3) The cycle-top checklist computes six of eight signals and shows AHR999 and Puell as "n/a" (GAPS #59) instead of a formula we could not verify. (4) The ETF page keeps the reference layout with coming-soon panels (GAPS #55). (5) Other option venues (OKX, Bybit, Binance) wait for a verified tickers check (GAPS #59). (6) The Delta adapter is a thin JSON client like the others; `packages/venues` is not pulled into the ingest.
**Consequences.** CoinGecko usage rises by about 1,500 calls a month (well under the 10k Demo cap). The premium history starts empty and fills hourly. The chart component gains a log axis, horizontal bands, filled regions and free labels. Whales (PR 5.4b) remain the only "coming soon" section.

## ADR-043 · Whales from a leaderboard scan of Hyperliquid wallets, alerts from poll diffs, walls from Bybit books (2026-09-09)
**Context.** PR 5.4b builds `/analytics/whales`. Hyperliquid's public info API has no "largest positions" or leaderboard endpoint; the leaderboard its UI reads (`stats-data.hyperliquid.xyz/Mainnet/leaderboard`, ~37 MB JSON, 45k rows) is undocumented, and its `accountValue` is not the perpetual balance (the top rows by that value held no positions when probed). `clearinghouseState` costs weight 2 of the 1200-per-minute IP budget; a scan of the top 80 wallets found 12 with positions, the largest at $607M notional. Binance is unreachable from the user's network, so resting walls come from Bybit's 500-level books. Exchange reserves need paid on-chain providers (GAPS #55).
**Decision.** (1) Wallet discovery: every hour the ingest reads the leaderboard, takes the top `WHALE_CANDIDATES` (200) by account value and merges `WHALE_WALLETS` from the environment; when the leaderboard fails the previous list or the env list is used and `source` says "watched wallets". (2) Polling: every fifth run polls every candidate, the other runs only the wallets holding positions plus the env wallets, eight requests at a time. (3) Alerts are the diff between two polls for polled wallets only: opened, closed, increased, reduced or flipped, above `WHALE_ALERT_USD` ($1M); the newest 100 are kept in memory and the hourly alert notional goes through `store.appendSeries` (7 days). (4) The Whale Index is our own composite: the current hour's alert notional against the busiest kept hour (floor $10M), 0–100, labelled Quiet / Active / Frenzy; the page says so. (5) Large orders are Bybit book levels worth at least `WALL_USD` ($1M) with first-seen tracking; a level that leaves the book is shown as "gone" for 30 minutes. (6) Exchange reserves stay a coming-soon panel. (7) The leaderboard download gets its own HTTP client with a 90-second timeout and no retry.
**Consequences.** Alerts see changes between one-minute polls, not individual fills, and only for scanned wallets; a whale outside the top 200 leaderboard rows is invisible unless listed in `WHALE_WALLETS`. Memory holds the tracker state, so alerts and the current hour restart empty after a redeploy while the hourly series survives in Redis. The Whales section was the last "coming soon" analytics page; the section map is now empty.

## ADR-044 · Adjustment workbench: one atomic adjust batch, a separate draft slice, before/after in the analysis pane (2026-09-09)
**Context.** Adjusting a running strategy went through the modal chain picker with no combined preview; paper legs were added blind and live legs had no margin preview (`preview={null}`), no mark band and no idempotency. The user asked for a workbench where the trader sees the combined position before confirming, can trim, close or add to existing legs, pick other expiries, and sees the updated max loss / profit afterwards. Two clickable mockups were built and reviewed with the front-end design skill, an architecture agent and an options-terminal UX critic; the user approved layout H2 (position + ticket | chain | preview) with six extras. Design: `docs/design/adjustment-workbench.md`; mockup: `docs/design/mockups/adjustment-workbench-v2.html`.
**Decision.** (1) One atomic API batch `POST /v1/strategies/{id}/adjust` carries `adds` (new legs or add-to-existing), `changes` (`{ legId, lotsAfter }` → trim / close), `expected` marks per symbol, an `idempotencyKey` and a `reason`; paper applies it in one transaction at the client marks (adds average into the existing leg's entry), live previews then places entries and exits through the executor with the mark band, rolling the batch back on a refused check; the existing `/legs` and `/legs/:id/close` routes stay for the Details buttons. (2) The live preview accepts the proposed batch (`legs` + `exits`) so it prices what will be sent, not the open legs. (3) On the web the draft lives in its own store slice (`adjust`), never in Builder legs (ADR-026 holds); the analysis hook computes before (open legs at entry) and after (open + draft, proposed at live mark) and the pane renders after with the before curve dashed. (4) Netting is client-side and explicit: opposite side on a held contract trims or closes, same side adds; the 10-leg cap counts after netting. (5) Bounds are analytical (net call / put exposure) and valuation supports several expiries at one date. (6) The live confirm is different by design: red frame, order type, per-leg band in ticks, per-leg fill states, hold-to-place. (7) The reason is stored with the adjustment and surfaces in Details as history (the journal hook).
**Consequences.** Three PRs (A1 API, A2 workbench + paper, A3 live + plans / quick fixes / scenario date / alert stub). New traceability rows HC-TR-148..154. The Builder keeps its own chain picker; the workbench reuses the picker body inline.
**As built in A1 (2026-09-09).** `AdjustBody { adds, changes: [{ legId, lotsAfter, price }], expected, idempotencyKey?, reason? }` needs at least one add or change; `lotsAfter` must be at most the open lots (more lots go through `adds`, which keeps a separate leg row with its own entry price rather than averaging into the existing leg, so realised P&L per fill stays exact and the data model is unchanged); `lotsAfter` equal to the open lots is a no-op; `expected` marks must be positive. The cap of 10 open legs counts after the closes in the same batch. Live: every check runs before the first order (`preview()` takes the adds as entries and the trims / closes as reduce-only `exits`: product state and sizing for both, the per-placement leg and notional caps and the wallet read for the entries only), then exits go to the venue one by one with realised P&L booked per fill, then the adds are inserted and placed with the mark band (`expected` keyed by symbol, mapped to the inserted leg ids). A failed exit stops the batch with 502: what filled stays booked, the adds are not inserted, and the history row records `Stopped: <reason>`; the client rebuilds the rest from the strategy as it is under a new key. Paper exits at the client `price`, sequentially (no transaction: `apps/api` has no `db.transaction` precedent; the history row written first and the per-fill booking make a partial paper batch visible rather than silent). Each batch writes a `strategy_adjustments` row first (migration 0008: reason, added / trimmed / closed counts, realised P&L, batch id; `(strategy_id, batch_id)` unique) and fills in the counts at the end, so a concurrent repeat of the same key returns the strategy unchanged instead of placing twice; the key wins over the body, which is why the workbench sends a fresh key per Review. `Strategy.adjustments` returns the rows oldest first; `StrategyOrder.batchId` links each order to its batch. The live preview takes the same `adds` / `changes`; its `legs` list the exits first (`legId` of the open leg, opposite side) then the adds as `new-n`. Traceability: the seven new rows are HC-TR-148..154 (HC-TR-146/147 were already Builder rows), added through `mockup-v2/features` when A2 lands.
**As built in A2 (2026-09-09).** The engine's `analyze()` takes an optional `valuationMs`: legs settled by then are intrinsic, later legs keep their Black-76 time value; extremes and break-evens of that curve are searched on the price axis (grid plus every strike, a near-zero and a far price) while the unbounded sides stay analytical (the ray above the top strike), and POP uses the time to that instant. The workbench is a mode of `/analyse` held in a non-persisted `adjust` store slice (draft = lots after per open leg, picks, value-at expiry, started-at): the left pane swaps to position + ticket | chain, the pane source follows the strategy and `useStrategyAnalysis` prices the position *after* the change on every tab (kept lots at entry, adds at the live mark) with the *before* result for the ghost curve and the before → after strip. Netting is client-side in `lib/adjust/model.ts` (same side adds, opposite side trims then closes then flips; extra lots on a held leg become a separate add row, as the API stores them). The confirm dialog builds one `AdjustBody` per Review with a fresh idempotency key and the marks shown as `expected`; paper applies at those marks, live first runs the venue preview of the batch and A3 adds the order type, hold-to-place and per-leg fill states. Rows HC-TR-148..152 carry build evidence; 153 (plans compare) and 154 (quick fixes) stay `static` until A3. Deferred to A3 with the venue figures: the "margin after exceeds available" guard rail (the live confirm shows available and margin held but does not yet warn), hold-to-place, order type and per-leg fill states. Leaving the followed strategy (another card, ticked positions, the Builder or Chain tab) discards the draft, so the ticket and the request are always built from the same open legs.
**As built in A3 (2026-09-09).** Limit orders enter the executor for adjustment entries only: `AdjustBody.orderType` ("market" default, "limit"), `placeEntries` sends a leg as a limit at its `expected` mark (a leg without one goes at market), the venue clients take `orderType` / `limitPrice` (`limit_order` + `limit_price` on Delta; the fake rests a limit that does not cross its fill price), `strategy_orders.order_type` (migration 0009) and `StrategyOrder.orderType` record it. Exits stay market reduce-only, so a batch never stalls on an unfilled exit; a resting limit entry is a pending order the existing Sync reconciles. The live confirm shows per-leg band movement against the venue's band, the order type, a 1.2 s hold-to-place (pointer or Space / Enter held; release cancels), and after placing keeps the batch's fill states (filled @ price / resting / failed) on screen until Done → Details; a notional above the available balance is a guard rail. Plans (up to three, A / B / C) live inside the draft and are priced with the same marks and valuation rule as the working change. Quick fixes are pure generators over the open legs and the listed strikes (roll strikes up, roll out to the nearest strike of the next expiry with the higher strike on a tie, hedge with a call one strike above the highest short call or above spot), each ranked by the engine; an unbuildable fix says why. The scenario slider stores any ISO date as the valuation ("today" and the expiries stay as chips). Risk alerts are a persisted stub (`riskAlerts`, one per strategy, kept in USD) that the Alerts item arms. Rows HC-TR-153/154 are now `working`. After review: a limit price is snapped onto the product's `tick_size` toward the passive side (`roundToTick`; a buy rounds down, a sell up) and stored on the order (`limit_price`); a resting limit the venue cancels is retried by Retry as the same limit at that price (cancelled entry orders are retryable, GAPS #61 covers cancel / replace from the app); the live hold is disabled while any add's mark sits outside the band (the venue would refuse it) and the rolls place their replacements as plain picks so a spread on adjacent strikes rolls whole instead of netting into a naked short. Cost note: an open workbench prices up to three quick fixes and one row per plan next to the before / after pair, all RAF-batched in the worker.

## ADR-045 · Analyse tabs read the live gateway chain; IV history stays a gap (2026-09-09)
**Context.** Phase 5 item 1 fills the three placeholder tabs of the analysis pane (Scenarios, Vol, Structure; HC-WS-088..100). The mock generates an IV history and a realised-vol series; the product has neither (GAPS #32), and the analytics Options page already computes max pain from the ingest's venue snapshots (ADR-042).
**Decision.** (1) Scenarios prices the followed position (ADR-026) through the pricing worker's `scenario` op: eleven price rows at ±10 % / ±20 %, six date columns from today to the nearest expiry's settlement instant, P&L / delta / theta modes, and an IV slider applied as an additive shift of `ATM IV × pct` (the engine's `ivShift` is in vol points); the smooth field is a pure canvas renderer (`heatDraw.ts`) like the payoff chart.
