(2) Vol and Structure read the **live gateway chain** of one listed expiry (the workspace expiry when the trader chose one on the chain strip or the term-structure chips, else the followed strategy's nearest listed expiry, else the nearest listed; a hint offers the strategy's expiry back) through a small adapter (`lib/chain/structure.ts`) into the pricing package's `smile`, `maxPain`, `putCallRatio` and `gammaExposure`; the term structure subscribes to every listed expiry (`useChains`), and a chip switches the workspace expiry. (3) IV rank and realised-vs-implied ship as `ComingSoon` panels naming GAPS #62 rather than generated numbers. (4) The Structure tab carries a basis line because its max pain (one expiry, live chain) legitimately differs from the Options page (all expiries, venue snapshots).
**Consequences.** HC-WS-088..094 and 098..100 carry build evidence; HC-WS-096/097 are `static` placeholders until the snapshotter (GAPS #62). The scenario matrix keys its rows and cells by position so a spot tick updates them in place (a click must never land on a node React replaced), labels every column from the instant it is valued at, and the day count follows the settlement instant like the payoff slider. Only the pane's spot / ATM amber is used for the max-pain and strategy-strike markers (ADR-003); OI and GEX bars stay green / red. A pane switch mounts one tab at a time, so each tab subscribes on mount and the gateway answers with a snapshot.

## ADR-046 · The terminal is a sidebar over the analytics datasets; venue pages sum the per-symbol snapshots (2026-09-10)
**Context.** PR 5.5 builds the `/terminal/*` family (174 rows, HC-MT-001..174): a second, sidebar-driven view of the market data the reference site mounted from its analytics subdomain. Every number it shows already exists in the ingest's snapshots (ADR-038..043); what it lacks are venue-level screens, sector screens and a few datasets we deliberately deferred (ETF flows, exchange reserves, unlocks, AHR999, Puell: GAPS #55, #59).
**Decision.** (1) The terminal reuses the analytics shell (header, section switcher with Terminal ▾, footer) and adds a 232 px sticky sidebar (`components/terminal/TerminalShell.tsx`) whose groups, active rule and section name live in `lib/terminal/nav.ts`; under the `lg` breakpoint the sidebar is a "Navigation" drawer that names the current section and closes on route change. (2) No new ingest job: the exchange overview and the Open Interest table sum the per-symbol `open-interest` and `funding` snapshots per venue (`lib/terminal/derive.ts` `venueTotals` / `venueCoins`) through a `useAnalyticsMany` fan-out, so a venue page is a pure derivation of what the ingest already writes; venues are the three perpetual venues the ingest reports (Delta only carries options), and an unknown venue or sector says so instead of showing zeros. (3) Sectors use the same `sectorOf` map as the hub and screener, so a coin never sits in two sectors across the app. (4) The coin page is the analytics `CoinPage` with a `base` prop that rewrites its links (back, compare, sector chip, venue rows → `/terminal/exchanges/{venue}`); the header search opens `/terminal/coin/{sym}` inside the terminal and toasts when nothing matches. (5) Per-venue 24 h volume needs venue tickers we do not ingest (GAPS #63); BTC balance, ETF flows and unlocks stay "coming soon" (GAPS #55); AHR999 and Puell stay `n/a` (GAPS #59). (6) Long and short liquidations keep the app-wide colours (long = green, short = red) rather than the reference terminal's inverted pair, so one colour means one thing everywhere in HapieCoin. (7) The terminal requires a session (HC-MT-038): its layout redirects to `/auth?next=%2Fterminal`, unlike `/analytics`, which stays public.
**Consequences.** The placeholder catch-all route is gone; every `/terminal/*` path renders a real screen. The palette gains nine `nav:terminal-*` entries. Coin prices on the terminal refresh on the markets snapshot TTL, not on the 2.5 s workspace tick (HC-MT-081 is `static`). Phase 5 item 5 (Market Analytics) is complete once this PR merges; items 2–4 (alerts, journal, share pages) follow.

## ADR-047 · The Journal is a view over archived strategies; tags and notes live on the strategy (2026-09-10)
**Context.** Phase 5 item 1 closes with the Journal tab (HC-TR-121, 128..137): closed trades with their realised P&L, stats, an equity curve, tags, notes and a CSV export. The server already keeps everything a trade needs (`status = archived`, `startedAt`, `closedAt`, `realizedPnl`, `tags`, `notes`, the squared-off legs with entry and exit); the question was whether to add a journal table or derive the view.
**Decision.** (1) No new table or endpoint: a closed trade is an archived strategy that was started and closed (`lib/strategy/journal.ts` `closedTrades`); an archived draft never traded and is not a trade. The squared-off legs of strategies that are still active appear in their own list with the leg's realised P&L. (2) A win is realised P&L > 0, a loss < 0; break-even trades count in neither; the profit factor is gross profit ÷ gross loss, ∞ when nothing was lost and 0 when nothing was won. (3) The equity curve is cumulative realised P&L over the close dates, starting at zero when the first shown trade opened, and follows the active filter, as do the stats. (4) Tags and notes are saved on the strategy through the existing `PATCH /v1/strategies/{id}` (allowed on any status); the three preset chips (earnings, range, hedge) toggle, custom tags are lower-cased and hyphenated, notes save 800 ms after the last keystroke or on blur, and every save toasts. (5) Export CSV copies the filtered rows to the clipboard (the browser sandbox has no reliable download from a workspace tab) with the mock's columns. (6) Strategy Details gains a Journal block for every traded archived strategy with an "Open in Journal" button.
**Consequences.** Stop (archive) and Square off all already set `closedAt`, so a trade appears in the Journal the moment it closes. Realised P&L excludes fees (they were never stored per strategy); the stats say "realised". The Workspace's last Phase placeholder is gone: every left tab is live.

## ADR-048 · Chain and payoff extras derive from what the chain and the worker already know (2026-09-10)
**Context.** Phase 5 item 1's remaining chain and payoff rows (HC-WS-029, 066, 074..076, 080, 081, 083..087) ask for footer statistics, a find-by-Δ control, a spot hairline, density-aware rows, IV ±5 % layers, a click-to-target chart, richer strips and slider ticks. An audit of the real build (10 Sep) found the data for all of them already on the client: the whole chain snapshot, the pricing package's max-pain and skew helpers, and the worker's scenario op with an additive IV shift.
**Decision.** (1) The chain footer reads the **whole** chain, not the ±range slice: max pain (`maxPain` over the listed strikes), the 25Δ skew (`skew25`) and the forward from ATM put-call parity (K + C − P at the strike nearest spot with both marks) in `lib/chain/structure.ts` `chainStats`; OI cells and footer sums show full integers with thousands separators. (2) Find-by-Δ (`nearestDelta`) picks the call nearest +Δ and the put nearest −Δ over the whole chain; when a hit sits outside the current range the table widens to every strike first, then scrolls between the two rows, focuses the call and pulses both for 1.6 s, with a toast naming both strikes and their deltas. (3) The spot hairline is a zero-height overlay between the two strikes bracketing spot, skipped when spot equals a listed strike (the ATM band already marks it). (4) Density drives the chain's row height (36 / 28 px through `useDensity`) and the ladder's cell padding; column widths are unchanged. (5) The IV ±5 % layers are two extra worker scenario calls on the target date with `ivShift ±0.05` over the same price axis, drawn dashed in the target colour; they run only while their layer is on. Chart layers persist in the UI store (`chartLayers`), so the trader's choice survives a reload. (6) A click on the chart sets the target price (the canvas reports the price under the pointer); the sliders gain five price ticks (−20 % … +20 %, SPOT in amber) and Today / mid / Expiry date ticks under native range inputs rather than a custom track. (7) The analytics strip adds Max ROI (max profit ÷ margin estimate) and the margin with its share of the strategy notional (spot × lots × lot size); the greeks strip adds the margin estimate and a "USD per strategy · N lots" basis; the hover readout reads `date @ price ±x% · target P&L · exp P&L`.
**Consequences.** No new API or worker op. The footer's max pain and skew are as good as the venue's greeks (null → "—"). Sliders keep the native control (accessibility, keyboard) instead of the mock's custom track (HC-WS-085 is `working` with that caveat). Remaining analyse rows (Greeks across price, share links, deep links, pane collapse, palette per expiry / template) follow in the next PR; header stats and the shortcuts help stay with item 3.

## ADR-049 · Share links carry the strategy; workspace state extras live in the UI store (2026-09-10)
**Context.** The second group of Phase 5 item 1 rows (HC-WS-006, 065, 067, 069, 101..103, 105, 106) asks for share links with a public landing route, Δ / Θ across price on the Greeks tab, vega in currency, a persisted ladder step, deep links into the tabs, pane collapse and per-expiry / per-template palette commands. None of them needs the API.
**Decision.** (1) A share link is `/s/<code>` where the code is base64url JSON `{v, a, n?, l: [[kind, side, strike, expiry, lots, price, iv?]]}` (`lib/strategy/share.ts`): nothing is uploaded, no table, no account to decode. `decodeShare` validates every field (asset, kind, side, integer lots ≤ 1000, decimal price and strike, ISO expiry or PERP for futures, ≤ 10 legs) and returns null otherwise; the landing page loads the legs into the persisted UI store through `addLeg`, names the strategy, sets the price mode to custom so the shared entry prices stick, toasts "Strategy loaded from link" and replaces the route with `/analyse` (which asks for a sign-in if needed; the legs wait in the store). (2) The Greeks tab prices the position at 49 prices ±20 % around spot on the target date through the worker's scenario op in `delta` and `theta` mode; vega everywhere is shown in the display currency per vol point. (3) The ladder step, the collapsed pane and the chart layers are UI-store fields persisted with the workspace; a template asked for from the palette is a transient store request the Templates panel honours once the chain rows exist. (4) Deep links `?tab=` / `?panel=` are read once on arrival and ignored when unknown; they never overwrite the persisted tabs otherwise. (5) Collapsing keeps the other pane mounted at full width behind a 16 px ⋮ handle that restores both; charts re-measure through their ResizeObservers. (6) The palette lists one "Switch expiry → date" per listed expiry of the workspace asset and one "Load template → name" per template, only when signed in.
**Consequences.** Share links are as long as the legs (about 40 characters per leg); a link made on a different lot size still loads the lots it carries. The Greeks tab makes two more worker calls per position change. Remaining analyse rows (HC-WS-004, 068, 070) belong to item 3 (portfolio bar, header stats, shortcuts registry).

## ADR-050 · Builder ticket, template pricing and picker parity from the data already on the client (2026-09-10)
**Context.** The trading rows left in Phase 5 item 1 for the Builder, Templates, chain picker, trade dialogs and palette (HC-TR-094, 096, 097, 100..108, 122, 126, 127, 140, 142) all ask for figures the client can compute: exchange fees per leg, totals, the strike width, template POP / R:R / outlook, the picker's Δ and OI, and commands the store can serve.
**Decision.** (1) The Builder ticket grows to six tiles: Premium at mark, Fees · est. (the same `feeFor` model as the trade dialogs: notional × fee %, capped at a share of the premium for options, plus GST), Total debit / credit (premium + fees for a debit, premium − fees for a credit, flagged "= max loss" when the debit equals the worst expiry loss), P&L at target, Margin est. with POP · R:R as its sub-line, and Width (max − min strike, strike count, % of spot). A net line under the legs repeats lot size, net premium per unit and in currency, and the net Δ / Θ per day / ν per vol point. (2) The exchange chosen in the trade dialogs is stored (`store.brokerId`, persisted) and the ticket reads it, so one fee model serves the ticket, the mode dialog and the preview. (3) Template cards are priced at the live chain: every template is materialised on the venue ladder and analysed in the worker once per chain snapshot (`useTemplateStats`), giving POP, R:R and an outlook classified from the expiry payoff at ±6 % (Bullish / Bearish / Volatile / Neutral); the "Recommended for outlook" chips filter on that classification, not on the template's label. Sketches fill profit green and loss red under the curve. (4) The chain picker adds a Δ column outboard, OI bars growing toward the strike and the ITM tint, from the rows it already subscribes to. (5) P starts a paper trade while the Builder is visible with legs and no dialog is open; the header shows the detected structure and "asset · expiry · DTE"; the Builder sub-tab line adds DTE and draft / unsaved. (6) The palette gains Builder: save draft (a transient store request the Builder honours), Paper trade the current strategy, Open Paper / Live trades and Open Journal. (7) Builder rows follow density (28 px compact with the instrument sub-line hidden).
**Consequences.** Template pricing costs one worker call per template per chain snapshot (28 calls, batched sequentially, only while the Templates tab is open). The remaining trading rows (paper / live summary strips, batch-id tag, details tiles and mini payoff, portfolio aggregation) follow in the next PR; Set alert rows wait for the alerts engine (item 2).

## ADR-051 · Portfolio figures come from the pricing worker per strategy, debounced on the quote stream (2026-09-10)
**Context.** The paper and live tabs (HC-TR-113, 116), the live card (HC-TR-115) and Strategy Details (HC-TR-118, 119) need figures no single strategy carries: net delta / theta / vega across the open strategies, the margin used, and per strategy the margin estimate with POP and the expiry payoff at the entry premiums. The mock computed these synchronously on every tick; the real build keeps pricing in the worker (ADR-023).
**Decision.** (1) `lib/strategy/usePortfolio.ts` prices every active strategy of a tab in the worker at the live marks (`toPricingLegs` with the book's `priceOf`), 400 ms after the strategies or the quote version change, and folds the results into net greeks, Σ margin estimates of the defined-risk strategies and a count of the undefined-risk ones (`foldPortfolio`, pure). The strip shows Net Δ (with Θ / day and ν under it) and Margin used; on the live tab the margin is set against the exchange wallet balance the header already reads, with a share bar. (2) Live cards show the order batch id (last six characters) as a tag. (3) Strategy Details grows to six tiles (Total, Unrealised, Realised, Days · since date, Legs open / total · adjustments, Margin est. · POP) and draws the expiry payoff of the open legs at their entry premiums with the analysis pane's canvas renderer (expiry line, fills, break-evens, spot hairline), from the analysis the dialog already ran.
**Consequences.** One worker call per active strategy per debounce window (a busy feed re-prices at most every 400 ms per tab). Margin used is an estimate (worst expiry loss) until the venue reports margin per position (GAPS #57 basis / tickers work). The remaining trading rows need the alerts engine (HC-TR-114, 117, 120, 139) and come with Phase 5 item 2.

## ADR-052 · Alerts: rules on the server, evaluation in the open client, push and email delivery (2026-09-10)
**Context.** Phase 5 item 2 (HC-SH-079, 094..100; HC-TR-114, 117, 120, 139): price, IV and strategy P&L alerts with a bell, an Alerts center, a New alert form and "Set alert" buttons on paper / live cards and Details. The mock evaluated an in-memory store on every tick and "sent" push / email / Telegram. The real build has the quote feed on the client (gateway WebSocket, ADR-018) and no server-side price consumer; IV rank needs a history nobody stores yet (GAPS #62); Telegram has no bot.
**Decision.** (1) Alerts are server records (`alerts` table, migration 0010; `/v1/alerts` list / create / patch / delete / trigger, per user, capped at 50) so they survive reloads and devices and the email channel can be delivered from the API. (2) Evaluation runs in the signed-in client (`AlertEngine`, mounted once in the header): spot per asset from the feed, ATM IV of the nearest listed expiry only for assets with an IV alert (one chain subscription per such asset), P&L from the paper book only when a P&L alert exists; a 1 s trailing throttle; each hit is posted to `POST /v1/alerts/{id}/trigger` with the reading, which marks the alert triggered, audits it and mails the email channel (a bounced mailbox is logged and never undoes the trigger). (3) Kinds are `price` (USD), `iv` (the ATM IV *level* in vol points — "IV rank" waits for GAPS #62) and `pnl` (USD, always, so the condition reads the same in either display currency); a P&L alert snapshots the strategy name. (4) Channels are `push` (in-app toast plus a browser notification once the trader allows it; permission is asked when a push alert is saved) and `email`; Telegram is shown disabled with "soon" (GAPS #64). (5) A triggered alert stays triggered until the trader re-arms it from the row switch; re-arming with the condition still met fires again at once, which is the honest reading of "arm". (6) The workbench's "Alert me if max loss exceeds" note (ADR-044 extra 5) stays a local note: it is about a projected max loss, not a live reading, and is out of scope here.
**Consequences.** Alerts fire only while a HapieCoin tab is open; a background evaluator for closed apps and Telegram delivery are GAPS #64. The engine adds three spot subscriptions per session (the header already holds one) and a chain subscription per asset with an IV alert. Mock parity: the seeded demo alerts (`alerts: true` in the e2e seed) mirror the mock's three rows for the visual captures.

## ADR-053 · Shared chrome extras: header stats from the chain, a sticky portfolio bar, a shortcuts registry and palette groups (2026-09-11)
**Context.** Phase 5 item 3 (HC-SH-077, 078, 083, 086..089, 092, 093, 101..108; HC-WS-004, 068, 070): the mock's header showed ATM IV / IV rank and the expected move, a 32 px portfolio status bar sat under the workspace, "?" opened a shortcuts help fed by a registry, and the palette had Recent / Settings groups, underlined matches and a registry. The real build had the price only, the paper / live strips per tab, chain keys handled inside the chain, and a flat palette.
**Decision.** (1) Header stats read the chain topic the workspace already subscribes to (`useHeaderStats`: shown expiry → `atmIvOf`, `expectedMove`); IV rank stays "—" until the IV history lands (GAPS #62) rather than showing a made-up number. Trimming follows the mock's breakpoints (1500 / 1330 / 1180 / 1000 px). (2) The portfolio bar is a sticky footer in the analyse layout (the workspace reserves 82 px) fed by `usePortfolio(…, "active")` (paper and live together, the worker pricing already used by the tab strips), the paper book for day P&L, the wallet balance for the margin denominator and the alerts query; every item is a button to the place that changes it. It keeps the active legs' quotes subscribed on every workspace tab, which the tab strips did only on their own tab. (3) Shortcuts are a module registry (`lib/shortcuts.ts`): default rows document the keys the chain and Builder already handle; `registerShortcut` adds rows with handlers the global dispatcher runs; typing targets and open dialogs block everything but `always` rows; "?" / T / D are shell keys. (4) The palette gains a registry (`registerCommand`, custom groups appended), recents (last five ids in localStorage, shown as their own group), Settings commands, Tab navigation, underlined matches and a named empty state; scoring moves to `lib/palette.ts` with a word-start bonus.
**Consequences.** One more chain subscription is shared, none added. The bar's permanent quote subscriptions cost a few topics per active strategy. The chain's own key handling is unchanged (no double dispatch: the dispatcher never binds J / K / B / S / E). IV rank and percentile remain open under GAPS #62.

## ADR-054 · Credential key rotation: key ids on sealed rows, a keyring of previous keys, lazy and batch re-seal (2026-09-11)
**Context.** GAPS #10: `CREDENTIALS_ENC_KEY` had no key id or rotation path, so rotating it made every stored exchange credential undecryptable until the trader reconnected. The spec asks for quarterly rotation with a KMS-held key before launch.
**Decision.** (1) Every sealed record carries `kid`, the first 8 hex characters of SHA-256(key) (non-secret); `broker_credentials.key_id` stores it (migration 0011; rows from before are `null`). (2) The vault is a keyring (`createKeyring(current, previous[])`): `seal` always uses the current key, `open` picks the key by `kid` (legacy rows try the current key, then each previous one), `isCurrent` says whether a row should be re-sealed. `CREDENTIALS_ENC_KEYS_PREVIOUS` (comma-separated base64 32-byte keys) lists the keys still allowed to open old rows; an entry equal to the current key is ignored, a malformed one refuses to start. (3) `openCredential` re-seals a row under the current key on its first live use after a rotation (one extra update, logged with the key ids, never the secrets); `pnpm db:reseal` re-seals every row in one pass and reports scanned / current / resealed / unreadable counts by row id. (4) A row whose key is in no ring stays as it is and the existing 409 "reconnect your exchange" path applies. Rotation procedure: generate a new key, set `CREDENTIALS_ENC_KEY` to it and move the old one into `CREDENTIALS_ENC_KEYS_PREVIOUS`, deploy, run `pnpm db:reseal`, then drop the old key from the previous list once the report shows no rows under it.
**Consequences.** Rotation no longer strands anyone. The key material still lives in environment variables; a KMS-held key would replace `credentialsEncKey` loading in `config.ts` without touching the vault contract. Rows written before this change open through the legacy path until their first use or the batch re-seal.

## ADR-055 · A real-integration smoke run: the web app against apps/api and apps/gateway, driven by Playwright (2026-09-11)
**Context.** GAPS #20: every Playwright run drives the in-memory mock API and the fake gateway that mirror the landed contracts; the real web ↔ api ↔ gateway path (Better Auth cookies through the Next rewrite, Drizzle on the real schema, the live Delta feed through the gateway) had never been exercised end to end by a test.
**Decision.** A second Playwright config, `apps/web/playwright.smoke.config.ts` (`pnpm --filter @hapiecoin/web test:smoke`), starts the REAL API and gateway through `test/real-servers.ts` (tsx, ports 3201 / 3202, `next dev` on 3200) with a development configuration that cannot trade: `TRADING_DISABLED=1`, no exchange key, no Resend key, PGlite in memory (or Postgres / Redis via `SMOKE_DATABASE_URL` / `SMOKE_REDIS_URL` after `pnpm db:up`). The capture mailer logs sign-up codes to stdout; a sidecar (:3203) parses them and answers `/otp?email=` so the test verifies the real OTP flow without a dev-only route in the API. `e2e-smoke/smoke.spec.ts` checks health (gateway lists Delta expiries), sign-up → OTP → /analyse with a live chain and header stats, a paper trade and an alert written by the real routes and read back after a reload and through `/v1/me` and `/v1/strategies`, then sign-out. The mock e2e stays the fast, deterministic suite; the smoke run needs network access to Delta Exchange India and is run before a release, not on every commit.
**Consequences.** The web ↔ API contract now has one honest run; the pixel tolerance for the captured screenshots (the other half of GAPS #20) remains open. The smoke run leaves PGlite data in memory only; against Postgres it leaves the smoke user behind (delete or keep as a canary).

## ADR-056 · IV history: the API snapshots ATM IV, spot and option marks from the venue; IV rank, realised vol and the sparkline read from it (2026-09-11)
**Context.** GAPS #62 (IV rank HC-WS-096, realised vs implied HC-WS-097), #32 (the option details sparkline) and #31 (24 h range) all need a price / IV history nobody stored. The ingest service writes analytics snapshots to Redis only; the API already owns Postgres and a background loop (the order reconciler, ADR-029).
**Decision.** (1) The API runs the snapshotter (`iv-snapshot.ts`, every `IV_SNAPSHOT_MS`, default 5 min, 0 = off): it reads the venue's public option tickers per underlying (no key, never a private endpoint) through the schema adapter and writes `iv_snapshots` (ATM IV per listed expiry = mean of the call and put mark IV at the strike nearest the spot, with the spot and the ATM strike; `front` marks the nearest expiry with two or more days left, so the expiry-day IV collapse never enters the year's range) and `instrument_marks` (every option's mark and mark IV). Retention: 400 days of IV rows, 7 days of marks, pruned on each run (migration 0012). A failing underlying is logged and the others still land. (2) Read routes, public like the analytics snapshots: `GET /v1/market/iv?asset=` returns the daily front-expiry series (the last snapshot of each UTC day, ≤ 365 points), the current point, IV rank and percentile over the recorded days (`ivRankOf`), 30-day realised vol from the daily spot closes (`realisedVolOf`, sample stdev of log returns × √365) with the IV − RV spread, and the 24 h spot high / low; 503 until the first snapshot. `GET /v1/market/marks/{symbol}?hours=` returns one option's mark history. (3) The Vol tab replaces its two placeholders with the rank gauge (with the interpretation line) and the realised-vs-implied chart, stating how many days are recorded while the history is short; the header shows "IV rank N"; the option details dialog draws the 24 h mark / IV sparkline. (4) The daily series is *front-expiry* ATM IV, not a constant-maturity interpolation; the ADR records that as the definition of "IV rank" in HapieCoin.
**Consequences.** One REST call per underlying every five minutes; roughly 900 mark rows per run (≈ 1.8 M rows a week at 5 min, pruned). Only one API replica may run the snapshotter (set `IV_SNAPSHOT_MS=0` on the others). The rank reads "n of 365 days" for the first year. GAPS #31 stays open for the chain's OHLC columns (the 24 h range is now available from the API but not carried in the gateway quote). Alerts keep comparing the ATM IV level; a rank-based alert can now be added.

## ADR-057 · Alerts fire server-side on every snapshot and deliver to Telegram through a long-polled bot (2026-09-11)
**Context.** GAPS #64: alerts (ADR-052) were evaluated only while a HapieCoin tab was open and delivered by in-app push and email; Telegram was shown as "soon". The IV snapshotter (ADR-056) now gives the API a reading every five minutes: the spot, the front-expiry ATM IV and every option's mark.
**Decision.** (1) `alerts-evaluate.ts` runs after each snapshot: price alerts against the latest front spot, IV alerts against the latest front ATM IV (vol points), P&L alerts against realised P&L + Σ (latest mark − entry) × lots × lot size over the strategy's open legs, skipped when a leg has no mark in the last 20 minutes. (2) The browser engine stays the fast path while a tab is open; both call one `fireAlert`, whose row update is the guard against double firing (the second caller sees a row that is no longer armed and stops), audited with `source: client | server`. (3) Telegram: `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN` (absent = channel not offered; the token is never logged). A trader presses Connect in the Alerts center, gets a one-time 8-character code and the bot's `t.me/<bot>?start=<code>` deep link; the API long-polls `getUpdates` (no public webhook URL needed), matches `/start <code>` to the user and stores the chat id (`users.telegram_chat_id`, migration 0013); Disconnect clears it, Send test proves delivery. The `telegram` channel is refused on an alert until the chat is linked. (4) Delivery is best-effort per channel: a bounced mail or a blocked bot is logged and never undoes the trigger.
**Consequences.** Server evaluation runs at the snapshot cadence (5 min) and only on the replica that snapshots. In-app push cannot reach a closed app (web push with VAPID keys would; not built). IV *rank* alerts remain a follow-up now that the history exists. The mock API links a pending code on the next status read so the e2e flow needs no bot.

## ADR-058 · Workbench presentation pass: the H2 form, no leg-selection popup (2026-09-11)
**Context.** The shipped workbench (ADR-044) reused the Builder's chain picker (Δ / OI columns, two-line rows), showed each open leg on two lines without a Close, and put "This change" in the footer instead of at the top of the analysis pane. The user compared it against the approved H2 mockup and found the chain "clumsy" and the positions not on one line; the competitor meanwhile moved adjustments into a popup with leg selection, which the user rejected. The model, the netting, the figures and the suggestions were right; the presentation was not.
**Decision.** (1) Keep the workbench as a left-pane mode of /analyse: no popup, no leg selection step; every open leg is already in the ticket. (2) The chain takes the H2 form (`WorkbenchChain`): Calls [B][S] · Mark · IV | Strike (ATM tag) | Mark · IV · [B][S] Puts, a filled pill on strikes the position holds (`10→0` when the change closes them), delta / open interest / bid-ask on hover only, the cap line above ("N more open legs allowed after netting"), the legend below, the same keyboard contract as the Builder picker. (3) Every open leg is one line: side · contract · entry → mark · P&L · lots now → after · effect · Close (lots after 0) or ↺ undo; proposed rows likewise. (4) "This change: …" with its credit / debit and guard rails moves to the top of the analysis pane (`AdjustChangeBox`) above six before → after tiles (max loss, max profit, POP, break-evens, net Δ · Θ/day, margin estimate), each with a ▲ better / ▼ worse verdict. (5) The footer carries the four H2 tiles: You receive / pay at mark with fees est. (ADR-021 fee model on the traded quantities only), Max loss after (before → verdict), Margin est. before → after (worst expiry loss; the venue figure appears at Review), Open legs after · of 10 · N now; then the P&L alert line and Reset / Review N changes. (6) The alert stub (ADR-044 extra 5) becomes real: "Alert me if P&L falls below X → Set alert" opens the Alerts center with a strategy P&L rule filled in (ADR-052, ADR-057); the local `riskAlerts` store slice is no longer written by the UI. (7) The plans comparison table folds under a Plans toggle and opens once a plan exists.
**Consequences.** The Builder keeps its own picker with Δ / OI columns; the two chains share `usePickerChain`, `PickerRow` and `ChainCellState`. A figures checklist test asserts the same change reads the same in the ticket, the change box and the footer (credit = one lot at the mark; loss after and margin after equal in both places). Visual baselines analyse-workbench / analyse-adjust-* change. Under 720 px the columns still stack.
**Addendum (2026-09-11, plans).** The user saw the "Current" row always equal to the plan just saved. Save as plan now keeps the change and starts the next one from the position as it stands; the table gains a "Before" row (the position with no change) and names the working row "Working change" with "unsaved", "nothing yet" or "= Plan A" when it is a copy of a plan (`matchingPlan`).
**Addendum (2026-09-11, guards).** Save as plan is refused while the change is a copy of a kept plan ("Already kept as Plan A"); Proposed gets a Clear all (= Reset); Exit asks before discarding a change or saved plans, as the mockup did ("Discard N proposed legs?"); the question is held by the store (`adjustDiscard`), so every route out asks it: another card, A or Adjust on another card, Back to Builder, the Builder / Chain tab, palette commands, Analyse positions. Adjust on the strategy already being adjusted keeps the work. Every write to the draft passes `normaliseLotsAfter` (entries equal to lots now or for legs no longer open are dropped), so a lots-after key always means an order and "has work" is exact. The question is mounted with the Workspace overlays so it shows in the stacked layout too; the Builder palette commands ask and stop instead of half-running; a trade placed while adjusting keeps the workbench; the tour declines to start while the workbench is open; a share link opened mid-adjustment asks first and leaves the Builder untouched. The question dialog prices only while it is open. The workbench's edge-case list lives in docs/design/adjustment-workbench.md §6, each line tied to a test, and every workbench PR gets a code-reviewer pass on top of the fact-checker.

## ADR-059 · Trade lifecycle: pre-filled names, overlap and drift, strategy stop and target rules, expiry settlement (2026-09-11)
**Context.** The user lost a 1,000-lot position when an exchange-level stop closed a contract shared by two strategies (800 + 200); asked for pre-filled trade names, an expiry on every card with expired strategies filtered out, and named Sensibull and Opstra as competitors. Analysis in docs/design/trade-lifecycle.md (§2.3.1 scenarios) and docs/research/competitors-sensibull-opstra.md; mockup docs/design/mockups/trade-lifecycle-v1.html. The user approved all options on 11 Sep 2026.
**Decision.** (1) Names default to `ASSET-CODE-DDMMMYY-HHMM` (template code table, legs fallback, `-2` on a clash), pre-filled and selected, kept when untouched, for trades and drafts. (2) An overlap line in every preview and in the workbench Review names the other strategy and the exchange's net after the order; never blocks. A drift check compares the exchange's net position per contract with Σ open legs; out-of-sync cards get a badge, a Reconcile (book the legs closed outside the app, no orders) and a Re-enter. (3) Stop and target rules live on the strategy and are executed by HapieCoin: strategy stop (P&L ≤ −X) and target (P&L ≥ +Y) exit **every open leg of that strategy**, its own lots only; a leg stop (a named short leg at N × entry) exits that leg only by the trader's explicit choice; a spot-level rule exits all legs on an underlying price; a time rule later. Levels in money or as a percentage (of credit received, max loss, or debit paid) with the other unit shown live. Execution: short legs first, market at the mark, retries with a visible "still open" state, no re-arm, kill switch and venue check, full audit and Telegram/in-app notice. Checked every tick by the API from the gateway's quote stream (new) and by the browser while open; the strategy row is the double-fire guard. Rules also run on paper strategies (no orders). The panel states that a server rule is best-effort and that an exchange stop acts on the whole netted position. (4) Sub-accounts (verified on Delta India: own margin, orders, positions): an accounts concept later (several labelled keys per exchange, one per strategy); until then the sub-account key can replace the main key in Settings → Exchanges. (5) Paper and Live tabs get lifecycle chips Open · Expiring ≤ 1d · Closed (same tab), start and expiry with days left on every card, sort by expiry, close reasons (expired · squared off · stopped · closed outside the app), and an expiry settlement job at the venue's settlement instant that settles paper legs at the settlement value and books live legs from the exchange, closing the strategy with reason `expired`.
**Consequences.** Five PRs in order: names + expiry + chips; overlap + drift + reconcile; settlement + close reasons; stop/target rules with the 1 s feed in the API; accounts. New traceability rows HC-TR-155 onwards. GAPS #66, #67 close as the PRs land. Every PR: tests at floors, Playwright, code-reviewer and fact-checker passes.
**Addendum (2026-09-11, names).** After PR #47 the user still saw no name dialog: it was skipped whenever the Builder's own name field had text (a pre-existing rule). A trade from the Builder now always confirms its name, pre-filled with the Builder name when there is one and with the suggestion otherwise; paper asks after the preview, live before the exchange preview. Trades started from a card keep their strategy name and do not ask. A Builder name that is only the loaded template's name ("Iron Butterfly", set by TemplatesPanel) is not a trader's name: it gives way to the suggested format (`isTemplateName`).
**Addendum (2026-09-11, calendars).** A calendar the user built (sell 12 Sep 77,200 C, buy 13 Sep 77,200 C) showed a flat −₹615 expiry curve, max profit = max loss, no break-even, POP 0 %: the Builder pane valued every leg as intrinsic at one moment, so same-strike legs cancelled. Decision: a position whose option legs span more than one expiry values its "on expiry" figures at the **nearest** expiry with later legs keeping their time value (`nearestExpiryValuationMs`; the calendar convention CoinGreeks and the NSE tools use), the pane says "Expiry figures on <date> · later legs keep time value", and the workbench's default valuation moves from the latest expiry (ADR-044 §3) to the nearest one, the chips and slider still reaching any other date. Two legs across expiries are named Calendar (same strike) or Diagonal, never Spread; the built-in calendar templates take their far leg from the next listed expiry (its strike and mark used to come from the near chain, so the two legs priced as a zero-debit spread). A settled expiry is skipped when picking the valuation date. The margin estimate is labelled understated for such positions (GAPS #77), and the live preview receives the same worst-loss figure the dialog shows. Checked against the user's calendar at their marks: max profit ₹4,113, max loss −₹620, break-evens 75,481 / 78,974 (CoinGreeks at its own IV: ₹4,924 / ₹642 / 75,094 / 79,383).
**Addendum (2026-09-11, overlap and drift as built).** Item 2 shipped as HC-TR-159..161: the trade preview and the adjust confirm list every contract another strategy of the same mode already holds, with the net before and after; the Live tab compares the exchange net position per contract with the open live legs and flags every strategy on a drifted contract; Reconcile books the missing lots closed at a confirmed price with no venue call (`POST /v1/strategies/{id}/reconcile`, audit `strategy.reconcile`). Two rules from the review: a failed exchange positions read is an error end to end (the venue client throws `positions_unavailable`, the API answers 503, the Live tab shows "could not be read" and pauses the check) because "holds nothing" and "could not read" must never look alike; and every strategy mutation refreshes the exchange positions so the check never compares stale lots. A server-side drift pass and Re-enter stay deferred (GAPS #67).
**Addendum (2026-09-11, expiry settlement and close reasons as built).** Item 3 shipped as HC-TR-162..164. Settlement value: the intrinsic value at the spot of the settlement instant (a dated future at the spot itself), the spot being the IV-history snapshot nearest the instant within 30 minutes, else the venue's live spot while the instant is that fresh, else nothing (the leg waits). Delta's official settlement price is not on the public API, so the leg's history states the spot used. A paper leg settles two minutes after the instant; a live leg settles only after a successful exchange positions read shows the contract gone, so an unreadable exchange books nothing on a guess and a contract the exchange still holds waits. The settler runs every `SETTLEMENT_MS` (60 s, 0 off) in the API, never in tests. Close reasons (`closeReason` on legs and strategies: expired · squared_off · stopped · outside_app) are set by every close path and shown on Closed-chip cards and in the Journal and its CSV; `stopped` is reserved for item 4. The drift check leaves legs past their settlement instant to the settler.
**Addendum (2026-09-11, stop and target rules as built, A4a).** Items shipped as HC-TR-165..168: `strategy_rules` (stop / target, typed in money or as a percentage of the credit received, the debit paid or the max loss, stored with the USD level), `PUT /v1/strategies/{id}/rules` replacing the set (fired rules stay as history), `DELETE` disarming, Reconcile disarming. The engine (`apps/api/src/rules-engine.ts`) runs as a leader-only job every `RULES_TICK_MS` (2 s, 0 off), reads the venue's public tickers once per underlying per tick (options and the perpetual, no key) and values each strategy from its filled open legs; a stop fires at P&L ≤ level, a target at ≥ level, the stop judged first. A fire claims the rule (armed → fired, once across ticks and replicas), exits short legs first then longs at market through `placeExit` with three attempts and a backoff, books each fill with the close reason `stopped` / `target`, disarms the strategy's other rule, records a `rule:` batch in the history, audits `strategy.rule_fire` with marks, fills and failures, and tells the trader by mail / Telegram; a refused leg is named on the card as still open. Paper fires the same way with no orders. The kill switch holds a live fire (the rule stays armed and says why). Decided against for A4a: browser-side firing (one code path; the tick is 2 s anyway) and the gateway's Redis feed (no new infrastructure until the tick proves too slow), both in GAPS #82. The Protect step follows a trade from the Builder and can be switched off; the capital gate now refuses a net debit above the available balance on the server (GAPS #81).

**Addendum (2026-09-11, leg stop, spot level, time exit and re-enter as built, A4b).** Items shipped as HC-TR-169..172 on the same `strategy_rules` table (migration 0016 adds `leg_id`, a cascade reference to the leg, and `scope`). Three more kinds, each with its own triggers: `leg_stop` watches one open leg's mark (`multiple` × its entry price, priced on the server from the leg's entry when armed, or a `price`) and exits that leg alone (`scope: leg`, the default) or the whole strategy; a short leg is stopped when its mark rises to the level, a long one when it falls to it. `spot` watches the underlying (`above` / `below`, the spot taken from the first ticker of the tick that carries one; no spot, no judgement that tick). `time` fires `at` an ISO instant or at `dte` days (inclusive, fractional days counted) to the nearest settlement of the open dated legs, with the reason `squared_off`; with only a perpetual open a days rule is disarmed as void, as is a leg stop whose leg was closed by a click, the settler or another rule. The level column keeps its name (`threshold_usd`) and now holds the kind's own unit; `ruleLevel` in the schema computes it for every kind. One rule per kind, one leg stop per leg, at most twelve. Judging order is stop, leg stop, spot, time, target (`RULE_KIND_ORDER`); a leg-scope fire leaves the strategy's other rules armed unless nothing is left open. The Protect dialog gained a Leg stops block (one row per held leg with the current mark), a Spot level row and a Time exit row (the instant typed on the trader's clock, sent as an instant); the card line and Details name every kind (`ruleText`). The workbench footer carries the armed rules and a Protect button beside the alert line, so the alert (tells) and the rule (exits) sit together. Re-enter on a closed card (any close reason, while its expiry has not passed) creates a fresh draft of the legs the strategy held, at today's marks, named "<name> re-entry", and opens the trade dialog of that tab on it: the entry, the capital check and the Protect step happen there; nothing is placed by the button itself. Decided against: a re-enter that rolls expired legs to the next expiry (that is the adjustment workbench's roll), and a time rule counted to a chosen expiry rather than the nearest (one number the trader can read off the card).


## ADR-060 · Platform direction: strategy catalogue to 48 with futures, a venue port before any second market, ECB rates with a manual override, three Indian languages together, PWA then Capacitor (2026-09-10)
**Context.** The user asked whether the strategies are tested and industry-checked, for futures and futures + options strategies, whether the app will serve NSE India, US and forex markets, multi-currency, multi-lingual, and Android / iOS apps, and for the best scalable, secure product. Three audits (strategy catalogue, portability, security and scale) and an architecture pass are written up in docs/research/platform-roadmap-2026-09.md. Findings: 28 option-only templates, textbook-correct and tested for placement but not per-template payoff shape; futures first-class in schema and pricing but expressible in no template; no venue abstraction (`Venue = "delta"`, hard-wired instrument ids, Delta symbol codec, fees, settlement hours, no `venue` column); Black-76 without a carry rate, 365-day count, European only; USD → INR display conversion by a typed rate; no i18n; desktop-only tests, no PWA; strong vault, rate limits, trading guards and coverage gates; no CI workflow, single-replica gateway and jobs, no error tracking, no TOTP.
**Decision.** (1) The catalogue grows to 48 now, beside the trade-lifecycle PRs: a template may hold a perpetual future leg (materialised at the live spot, `no-spot` refusal), twenty templates are added (futures-only, covered call, protective put, collar, covered put, synthetic straddle, back and ratio spreads, diagonals, double diagonal, calendar strangle, broken-wing butterflies, risk reversal, ladders), a `tags` badge marks futures / calendar / ratio, no instrument-type tab, and a per-template invariants test asserts each template's max profit / loss shape, breakeven count and outlook on an arbitrage-free synthetic ladder. Product copy says "ready-made structures", never "proven": the only data-backed claim will come from backtests on our own snapshots (roadmap item 9). (2) No second market before a venue port: `VenueAdapter` in `packages/venues` with a registry instead of enums, `DeltaIndiaAdapter` wrapping today's code with no behaviour change, a `venue` column with `delta_india` default, pricing gains a carry rate and an injected trading calendar, and the second venue is **Deribit, data-only** (public API, inverse contracts), chosen by the user. NSE follows paper-only through a licensed feed or broker market-data API; live NSE only through a SEBI-registered broker API; US and forex are out of scope for live trading. (3) Currency: `Money { amount, ccy }`, quote / settlement / display separated, ECB reference rates through the ingest with the manual rate kept as an override and shown with its basis; billing stays INR via Razorpay. (4) Languages: next-intl with a cookie locale; **Hindi, Tamil and Telugu ship together**, chosen by the user; trading vocabulary stays English; the assistant gets per-locale match tables. (5) Mobile: **PWA first, then a Capacitor shell** for the stores, chosen by the user; React Native rejected as a rewrite of every component for a team of one. (6) Hardening lands with deployment: CI workflow, body limit and order-route limiter, replica guards and a feed-leader lock, error tracking and API metrics, TOTP and a typed LIVE confirmation, `zod/mini` and the bundle check inside `pnpm ci`.
**Consequences.** Roadmap sections D, E, F (docs/ROADMAP.md); GAPS #68–#75. Hard-coded template counts in tests derive from the catalogue. `packages/schema` and `packages/pricing` stay DOM-free so a native rewrite remains possible. The memory file `.claude/memory/systemPatterns.md` is corrected: the gateway runs on `ws`, there is no BullMQ worker app (the scheduler lives in `apps/ingest`), charts are hand-written canvas, Node is 24.

## ADR-061 · CI on every pull request; every request body bounded; a per-user budget on the order routes (2026-09-11)
**Context.** GAPS #69: the repo had no `.github/` directory, so the strict gates (`pnpm ci`: typecheck, lint, coverage thresholds, build), the 350 KB bundle budget and the Playwright suite only ran when someone typed them. GAPS #70: apart from the 5 MB banner image upload no request body was bounded, and the routes that send orders to the exchange shared the 300 requests / minute global bucket with everything else. Both were listed as go-live blockers (roadmap F24, F25; docs/research/platform-roadmap-2026-09.md §6).
**Decision.** (1) `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs on every pull request and every push to main: a **checks** job (`pnpm ci` then `check:budget`, coverage summaries as an artifact) and an **e2e** job (Playwright against the mock API and fake gateway, Chromium only, `PW_CHANNEL=chromium`, report uploaded on failure) in parallel, and a **docker** job on main that builds the api and gateway images without pushing. `smoke.yml` runs the real-API smoke (ADR-055) on manual dispatch only, because it needs Delta network access. `dependabot.yml` groups minor and patch npm bumps weekly and watches the actions. `prettier --check` stays out until the tree is formatted; the smoke run stays manual. (2) `security/body-limit.ts` caps every request body at `API_BODY_LIMIT_BYTES` (1 MB) as the second middleware, before the auth handler, guards and validators, answering 413 `PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE` in the ApiError envelope; routes with their own larger limit (the banner image, 5 MB) are exempt by prefix. (3) `orderRateLimit` in `security/rate-limit.ts` budgets the live placement, retry, batch and positions-exit routes at `ORDER_RATE_MAX_PER_MIN` (20) per signed-in user, mounted after the session guard so a refused request never reaches the executor; 429 `ORDER_RATE_LIMITED` with `Retry-After`. The budget counts requests: a batch request may carry up to twenty strategies, and a body that fails validation still spends one. The adjust route and the two close routes (orders in live mode) get the same middleware once the trade-lifecycle A2 branch, which edits that file, has merged. The order limiter writes its own `X-Order-RateLimit-*` headers so the global `X-RateLimit-*` keep one meaning. (4) The repository carries a proprietary `LICENSE` and `"license": "UNLICENSED"`.
**Consequences.** A pull request cannot merge green without typecheck, lint, the coverage floors, the build and the e2e suite passing on Linux. The bundle budget runs on every PR but only warns (`continue-on-error`) until roadmap F29 brings /analyse back under 350 KB: at ADR time main measures 382.8 KB (GAPS #19). Both limits are environment-tunable and tested (`config.test.ts`, `security.test.ts`). GAPS #69 and #70 close; roadmap F24 and F25 are done. Web and ingest Dockerfiles and a deploy job join the workflow with deployment (C13).

## ADR-062 · Replicas: a Redis leader lease for the API jobs and the gateway feed; follower gateways serve clients from a shared registry and snapshot store (2026-09-11)
**Context.** Every background job (order reconciler, IV snapshotter with the alerts pass, Telegram linker) started in every API process, so a second replica would double-run them (GAPS #71); every gateway process opened its own venue feed and published every frame, so two gateways double-published (GAPS #16). The stack could not be restarted without downtime or scaled past one copy of each process (roadmap F26; docs/research/platform-roadmap-2026-09.md §6). The user asked for fan-out across gateways, not only standby.
**Decision.** (1) A leader lease in Redis (`SET key owner NX PX ttl`, renew and release by compare-and-set script; `leader.ts` in both apps, the memory lock for a single process) with `runAsLeader`: acquire, renew every ttl / 3, give the role up on a failed renew or a Redis error, keep retrying. (2) API: `startJobs` starts the jobs on acquire and stops them on loss; `API_JOBS_ROLE` = leader (default) / always / off; `LEADER_TTL_MS` 15 s; `/healthz` reports `jobs: { role, active }`. (3) Gateway: `RoleFeed` wraps the feed in two modes. The leader runs the venue socket and publishes; a follower loads the instrument list over REST, opens no socket and serves clients: its clients' holds go to the **held-topic registry** (one Redis hash of (topic, instance) leases, re-leased every `HOLD_SYNC_MS`) so the leader watches the union across gateways; a follower's first frame comes from the **snapshot store** (the leader writes each held chain's rows with its `seq` at most once a second while it changes, and every spot) with a short wait for a topic nobody held before, else the local REST rows at seq 0; live frames arrive through the Redis fan-out; frames that arrive while the first snapshot is fetched are queued and replayed after it. Mode changes (lease taken or lost) never touch client connections; a new leader watches its clients' topics at once and the registry's within one sync, and it **announces a snapshot frame on the fan-out for every chain it (re)watches**: its per-topic seq starts again, and the web reducer ignores frames at or below the seq it last saw, so without the announced snapshot every client would freeze after a takeover; the snapshot resets each subscriber on every gateway and the following deltas continue from it (a server drops an announced snapshot queued behind the one it is sending itself). `GATEWAY_ROLE` = auto (default) / follower; `/healthz` carries `role`, `/metrics` `hapiecoin_gateway_leader`. Without Redis the single process is the leader through the memory lock, so a one-copy deployment behaves as before.
Every lock call is bounded to ttl / 2 (a hung Redis connection counts as a lost lease, because ioredis queues commands while disconnected), and a leader whose `onAcquire` throws (the venue session did not start) gives the lease back and retries. Instance ids always carry a host:pid:random suffix, so one env file shared by several replicas cannot make them share a lock owner. The leader rewrites every held chain to the store at least every 20 s even without ticks, so a quiet expiry never expires under a follower.
**Split-brain window.** After a partition or a process pause the old leader can keep publishing until its next bounded renew fails: at most renewMs + timeoutMs (about 12 s at the 15 s default lease). Both leaders then write the snapshot store (last writer wins) and both publish frames; the announced snapshot from the new leader and the reducer's gap handling put every client on the new stream once the old one stops. Acceptable for market data; the API jobs are idempotent per pass (the reconciler re-reads venue order state), so a doubled pass costs a duplicate query, not a duplicate order.
**Consequences.** Any number of API and gateway replicas can run; exactly one runs the jobs and one the feed; takeover within the lease. GAPS #16 and #71 close. The client reducer's gap handling covers the seq change at a failover (a fresh snapshot after resubscribe). Not yet done: a two-process run against a real Redis (Docker) recorded with deployment (C13); the ingest scheduler stays one replica by convention. Tests: leader.test.ts (both apps), jobs.test.ts, coordination.test.ts, role.test.ts, fanout.test.ts (two gateways on a fake Redis: follower first snapshot, live frame through the fan-out, takeover).

## ADR-063 · Venue port: `VenueAdapter`, a registry keyed by the schema venue id, Delta India behind it with no value changed (2026-09-11)
**Context.** ADR-060 (2) ruled that no second market comes before a venue port. Until now the apps imported Delta clients and mappings directly: `DeltaRestClient`, `DeltaTradingClientImpl` and the schema-adapter functions in `apps/api/src/main.ts`, `createDeltaMarketData` in the gateway, the lot sizes `{ BTC 0.001, ETH 0.01, XAUT 0.001 }` typed out in three API files, the seed broker's fees in `seed.ts`, the literal `"delta_india"` in the gateway feed, and the symbol *format* only in the web client (`deltaSymbol`) while the *parse* lived in the venues package.
**Decision.** `packages/venues/src/port/` holds the port: `VenueAdapter { id, label, underlyings, markets, symbols, calendar, fees, capabilities, schema, rest(), marketData(), trading?() }` with `MarketSpec { quoteCcy, settleCcy, multiplier, lotSize, tickSize, exerciseStyle, settlementHourUtc, timezone, isInverse, perpetualSymbol }`, a `SymbolCodec` (parse and format), a `VenueCalendar`, `FeeDefaults` in the shape of the `brokers` table, `VenueCapabilities` flags and a `SchemaBridge` to `@hapiecoin/schema`. `VENUE_REGISTRY` is keyed by the schema `Venue` id (`delta_india`), so the venues package keeps its internal `"delta"` tag and `SCHEMA_VENUE` remains the bridge (GAPS #8). `getVenue(id)` throws `UnknownVenueError`; `marketOf` and `tradingClientOf` throw `VenueCapabilityError` for a market or a trading client a venue lacks, which is how a data-only venue (Deribit, E20 step 4) will present. `DELTA_INDIA` wraps today's code: contract sizes and ticks from the recorded product list, 12:00 UTC settlement (16:00 for XAUT), USD quoting and settlement, European exercise, linear contracts, the seed fee defaults 0.05 / 18 / 10, and `formatDeltaOption` with the option half of the web client's exact algorithm (the future leg's symbol is `symbols.perpetual`). The API resolves `getVenue(DEFAULT_VENUE)` once and takes its trading and REST clients, schema mapping, lot-size defaults (`defaultLotSizes`, "1" for a market the venue lacks, the old fallback) and seed fees from it; the gateway takes its market-data session and venue id from it. `DEFAULT_VENUE` is the single constant to replace by the `venue` column in step 2.
**Step 1 of 4, no behaviour change.** Every value the apps used before is asserted equal in `port.test.ts` against the products fixture (972 products), the web formatter's vectors and the seed values; the API and gateway suites run unchanged. Not in this step: the `venue` column and instrument-id widening (step 2), pricing with a carry rate and a trading calendar (step 3), the Deribit adapter and a per-venue gateway feed (step 4). The web client keeps its own copy of the codec, lot sizes and the settlement rule until step 2 gives it a venue to ask (GAPS #79). The factories are typed with the Delta classes in this step; step 4 narrows them to venue-agnostic slices (the gateway's `MarketDataLike` is one already). Registry and market lookups use own keys only, so a prototype name such as "toString" is an unknown venue or market, never a function.
**Consequences.** Adding a venue is one registry entry plus its id in `VENUES`; nothing in the apps names Delta for a client, a size or a fee any more. `packages/schema` stays the vocabulary of ids. Tests: `packages/venues/src/port/port.test.ts` (HC-SH-118).

## ADR-064 · Venue port: a browser-safe core entry, the web client asks the port instead of carrying Delta copies (2026-09-11)
**Context.** E20 step 2 moves the web client's copies of the Delta specifics (GAPS #79) onto the port from ADR-063. The web app could not import `@hapiecoin/venues`: the trading client signs requests with `node:crypto`, and the port registry reaches it through the Delta adapter's factories, so the package root cannot enter a browser bundle. The bundle is also over its budget already (GAPS #19), so the web client must not pull in the REST, socket or trading code it never uses.
**Decision.** The port splits into `VenueCore` (id, label, underlyings, market specs, symbol codec, calendar, fee defaults, capabilities, schema bridge) and `VenueAdapter extends VenueCore` (the REST, market-data and trading factories). `port/delta-india.ts` holds `DELTA_INDIA_CORE`; `port/delta-india-clients.ts` adds the factories as `DELTA_INDIA`; `port/core.ts` is published as `@hapiecoin/venues/core` with `VENUE_CORES`, `getVenueCore`, `DEFAULT_VENUE` and the helpers, and the package root keeps the full registry for the API and the gateway. The codec gains `parsePerpetual`. The web app depends on the core entry only (transpiled, aliased to the built `dist/port/core.js` like the other workspace packages): `venueSymbol` replaces `deltaSymbol` (perpetual and option formatting from the codec), `parseVenueSymbol` replaces `parseDeltaSymbol` (perpetual, option, own-listing check; an impossible calendar date now yields null instead of a bogus leg, and the strike comes back in canonical decimal form, "080000" as "80000"), the adjustment workbench takes its lot defaults from `defaultLotSizes`, the expiry discovery takes its settlement hour from the venue calendar, and the seven `"delta_india"` topic literals become `CURRENT_VENUE`. `apps/web/src/lib/venue.ts` (`CURRENT_VENUE`, `currentVenue()`) is the one seam step 2b replaces with the strategy's venue; an ESLint rule keeps the package root out of the web app.
**Consequences.** Nothing in the web client names Delta for a symbol, a size, a settlement hour or a venue id any more; step 2b replaces `CURRENT_VENUE` with the strategy's `venue` column. A second venue implements `VenueCore` for the browser and `VenueAdapter` for the servers. The schema's own `settlementHourUtc` rule (packages/schema/src/strategies.ts) stays the pricing default until step 3 injects a trading calendar. Tests: `port.test.ts` (core and registry list the same venues, the core carries no factory, prototype keys throw), web `legs.test.ts`, `positions.test.ts` (HC-SH-119).

## ADR-065 · The venue column: strategies, brokers, alerts and history rows name their venue; a broker cannot trade a strategy of another venue (2026-09-11)
**Context.** E20 step 2b (ADR-060 decision 2). After the port (ADR-063) and the web client on it (ADR-064) the database still had no idea of a venue: strategies, brokers, alerts, iv_snapshots and instrument_marks were keyed by asset and a bare Delta symbol, the seed broker was Delta only by name, and the shared `Strategy`, `Alert`, `Broker`, `IvHistory` and `MarkHistory` objects carried no venue. A second venue would have mixed its rows with Delta's.
**Decision.** Migration 0016 adds `venue text not null default 'delta_india'` to the five tables and leads the history indexes with it (`iv_snapshots (venue, asset, ts)`, `(venue, asset, front, ts)`, `instrument_marks (venue, symbol, ts)`); `strategy_rules` inherits the venue through its strategy. The shared objects gain `venue: Venue` (required on the server shapes; `StrategyCreate`, `AlertCreate` and the broker create input default it to the only venue, so every existing client body still parses). The API writes the venue on create, a P&L alert takes its strategy's venue, the seed broker names it, the snapshotter stamps its source's venue on every row, and history reads (`ivHistory`, `markHistory`, the alert evaluator's front reading and mark P&L, the settler's spot) are scoped to a venue, with an optional `venue` query on the market routes (an unknown one is a 400). Paper start refuses a broker whose venue differs from the strategy's with a 409 naming both; the live preview reports the mismatch as its first reason before any call reaches the venue, live place answers 409, and a live batch reports it as the failed item with the strategies placed so far (the broker's venue is read once per batch). The web client sends `CURRENT_VENUE` when it creates a strategy, an alert or an exchange, and the adjustment workbench takes its lot defaults from the strategy's own venue.
**Consequences.** Every row that a second venue would need to keep apart now carries the key, and the single-venue behaviour is unchanged (the default fills every existing row and body). Still on `DEFAULT_VENUE` / `CURRENT_VENUE` until step 4 (GAPS #83): the one gateway feed, the one trading client, the lot-size fallback in the live executor and the alert evaluator, and the client's chain and quote subscriptions. Instrument-id widening needs no code of its own: `INSTRUMENT_ID_RE` and the topic regexes derive from `VENUES`, so the second id lands with the Deribit adapter (step 4). Migration numbering with the parallel A4b branch was agreed as "the second to merge regenerates as 0017, never renames". Tests: `apps/api/src/venue-column.test.ts` (HC-SH-120), the schema, API and web fixtures updated for the new field.

## ADR-066 · Pricing: carry (r, q) and an injected trading calendar; exercise style shown, never modelled (2026-09-11)
**Context.** E20 step 3 (ADR-060 decision 2). The engine priced Black-76 on the spot as the forward with a rate the apps never set, measured time as act/365 with a settlement hour every caller passed by hand (three copies of the 365-day count, a dozen call sites naming the hour), and knew nothing of exercise style. A second venue with a carry (a dividend or foreign-rate yield), another settlement hour or a business-day count had no seam, and the pricing worker crosses a `postMessage` boundary, so any seam has to be plain data.
**Decision.** `packages/pricing` gains `bsm.ts` (Black-Scholes-Merton as Black-76 on the forward F = S · e^{(r − q) T}, discounted at r; delta and gamma per unit of spot; `forwardPrice`, `bsmPrice`, `bsmGreeks`, `bsmImpliedVol`) and `calendar.ts` (`TradingCalendar { daysPerYear, settlementHourUtc }` as data, `ACT_365`, `calendarFor`, `settlementMsOf`, `yearFractionOf`, `daysToExpiryOf`). `ValuationOptions` take `calendar`, `rate` and `dividendYield`; the engine builds every leg's T on the calendar and a per-leg growth factor, and with the defaults (no rate, no carry, act/365 at the caller's hour) every result is bit-identical to before: the whole existing suite passes unchanged, and the new tests assert exact equality. The web client takes its calendar from the venue port (`venueCalendar(asset)`, act/365 at the venue's settlement hour) and its settlement hour from the same port instead of the schema constant, names the exercise style on the Greeks model caption and in the option details, and flags American as "European-model estimate" because the engine prices European options only (display, never a different model). The API reads the settlement instant of a leg and the snapshotter's front expiry from the venue's calendar, so XAUT's 16:00 settlement is honoured there too.
**Consequences.** A second venue supplies a calendar and, when its quotes carry one, a rate and carry, without touching the engine. Rho stays the forward-price rho of Black-76 (documented in bsm.ts); theta at a fixed spot carries the forward's drift and is always per 365-day calendar day, whatever the calendar's day count. A non-integer or out-of-range settlement hour now throws instead of being truncated by `Date.UTC`; no caller passed one. Still act/365-with-12:00 by their own arithmetic (GAPS #84): the client's `daysToExpiry` and `daysLeft` helpers, `nearestExpiryValuationMs`, the position ticket, the realised-vol annualisation, the chain's expected move, and the schema's asset-keyed `settlementHourUtc`; they move to the calendar when a venue needs it. Tests: `calendar.test.ts`, `bsm.test.ts`, the calendar and carry cases in `strategy.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/venue.test.ts`, the front-expiry hour case in `iv-snapshot.test.ts` (HC-SH-121).

## ADR-067 · Deribit, data-only: a second venue behind the port, inverse contracts priced in USD at the adapter, one gateway session per venue (2026-09-11)
**Context.** ADR-060 decision 2 chose Deribit, data-only, as the second venue because its inverse BTC- and ETH-settled contracts exercise the port properly. Verified on 11 Sep 2026 against the live public API (fixtures in `spec/fixtures/deribit-*.json`) and docs.deribit.com: `public/get_instruments` (one request per second sustained), `public/get_book_summary_by_currency`, `public/ticker`, and the JSON-RPC socket with `public/subscribe` on `ticker.<name>.100ms`, server heartbeats and `test_request` frames answered with `public/test`. Options are one coin per contract, quoted and settled in the coin, with `mark_iv` in percent, `open_interest` in coin and names `BASE-DMMMYY-STRIKE-C|P` (decimal strikes written `0d5`, the day without a leading zero); they settle at 08:00 UTC. A live comparison of sixteen near-the-money tickers with Black-76 on the venue's forward showed the venue's delta, gamma, vega (per vol point) and theta (per day) equal to the engine's per-unit greeks.
**Decision.** (1) `packages/venues/src/deribit/` (raw, normalize, rest, ws, market-data) plus `port/deribit.ts` (`DERIBIT_CORE`: BTC and ETH, `isInverse: true`, `settleCcy` the coin, settlement 08:00, zero fee defaults, `liveTrading: false`, no spot fallback) and `port/deribit-clients.ts` (`DERIBIT`: REST and market-data factories, no trading factory, so `tradingClientOf` refuses it). The adapter converts every coin price to USD per underlying unit with the index (`premium = mark × index`, bid and ask alike, the seed uses the delivery estimate) and stamps `quoteAsset: "USD"`, so the chain, the engine and the client keep their one convention; greeks pass through unchanged; the internal expiry code stays DDMMYY so the chain helpers serve both venues. (2) The port's factories are typed with venue-agnostic interfaces: `VenueRest { products, tickers }` (what the API's snapshotter and rules tick read) and `VenueMarketData` (the gateway's session shape); the Delta REST loop moved to a shared retrying `JsonHttp`; `chain.ts` and the schema bridge stamp the record's own venue (`schemaVenueOf`); `VENUES` gains `deribit`, so the instrument-id and topic regexes widen by construction. (3) The gateway opens one session per venue in `GATEWAY_VENUES` (default `delta_india`; `DERIBIT_REST_URL`, `DERIBIT_WS_URL`, `DERIBIT_WS_INTERVAL`), routes every chain topic to its venue's session, announces and diffs on `chain:deribit:…`, reports every venue in `/healthz` `feed.venues`, and keeps the venue-free `spot:` topics as the default venue's index (another venue's quotes carry their own). A venue the gateway does not open is simply unsupported, and a failed secondary load never blocks the default feed. (4) The API accepts Deribit exchanges and paper strategies on them, but refuses API keys and any live path for a data-only venue with a 409 that says so (`dataOnlyReason`).
**Consequences.** A Deribit chain can be served today by enabling the venue on a gateway; the client still asks for `delta_india` until 4b adds the venue switch (store, header chip, venue-keyed chain and leg-quote subscriptions), and 4c moves the API's trading client and lot defaults to the broker's / strategy's venue (GAPS #83 stays open for those two). Tests: `deribit/*.test.ts` (names round-trip over all 950 fixture instruments, USD conversion, greeks against the forward model, REST envelopes and retries, the socket protocol, the session), `port/deribit.test.ts`, `schema-adapter.test.ts`, the gateway's `feed-venues.test.ts`, `config.test.ts`, `market-data.test.ts`, `app.test.ts`, and the API's data-only case (HC-SH-122).

## ADR-068 · Accounts: several labelled exchange keys per broker; a strategy trades through one of them (2026-09-11)

**Context.** Delta Exchange India offers sub-accounts, each with its own margin balance, orders and positions (research §6; the user confirmed one on the demo account). Roadmap A5 (trade-lifecycle §2.3 C): a trader who keeps one strategy per sub-account is shielded from an exchange-level stop on a shared contract, but HapieCoin held one key per exchange, chose it by broker id everywhere, and compared every live strategy with one positions read.

**Decision.** An *account* is one connected key. `broker_credentials` gains `label` (default "Main") and the unique index becomes (user, broker, label); at most `MAX_ACCOUNTS_PER_BROKER` (5) per exchange. `strategies.account_id` names the key a strategy trades through (a nullable reference; `set null` on delete, but the route refuses the delete first). The key is resolved once, on start or place, by `openCredential(deps, user, brokerId, venue?, accountId?)`: the named account, else the broker's only key; several keys with none named is a 409, never a guess. Every path that opens a key for a strategy (place, retry, sync, exits, adjustments, close-all, the reconciler, the settler, the rules engine, the positions and positions-exit routes) passes the strategy's account. When a second key arrives for an exchange, the paper and live strategies on that exchange with no account named are stamped with the first key, the one they were placed through. `DELETE /v1/credentials/{id}` removes one key and answers 409 while a live strategy trades through it (or, from before accounts, through that exchange with no key named); an exchange id is still accepted while the exchange has a single key. Web: API Settings lists the keys with their labels and a per-row Disconnect, and proposes the next free name (Main, Sub 1, …); the trade-mode dialog shows an Account select when the chosen exchange has more than one key (the strategy's own account preselected on Go live); the Live tab makes one positions read per distinct account and compares each strategy with its own (a strategy from before accounts on an exchange with several keys shows "no account named" and is left out of the check); the wallet chip, the portfolio bar, net positions and the workbench read the account chosen last (`store.accountId`), with a select in the net-positions panel. The card and Details show the account label once an exchange has more than one key.

**Consequences.** Paper strategies record an account too, so Go live and the drift check read the right key. A trader can map one strategy to one sub-account and let the exchange net per sub-account. Not verified: whether Delta scopes an API key to the sub-account it was created under; the design does not depend on it (a key is whatever Delta scopes it to), and the API Settings copy tells the trader to switch to the sub-account before creating the key (GAPS #85). Rejected: separate HapieCoin logins per sub-account (breaks the Journal and the verified P&L later); a silent fallback to another key on delete (an exit through the wrong sub-account is worse than none). Tests: `credentials.test.ts` HC-SH-123, `trading-live.test.tsx` HC-TR-173..175, `dialogs.test.tsx`, e2e `settings.spec.ts`.

## ADR-069 · The venue is workspace state: a header chip, venue-keyed subscriptions, data-only gating in the client (2026-09-12)
**Context.** After ADR-067 the gateway serves a Deribit chain, but the client still asked for `delta_india` through a constant (`CURRENT_VENUE`) read by the chain, term-structure and leg-quote subscriptions, the option details dialog and the four create bodies (builder save, trade flow, alerts, exchanges); the symbol codec, the settlement hour and the expiry liveness filter read `currentVenue()`, the expiries came from the gateway's default-venue list, the market history client never sent `?venue=`, and lot sizes came from the trader's Delta settings for every venue. Deribit lists BTC and ETH only and is data-only.
**Decision.** (1) The workspace store owns the venue (`UiState.venue`, persisted, an unknown id falls back to the default). `setVenue` is a workspace reset: the Builder legs, strategy meta and adjust work of every asset go, the asset moves to one the venue lists, and the header chip asks first when legs exist. The store binds its reader into `lib/venue.ts` at load (`bindVenueSource`), so the codec, calendar, settlement hour and lot sizes follow the chip without the plain modules importing the store; components read `useVenueId()` / `useVenueCore()`. (2) Every subscription is keyed by venue: `useChain`, `useChains`, `useLegQuotes` (a strategy passes its own venue; the paper book keeps one subscription set per venue and asset), the chain panel and the option details topics; the create bodies send the workspace venue (an alert on a strategy sends the strategy's). (3) Expiries per venue: `discoverExpiries(asset, { venue })` reads `feed.venues[id].expiries` off the default (the `GatewayHealth` schema gains `feed.venues`), filters liveness with that venue's settlement hour, and has no env fallback there; the query keys carry the venue and every consumer (chain panel, chain picker, templates loader, per-leg select) reads the one `useExpiriesQuery`. The pane panels (greeks, scenarios, payoff IV curves) price on the analysis venue (`StrategyAnalysis.venue`). The market history client sends `&venue=` off the default and keys its cache by venue. (4) Lot sizes: `lotSizeFor(venue, asset, settings)` keeps the trader's Settings lot sizes on the default venue and uses the venue's listed lot elsewhere (4c moves the setting itself). (5) Data-only gating mirrors the API's 409s: the trade-mode dialog blocks Live with the plain sentence (`dataOnlyNote`), the exchange form names the venue it creates on, and the asset chips grey an asset the venue does not list. Exchange positions parse with the connected exchange's codec (Delta India; 4c keys them by the credential's venue). Loading a strategy into the Builder, opening the workbench on it (`openAdjust(id, force, venue)`) or opening a share link first switches the workspace venue to the strategy's through `requestVenue`, the same store-owned question the header chip uses (`VenueSwitchDialog`, mounted with the Workspace overlays: asked only when Builder legs exist), so no Delta leg can land on a Deribit strategy or the reverse; strategy-scoped figures (cards, rules, details, plans, portfolio, lifecycle) take the strategy's venue for the lot size and the settlement hour; the workbench prices with the strategy's venue lot size; share codes carry the venue (`e`, absent = Delta India); batch live excludes data-only strategies and exchanges; and the API refuses a strategy whose asset the venue does not list (400). (6) Spot stays the venue-free topic (ADR-067 decision 3): a Deribit chain shows Delta's index in the header and in spot-based figures until spot topics are keyed by venue (GAPS #86).
**Consequences.** A trader can view and paper-trade Deribit chains today; every Delta path is unchanged with the default venue (the e2e topic assertion and the create-body fixtures still read `delta_india`). Tests: `lib/venue.test.ts`, `lib/store.test.ts`, `lib/strategy/share.test.ts`, the API's `venue-column.test.ts` (listed-asset guard), `lib/gateway/hooks.test.tsx`, `lib/chain/expiries.test.ts`, `lib/api/market.test.ts`, `lib/strategy/positions.test.ts`, `components/header/widgets.test.tsx`, `components/trading/trading-more.test.tsx`, `components/dialogs/dialogs.test.tsx` (HC-SH-124). Left to 4c: the API's trading client and lot defaults per venue, positions by credential venue; GAPS #83 stays open for 4c, GAPS #86 records the venue-free spot.

## ADR-070 · The API per venue: a trading client by broker venue, lot defaults by strategy venue, market-data jobs per venue (2026-09-12)
**Context.** After ADR-067 and ADR-069 the gateway and the client work per venue, but the API still built one adapter from `DEFAULT_VENUE`: one trading client, one public REST client, and `lotSizeFor(deps, user, asset)` falling back to Delta India's lots for every venue.
