A Deribit paper strategy's stop rule was skipped on every tick (the tick read Delta's marks, keyed by bare symbol, cached by asset), a Deribit price or P&L alert never fired (no Deribit snapshots or marks were written), a Deribit expiry had no settlement spot, and the positions payload named no venue. The harness holds one `FakeDeltaTradingClient` that ten test files drive through `t.trading`; main.ts is outside coverage.
**Decision.** (1) `apps/api/src/venues.ts` builds the API's venue clients from the port: a trading client for every adapter with a `trading` factory (Delta India today; `tradingFor` on a data-only or unknown venue throws `VenueCapabilityError`, which the routes never reach because `dataOnlyReason` and the venue-mismatch guard answer 409 first), and for every venue in `API_VENUES` (csv, default `delta_india`; unknown ids are a `ConfigError`; `DERIBIT_REST_URL` defaults to the public base) a public REST client, a venue-stamped snapshotter source, a live spot and a rules tick, each through that venue's schema bridge. (2) `AppDeps.trading` stays as the default venue's client for the test harness only (deprecated; no production caller) and `AppDeps.tradingFor(venue)` is the per-venue seam: `planLegs`, `preview`, `placeEntries`, `retryFailed`, `tryExit`, `syncOrders`, the exit-fill booking, the settler's positions read and the positions routes all take the strategy's or the account's venue (`resolveAccount` now returns the broker's venue with the credentials; `planLegs` requires the venue, so no caller can fall back to a default). A live preview of a strategy on a data-only venue, or against a broker of another venue, asks no executor at all: the legs are listed unsized and the reasons say why (never a 500). (3) `lotSizeFor(deps, user, asset, venue)`: the trader's Settings lot sizes on the default venue, the venue's listed lot elsewhere (the client's rule from ADR-069); every caller passes the strategy's venue (rules engine, settlement, executor, strategies routes, square-off, alert P&L). (4) Jobs per venue: one `iv-snapshotter:<venue>` job per enabled venue (the first venue's job evaluates the armed alerts once per interval; they read the venue-scoped tables), the settler's live spot takes the venue, and `RulesTickSource.tick(asset, venue)` returns null for a venue this API does not read (its rules wait and the pass logs it once) with the tick cache keyed by venue and asset. (5) `LivePositions` gains an optional `venue`, the account's exchange, so the client parses the symbols with that codec.
**Consequences.** With `API_VENUES=delta_india,deribit` a Deribit paper strategy's stop and target rules, P&L alerts, IV and mark history and expiry settlement all work from Deribit's own data and lot size; with the default configuration nothing changes for Delta India. GAPS #83 closes; GAPS #87 keeps what is still single-venue: the credential-verify client `deps.delta`, the user's lot sizes stored per asset rather than per venue, and legs matched to venue positions by raw symbol equality. Tests: `venues.test.ts` (clients from the recorded fixtures through an injected fetch), `config.test.ts`, `venue-column.test.ts` (Deribit lot defaults, a Deribit stop fired from the Deribit tick, the positions venue), `rules-engine.test.ts` (one read per venue and asset, a silent venue waits), `settlement.test.ts` (the spot source asked for the strategy's venue at 08:00 UTC, booked with the Deribit lot), `alerts-server.test.ts` (Deribit marks value a Deribit strategy) (HC-SH-125).

## ADR-071 · Spot per venue: `spot:<venue>:<asset>` topics, the venue on the frame, every venue's session feeds its own index (2026-09-12)
**Context.** ADR-067 kept the `spot:` topics venue-free and Delta India's, so after the venue switch (ADR-069) a Deribit chain showed Delta's index in the header and in every spot-based figure (ATM, expected move, payoff spot line, template stats, the client alert engine), while the chain rows, the server alert evaluator and the settler already used Deribit's own index (GAPS #86). The grammar had no venue segment, the frame carried none, the gateway kept one spot per asset fed by the default session only, the store key was `spot:<asset>`, the web cached one spot per asset, and a Deribit session never delivered a perpetual ticker because its instrument map held options only.
**Decision.** (1) Grammar: `spot:<venue>:<asset>`; the bare `spot:<asset>` stays valid as an alias for the default venue (`parseTopic` fills the venue in, `canonicalTopic` gives the one spelling), and the spot frame gains `v` (optional in the schema for old producers; the gateway always sets it). (2) The gateway canonicalises a bare spot subscription, keys its spots, the coalescer and the store by venue and asset (`hapiecoin:gw:spot:<venue>:<asset>`; old keys expire within the TTL), resolves the spot symbol through the port (`ownMarket(...).perpetualSymbol`, so a pair the venue does not list is an unsupported topic), watches the perpetual on the topic's venue session, learns the spot from every quote of that venue, and reports `feed.venues[id].spot` in `/healthz` beside the flat default-venue `spot`. A chain snapshot and a schema quote fall back to their own venue's spot only. (3) `DeribitMarketData.load()` also lists the futures so the perpetuals are known by symbol: a subscribed `BTC-PERPETUAL` ticker becomes a quote whose `spot` is the index. (4) The web caches spot per venue and asset, `useSpot(asset, venue?)` follows the workspace venue by default (a strategy passes its own; `null` skips a pair the venue does not list), the paper book keeps one spot per venue and asset and `spotOf(asset, venue)`, the alert engine's readings are keyed `venue:asset` so a price alert reads its own venue's index like the server does, the public landing and sign-in strips stay on the default venue, and the header's futures label and the future dialog name the venue's perpetual from the port. (5) The e2e fake gateway answers venue-keyed spot subscriptions and broadcasts on the venue topic.
**Consequences.** A Deribit chain shows Deribit's index everywhere; Delta India is unchanged with the default venue (the bare topic and frames without `v` still mean Delta). Followers and leaders must be upgraded together: a follower on the previous build reads the old store key and channel and gets no spot until it is upgraded (the feed also canonicalises holds, so a remote hold in the old spelling shares the subscription rather than fighting it). `c24` is still never produced by the real gateway for any venue (a pre-existing gap noted in GAPS #87). Tests: schema `gateway.test.ts` (grammar, alias, canonical form, the frame's `v`), venues `deribit/market-data.test.ts` (the perpetual ticker), gateway `feed.test.ts`, `feed-venues.test.ts`, `coalescer.test.ts`, `coordination.test.ts`, `role.test.ts`, `feed-roles.test.ts`, `server.test.ts`, `server-follower.test.ts`, web `client.test.ts`, `hooks.test.tsx`, `widgets.test.tsx`, `landing.test.tsx`, `alerts/engine.test.ts` (HC-SH-126).

## ADR-072 · Strategy Wizard: a view, a move and a date become three defined-risk strategies priced at the live chain (2026-09-12)
**Context.** Roadmap item 8 from the competitor research: a first-time trader has a thesis ("BTC up 3 % by the 25th"), not a strategy name. The Templates tab already places every template on the venue ladder and prices it in the worker (`useTemplateStats`, ±6 % outlook), and `useTemplateLoader().load` is the one place a template becomes Builder legs; but nothing turns a thesis into a ranked shortlist, the templates carried no risk metadata (only the invariants test's shape table knew which have a bounded loss), and the Builder had two sub-tabs with a coercing tab handler. Design pass: `docs/design/strategy-wizard.md`.
**Decision.** (1) Every template names its risk: `StrategyTemplate.risk` is `"defined"` when the loss at expiry is a finite number and `"undefined"` otherwise (eight templates: the naked short call, the short synthetic and short perp, the covered put, the short straddle and strangle, the jade lizard and the call ratio spread); the invariants test asserts the field against the priced payoff for all 48, so it cannot drift. "Defined" is the mathematical bound, not a promise of a small number: a sold put or a long perpetual is defined and its worst case is shown as the figure it is; the ranking by return on risk pushes such structures to the bottom. (2) The wizard is a third Builder sub-tab, `wizard` (`BuilderSubTab`), reached from the tab bar, the Builder empty state ("Ask the wizard"), the palette command "Strategy wizard" and the shortcut `W` (registered by the workspace while mounted, listed under Analyse workspace in the ? help). Its inputs are component state: the view (Bullish, Bearish, Neutral, Volatile), an unsigned move in percent (the view gives the sign; a typed target price sets the move) and a listed expiry; the shared payoff target is written only when a card is used. (3) Candidates are the eligible templates (`risk === "defined"` and no `calendar` tag; calendars need the later chain, GAPS #76) materialised on the live chain at the chosen expiry and priced one pass per chain snapshot (`useWizardCandidates`: sequential, 81 points, the venue calendar; a pass is never abandoned, a newer chain waits for it and one more pass follows, at most one pass every two seconds, so a fast feed can neither keep the first result from landing nor flicker the cards). Every thesis figure derives from the expiry curve without repricing (`wizard.ts`): the P&L if right is the expiry payoff at the target price for a directional view, the least anywhere inside the ±move band for Neutral and the smaller of the two band edges for Volatile (`pnlAtExpiry` extends the curve as a straight line beyond the engine's range, which already reaches past the outer strike); the fit reads the same curve: a directional view fits when the structure profits at the target and more than at the opposite move (a condor that profits equally either side is a bet on the range, an OTM credit spread that profits most in its direction still counts; a bought call that cannot pay for itself at +0.5 % is not offered as bullish at +0.5 %), Neutral when it profits everywhere inside the band, Volatile when it profits at both band edges and less at spot; the rank is return on risk (`pnlThesis / |maxLoss|`) descending, then POP, then name; only fits with a positive thesis P&L are shown, at most three, each with one earned tag (best return on risk, highest POP, smallest max loss: an argmax over the shown cards, never repeated). Fewer than three fits says so ("2 of 3 fit this ladder"). (4) "Use this" writes the legs the card shows (the placement the pass priced, not a fresh one on a chain that may have moved) through the Templates loader's store writes (legs, name, Builder sub-tab, toast) and then sets the payoff target to the thesis price (the upper band edge for Volatile, spot for Neutral) and the days to the expiry, so the chart opens on the thesis. (5) Copy follows the design: every figure carries its unit and basis (the basis line names lots × lot size, marks and the expiry; "Max loss (the most you can lose)"; "If right on 25 Sep"); amber only on card 1's button; green and red only on P&L, max profit and max loss; with the feed disconnected the basis line reads "Feed disconnected · figures from HH:MM:SS"; arrow keys move between the view chips; the move field holds the typed target price at full precision and a price on the other side of spot switches the view. Not built from the design: the "updated N s ago" age on the basis line.
**Consequences.** A thesis becomes a shortlist in one pass of about 34 worker requests per chain snapshot; changing the view or the move is instant. The wizard never trades and never invents a strike (ADR-006: strikes come from `materialiseTemplate` on the listed ladder; a template that reaches past the ladder drops out). Not built (GAPS #88): a target date before the expiry (the thesis is valued at expiry only), calendar-family candidates, and the assistant's free-text thesis parsing. Placement (materialise → addLeg → toPricingLegs) is one shared helper, `placeTemplate`, used by the Templates tab and the wizard. Tests: `wizard.test.ts` (eligibility, the thesis prices and P&L, extrapolation, the fit rule, ranking and tags on the Black-76 ladder), `useWizardCandidates.test.tsx` (the pass lands under React's development double-mount, which the first CI run of the e2e caught: the scheduler lives in refs and never closes over a render; a newer chain reprices after the gap; no inputs → waiting), `templates.invariants.test.ts` (HC-TR-177: risk against the payoff for all 48), `builder.test.tsx` (HC-TR-176..178: states, units, the derived price, the band, no-fit, Use this → legs, name, target), `shell.test.tsx` (the palette command), `workspace.test.tsx` (`W`), `assistant.test.ts`; e2e `analyse.spec.ts` HC-TR-176..178 and the `analyse-wizard-{dark,light}` screenshots in `visual.spec.ts`.
## ADR-073 · Verified P&L from the exchange's fills: read per key, average cost per contract, the Journal's figure beside it (2026-09-12)

**Context.** Roadmap B7 (research §3 takeaway 2): a P&L figure that does not rest on HapieCoin's own bookkeeping, so it holds trades made on the exchange's own app and cannot be faked with a screenshot. Nothing in the repo read fills before this; the venue client had orders, positions and balances only.

**Decision.** (1) The trading client gains `listFills(creds, { after, pageSize })`, read-only, cursor-paged newest first, tolerant of the documented Delta fill shape (`id, size, price, side, product_id, product_symbol, order_id, role, commission, created_at`); an unreadable page throws like positions do, so "no fills" and "could not read fills" never look alike. (2) `venue_fills` keeps every fill per key (unique on account + venue fill id, the venue's row kept raw) and `fill_watermarks` records each account's last read, newest fill and last error; migration 0019. (3) `ingestFills` walks each account's pages newest first until it meets a fill already held; a first read stops after its page cap (3 for the job, 5 for the button) and keeps the cursor on the watermark, and later passes backfill from that cursor a few pages at a time until the venue's history ends, so nothing is skipped and the block says "older fills still being read" meanwhile. The product's contract value is looked up once per symbol at ingest, while the product is still served, and stored on the fill. After each pass the venue's net position per product is compared with what the held fills add up to; a product that disagrees (opened before the read, or beyond the venue's history) is named as partial on the account, never guessed. A leader-only job runs it every `FILLS_INGEST_MS` (5 min, 0 off); `POST /v1/verified/refresh` runs it for the caller now and skips an account read within the last 30 s. A failure is written on that account's watermark as a sentence and shown, never thrown. (4) `verifiedFromFills` in the schema package is the arithmetic, shared with the web mock: fills in time order, average cost per product, a reducing fill realises (price − average) × contracts × contract value signed for shorts, a flipping fill closes the old side and opens the rest at its price, commissions come off on their day, a product without a contract value is skipped and counted. (5) `GET /v1/verified/pnl` answers per account and in total (net of commissions, gross before them, 7 days, 30 days, commissions), the oldest fill held, the last read, and the Journal's own realised figure over live strategies closed since that oldest fill with the difference taken gross to gross, because HapieCoin books live strategies before fees and at expiry through the settler; the block says so when they differ. (6) The Journal shows the block above its stats once a key is connected: totals, fills and since, the agreement line, per-account rows with errors, and Refresh.

**Consequences.** Verified P&L covers every connected key, so a sub-account traded by hand counts too (that is the point). Disconnecting a key drops its fills; reconnecting re-reads what the venue still offers. Not yet verified against a live response: the exact fill shape, whether the cursor is `meta.after`, and that pages come newest first (if the venue paged oldest first the walk would stop after its first pass; GAPS #89 tracks all three, and the venue's rows are kept raw on every fill for the comparison). Money arithmetic uses the engine's existing float-then-round-to-cents convention (HapieCoin's P&L everywhere), not integer minor units; a mismatch of a cent against the venue's own statement is possible and is what the raw rows are for. Deferred to B7b: the public page per trader and the share buttons. Rejected: calling HapieCoin's own order fill prices "verified" (they cover only the orders HapieCoin placed).

## ADR-074 · Mindful Trading pause: a countdown before a live order on a day the trader is already down (2026-09-12)
**Context.** Roadmap item 10 from the competitor research: "a pause with the strategy's max loss, margin and the day's realised P&L before placing a live order when the trader is down on the day; cheap; fits the no-confusion bar." Every live entry (the Builder, the palette, the templates, Re-enter, Go live from a card) already converges on `TradeFlow` → `TradePreviewDialog` → the place call, and the batch on `BatchLiveDialog`; the preview already states the order's capital at risk and the exchange's margin in use. What did not exist: a day P&L the trader could be held to (the bar's Day P&L is browser-computed over paper and live together; the server has no per-day figure, GAPS #38 was closed by the browser writer), a per-user preference for it, and a place in the flow that waits. Design pass: `docs/design/mindful-pause.md`; ADR-073 is the other session's (Verified P&L).
**Decision.** (1) Scope: live entries only, the single flow and the batch. Live adjustments, exits and square-offs are never paused (they reduce or repair risk); paper is never paused. (2) "Down on the day" is `liveDayPnl` (`lib/strategy/mindful.ts`): the live strategies' day P&L as the bar computes it (`dayPnl` against the last P&L point before today) plus the live trades closed today at their day share, read from the same book; the day is the UTC day the P&L points are keyed by and is written on screen as "since 05:30 IST (00:00 UTC)" so the pause and the bar's tile never disagree; the figure is unknown, and no pause is decided, while an open live leg has no price. (3) The preference lives in `UserSettings.mindful` `{ enabled, thresholdUsd, pauseSeconds }` (defaults on, "0" = any loss, 30 s; 10..300 s), a jsonb column with the same default (migration `0019` on this branch, renumbered behind the other session's 0019 when the branches meet), defaulted in the zod object so an older client's PUT keeps validating (a tab loaded before the deploy that replaces the whole object without `mindful` puts the pause back to its default: the safe direction, accepted); every settings dialog's save now completes its patch from the cached settings before the strict PUT (`useUpdateSettings`), the trap GAPS #45 recorded for the header toggle; the `MindfulDialog` is reached from the settings menu (the row shows "30 s" or "off"), the palette ("Open Mindful trading") and the bar's Day P&L tile; switching it off shows one sentence, not a confirm. (4) The pause is decided once per flow, when the live preview opens (`TradeFlow` latches `mindfulFor(settings.mindful, strategies, book)`), so a tick that lifts the day above the threshold mid-pause does not cut it short; the preview and the batch dialog then show the `MindfulPause` block (today's live P&L in red, "This order could lose" from the preview's own capital-at-risk figure, "Margin in use" from the exchange check, the basis line naming the strategies counted and the day, "Nothing is blocked: the button returns when the pause ends") and the Place / batch button is replaced in place by a disabled outline countdown until it reaches zero; there is no skip. Reopening the flow restarts it. (5) Browser-side only, and the copy says so: the server has no day P&L yet, so this is a mindfulness aid, not a control (GAPS #90 keeps server enforcement for when a server-side daily writer exists).
**Consequences.** A trader who is down on live trades sees the loss, the worst case and the margin together and waits the seconds they chose before the next live order; nothing else in the flow changes, and a good day, a paper trade or an exit never meets the block. Tests: `mindful.test.ts` (the day figure over active, closed-today, older-close and paper rows; unknown while unpriced; the pause rule and thresholds), `trading-live.test.tsx` HC-TR-182 (the block, the countdown replacing the button, the button returning, off / threshold / paper never pause, the batch), `dialogs.test.tsx` HC-TR-183 (prefill, validation, the off note, persistence, mounting through the registry), `shell.test.tsx` (the palette row), `accounts.test.ts` and `settings.test.ts` (the schema bounds, the default for an older client, the round trip, an out-of-shape row); e2e `settings.spec.ts` HC-TR-183.
## ADR-075 · The public trader page: a chosen handle, off by default, totals net of fees always, three sections opt-in, shared by intent links and a client-drawn card (2026-09-12)

**Context.** Roadmap B7b (research §3 takeaway 2): a verified figure markets itself only if a trader can show it. The figure exists since ADR-073; what leaves the server for a stranger has to be decided once, and sharing must not depend on platform keys or an image service. The user chose: a handle the trader picks, totals plus a trend with the rest opt-in, and X, Telegram, a copied link and an image card as the share targets.

**Decision.** (1) `trader_pages` (user_id PK, unique lower-case `handle`, `enabled`, `show_days`, `show_accounts`, `show_months`; migration on this branch): the page is off until the trader turns it on, and it cannot be on without a handle. `Handle` in the schema package: 3–20 letters, digits or underscores, lower-cased on input, a reserved list (route words, "hapiecoin", "admin", "delta"); a taken handle answers 409, never a silent suffix. (2) `GET /v1/public/traders/{handle}` is unauthenticated, cached `public, max-age=60`, and answers 404 alike for an unknown handle, a malformed one and a page that is off, so the response never tells a visitor whether a handle exists. Its body is `publicTraderFrom` in the schema package, the only projection that leaves the server: name, handle, all-time / 30-day / 7-day realised P&L net of fees, fill count, since, last read, a `partial` flag; days (merged across accounts), accounts (label, figure, fill count, since) and months only when the trader ticked them; never account ids, commissions, balances, positions, products, order sizes or the Journal comparison. The visitor sees USD only. (3) `/t/<handle>` is a public Next page like `/s/<code>`: the body is a client component reading the same-origin API; `generateMetadata` reads the API server-side (API_URL) so a shared link previews the 30-day figure, and falls back to a generic title when the page is off or the API is down. (4) Share: an X intent link and a Telegram share link built from the 30-day figure and the URL (the referrals precedent, no SDK), copy link through the clipboard helper, and a 1200×630 PNG card drawn on a canvas in the browser and saved through a data URL, so no image library, no server rendering and no CSP change. (5) Settings: a "Public page" dialog under Account (both header menus) with the handle, the switch (disabled until the handle is valid), the three checkboxes, the link with Open, and the share bar once the page is live; renaming warns that shared links break.

**Consequences.** Anyone with the link can read the totals; the trader accepts that by switching the page on, and can switch it off or rename the handle at any time (old links then 404). The public route recomputes the figures per request behind a 60 s cache and the global per-IP limit; a keyed limiter or a stored figure comes if a page gets popular (GAPS #91). The card's colours are fixed (dark card, amber rule, green and red figures) whatever the viewer's theme, since it is an image. Rejected: a random slug (harder to say aloud, and the user chose a handle), a display-name slug (collisions, renames), OG images rendered on the server (a library the project does not carry), a public JSON that reuses `VerifiedPnl` (it names account ids and the Journal comparison).

## ADR-076 · Options screener: every listed option ranked on the chain data the browser already holds (2026-09-12)
**Context.** Roadmap item 11 from the competitor research ("a screener on the chain data we already hold: highest IV rank, richest premium per day, widest skew"). The browser already subscribes every listed expiry's chain (the Vol tab, `useChains`, well inside the gateway's 50-topic budget), the quotes carry mark, mark IV, greeks and OI, and `atmIvOf`, `skew25`, `chainTotals`, `expectedMove` and `termShape` exist; what did not exist was a premium-per-day or per-row break-even figure, a cross-expiry table, or any sortable table inside the workspace (the analytics pages own the one sortable `DataTable`). IV rank exists per underlying and front expiry only. ADR-075 is the other session's (Verified P&L public page). Design pass: `docs/design/options-screener.md`.
**Decision.** (1) A sixth left-pane tab, `Screener` (`WorkspaceTab` "screener", deep link `?tab=screener`, palette "Options screener"); no server work. (2) One pure fold (`lib/chain/screener.ts`): from every priced expiry's rows, one strike row per quoted side with days to settlement on the venue calendar (rows at or past settlement dropped; per-day figures read over at least one hour), premium per day = mark ÷ days (USD per unit per day), yield per day = premium per day ÷ spot × 100, IV−ATM = (mark IV − the expiry's ATM IV) × 100 vol points, |Δ| from the quote's greeks, break-even against spot at expiry (strike + mark for a call, strike − mark for a put), OI; and one expiry row each with days, ATM IV, 25Δ skew, expected move, call / put OI, PCR, plus the term shape over the set. Without spot the spot-based figures are null and say so. (3) The panel folds once per tick burst (`useChains` batches per animation frame), filters in component state (side, |Δ| band, expiry set, minimum OI), renders through the analytics `DataTable` (client sort, nulls last, column menu, CSV) sorted by premium per day, shows the top 100 with the count, and an Expiries view sorted by days. The basis line names the count, "marks", the front IV rank (1 y), spot, the term shape and whether the feed is live. (4) Buy / Sell add one Builder leg at the chain lots with the row's mark and IV, the chain panel's rule and toasts; Chain sets the workspace expiry and opens the chain tab. (5) Every column header states its unit and basis; premium per day says it is earned only by a seller whose option expires worthless; IV−ATM is named as vol points against the expiry's own ATM so it is not read as IV rank.
**Consequences.** A trader scans the whole board of an asset in one table and hands a pick to the Builder in one click, on data already in the tab (no new subscriptions beyond what the Vol tab makes; the only request is the IV history the Vol tab and the header share, for the basis line). A spot frame alone does not refold the board: spot enters the fold quantised to about 0.06 %. Not built (GAPS #92): a per-instrument IV rank (needs per-symbol history beyond the 168 h mark history and one request per symbol), plan gating of the screener (the research puts it behind the paywall; no entitlement key exists), saved filter presets. Tests: `screener.test.ts` (rows per quoted side, the per-day figures, IV−ATM, the break-even sign, the one-hour floor, settled expiries dropped, no-spot nulls, the expiry rows, the filters), `screener.test.tsx` (two expiries on the fake gateway: sort, filters, the Expiries view, Buy / Sell / Chain), `workspace.test.tsx` (the tab), `shell.test.tsx` (the palette row); e2e `analyse.spec.ts` HC-WS-110..112 (its first run is the PR's CI; the `analyse-screener-{dark,light}` screenshots in `visual.spec.ts` land on the next successful local visual run, GAPS #92).
## ADR-077 · Backtest on our own end-of-day chains: a daily per-strike table at the settlement hour, a template entered every day and held to expiry, recorded marks or a labelled model (2026-09-12)

**Context.** Roadmap item 9 (research §3 takeaway 5): Opstra's paid moat is a backtest on EOD data, and ADR-060 reserved the only data-backed claim about the 48 templates for "backtests on our own snapshots". What we record today is per-strike marks for seven days (the 5-minute stream, ADR-056) and ATM IV for 400 days; the ATM series carries no strike ladder, so a chain older than a week cannot be rebuilt from it, and skew before the record began is not recoverable at all. The pricing package already had every piece of arithmetic (Black-76, intrinsic settlement, calendars, the template placement) but the templates lived in the web app.

**Decision.** (1) `chain_eod` (venue, asset, day, expiry, strike, kind, mark, mark IV, spot, ts; unique per row; migration on this branch): the first snapshot pass at or after the asset's settlement hour (12:00 UTC, XAUT 16:00) writes that day's whole chain from the pass it already read, once a day and in one transaction (a failed batch leaves no half day for the once-a-day gate to mistake for a done one), kept 400 days (about 900 rows per venue and day; pruned by a ts index once a day, with the write). The first write for a venue and underlying backfills up to the previous week from the mark stream (the last pass at or before the settlement hour each day, symbols parsed back through the venue codec, the spot from the same pass's IV snapshot), so the tab has close to a week of history on day one; the oldest day may already be pruned when a later settlement hour (XAUT) runs the first write. (2) The template catalogue and `materialiseTemplate` move to the pricing package (the web module re-exports them typed on `Underlying`), so the API places a template exactly as the Builder, the Templates tab and the Wizard do. (3) `runBacktest` in the pricing package, pure: on every recorded day the template is placed on that day's ladder around the Builder's ATM row (the last strike at or below the spot, the gateway reducer's rule), on the nearest listed expiry with at least `minDte` days left (1 or more); each option leg settles at intrinsic value on the first recorded day on or after its expiry date (a backfilled day is stamped before the settlement hour, so the date decides; a calendar's legs settle separately), a perpetual rides to the exit day and a template made only of perpetuals is refused as unfit (nothing to hold to); a trade whose legs have not all settled by the last recorded day is "open" and marked on that day from the recorded mark of the same instrument, or, when the day's chain lacks it, from Black-76 at that day's ATM IV (else the entry IV, else intrinsic) and is then "modelled". Equity is realised P&L over closed trades per recorded day; stats are count, win rate, average, median, best, worst, max drawdown and profit factor; coverage names the first and last day, the entries, the days skipped (no expiry far enough out, ladder too short), the open and the modelled trades. No fees, no slippage, and the tab says so. (4) `GET /v1/backtest?asset&template&lots&minDte&from&to&venue` behind the session, every plan (GAPS #93), money to cents, `private, max-age=300`, 503 until a day is recorded, 400 for an unknown template or a reversed range; the folded days are memoised in process per venue and underlying and revalidated by a count (the table changes once a day, the tab asks once per template), `from` bounds the days read and `to` only the entries, so a trade entered inside the range still settles after it. (5) The Backtest tab in the analysis pane (also from the palette): template by category, lots, minimum days to expiry, 7D / 30D / 90D / 1Y range, the coverage line first, five tiles, the equity curve on the analytics SVG chart, the trades newest first with legs, cost, exit and P&L, "open · marked <day>" and "modelled" where they apply, and an honest empty state before the first recorded day.

**Consequences.** The evidence base grows one day per day; a claim about a template is only as long as the coverage line, which is why the line comes first and why the copy stays "ready-made structures". Replay of an expiry's chain (B9b) can use the same table for daily steps and the 5-minute marks for the last week. Rejected: computing in the browser from a shipped history (megabytes per run), extending the 5-minute mark retention (1.8 M rows a week), and modelling pre-record days from ATM IV alone (no strike ladder, no skew: it would read as history and is not).

## ADR-078 · A typed LIVE interlock on every live entry, and a TOTP second factor through better-auth's twoFactor plugin (2026-09-12)
**Context.** Roadmap item 28 / GAPS #73: an account that places real orders confirmed them with one plain click (`trade-now`, `batch-go`, the card's one-click retry, the adjust hold button), no route read a confirmation, and better-auth ran with email OTP, passkeys and optional Google only, so a stolen password or a signed-in laptop was enough to trade. Better-auth 1.7.3 ships a `twoFactor` plugin (server `better-auth/plugins/two-factor`, client `twoFactorClient`) that needs a `two_factors` table, a `users.two_factor_enabled` flag and nothing installed; its sign-in hook covers only the password, username and phone paths, so the email-OTP and social paths would have bypassed the second factor. The order bodies are strict objects.
**Decision.** (1) Typed LIVE is a server-side interlock on live ENTRIES, mirrored in the client: `LivePlaceBody`, `LiveBatchBody`, `AdjustBody`, `AddLegsBody` gain an optional `confirm` (trimmed, up to 16 chars) and retry gets a body `LiveRetryBody { confirm? }`; `requireLiveConfirm` in `routes/live-exec.ts` answers 400 "Type LIVE to confirm a real order" unless `isLiveConfirm(word)` (case-insensitive, trimmed) holds, on place (after the idempotency replay, before status and entitlement), batch (first), retry (before the status check), a live adjustment that carries entry orders (`adds`) and add-legs on a live strategy. Exits, square-offs and partial exits stay one click: they reduce risk (ADR-074's line). Paper never asks. The client sends the word as typed, never a constant, so a client that skips the field is refused too. (2) One `TypedConfirm` field ("Type LIVE to place / retry / adjust", mono, upper-cased as typed, Enter submits when the word is in) under the red block of `TradePreviewDialog` (live), `BatchLiveDialog`, `AdjustConfirmDialog` (live with adds; the hold button stays and is disabled until the word) and a new `RetryDialog` that names the refused legs; the destructive button is disabled, not hidden; with the Mindful pause the countdown still replaces the button and the field is typeable during the pause. (3) TOTP through the `twoFactor` plugin (issuer "HapieCoin", `skipVerificationOnEnable: false`, so nothing changes until the first code): drizzle `two_factors` + `users.two_factor_enabled` (migration), `authOptionsPublic.totp: true`, `/v1/me` gains `twoFactorEnabled`. With 2FA on, only password + code signs in: a `hooks.before` refuses `/sign-in/email-otp` and a sign-in `send-verification-otp` for such an account with 403 `TWO_FACTOR_REQUIRED` ("This account uses an authenticator app: sign in with your password and the code"), and a `session.create.before` database hook refuses a session for a 2FA account from any path other than `/sign-in/email` and the `/two-factor/verify-*` endpoints (so Google and passkey sessions cannot skip the code either). The verify endpoints refuse `trustDevice` (the plugin would otherwise honour it from any caller and skip the code for 30 days), and the plugin's 3-per-10 s rule is widened to 10 per minute on the two verify paths so a mistyped first code never meets a 429 (the plugin still counts failed codes and locks the account). A Google sign-in on a 2FA account returns to `/auth` with the reason in the URL (`errorCallbackURL`), which the login form shows. (4) Web: `twoFactorClient()`; `LoginForm` branches on `twoFactorRedirect` to a new `AuthTab` "totp" (6-digit `OtpInput`, a backup-code alternative, `trustDevice: false`); a `SecurityDialog` (DialogKind "security", the settings menu row reads "2FA on / off", palette "Open Security") turns it on in three acts (password → the key in groups of four with the otpauth link, no QR image → the first code) and then shows the backup codes once; turning it off asks for the password. The mock API mirrors the flow with one fixed test code (654321) and the same refusals.
**Consequences.** No client and no script places a live entry silently: the word travels with the order and the API decides. A trader who turns 2FA on signs in with the password and a code from the app, or a backup code once; email codes and Google are refused for that account with the reason spelled out (the plugin's `twoFactorRedirect` never fires on those paths, so refusing is the only honest answer). Not built (GAPS #94): a QR image (needs a library; the key and the otpauth link cover manual entry and same-device apps), a trusted-device option (refused server-side), admin visibility of 2FA state, a second factor on the API-key routes, the passkey UI (GAPS #12), and a server-side check that the word came from a human (it is an interlock, not proof). Tests: schema `strategies.test.ts` (HC-TR-186 word rule), api `live.test.ts` HC-TR-186 (place / batch / retry / live adjust add / live add-legs 400 without the word, trim and paper add 200), `auth.test.ts` HC-PB-068 / HC-SH-129 (enable → first code → `/v1/me` flag; password sign-in → redirect → code → session while the email-OTP paths answer 403; disable), web `trading-live.test.tsx` HC-TR-186 (button disabled until LIVE, batch, the retry dialog), `AuthForms.test.tsx` HC-PB-068, `dialogs.test.tsx` HC-SH-129, `shell.test.tsx` (palette row); e2e `analyse.spec.ts` (the word typed before every live click), `auth.spec.ts` HC-PB-068, `settings.spec.ts` HC-SH-129 (first runs are the PR's CI).

## ADR-079 · Replay of an expiry's chain: recorded instants only, daily over the end-of-day history and every five minutes over the last week, the Builder's legs marked from what was recorded (2026-09-12)

**Context.** Roadmap item 9, part b (research §3 takeaway 5: Opstra's simulator replays a past session in 5-minute steps). After ADR-077 the venue's chain is recorded once a day for 400 days (`chain_eod`) and every five minutes for seven days (`instrument_marks`, ADR-056), so a replay can show what the venue published at those instants and nothing else.

**Decision.** (1) Three session-guarded routes, read-only over the snapshot tables and bound by their indexes: `GET /v1/replay/expiries` (every expiry with a recorded chain, the end-of-day days held and whether the venue still lists it, from the latest pass's symbols), `GET /v1/replay/steps` (the daily instants that carry the expiry and, through one of its symbols, every 5-minute pass of the last week with the spot of the same pass) and `GET /v1/replay/chain` (the ladder at an instant: the end-of-day rows stamped then, else the marks of that pass parsed through the venue codec; 404 when nothing was recorded then). 503 while nothing is recorded for the underlying. (2) A Replay tab in the analysis pane (also from the palette): expiry (the first still listed by default; a past expiry the venue no longer lists reads "settled", a future one "delisted"), Daily / 5-min resolution with the step counts (the other resolution takes over when the chosen one has no instant for the expiry), a slider with previous / next / play (one step per 400 ms, from the start again when at the end; the position is kept per expiry and resolution so a refetch never moves it), the readout (instant, spot, "recorded end of day" or "recorded 5-minute pass", n of N) and the ladder with the ATM row (the Builder's rule) marked; while the next instant loads the previous ladder stays on screen dimmed and the readout waits, so a figure never sits under the wrong instant. The 5-minute instants of an expiry are read through every symbol the latest pass lists for it (a strike listed late must not shorten the window), the latest pass itself through the IV rows, and the spot of each pass through the front-expiry IV row. When the Builder holds legs on that expiry, their side is shown on their strike row and the readout gives the position's mark-to-market versus entry from the recorded marks alone; a leg without a recorded mark at that instant makes the readout say so instead of a number. (3) Nothing is modelled and nothing is a fill: the copy says "marks as the venue published them", never "replayed live".

**Consequences.** Replay reaches seven days at 5-minute resolution and the whole end-of-day history daily; a symbol the venue delisted before its expiry (or that was never listed at a pass) simply has no row at that instant. The mock API serves synthetic instants from the recorded instrument fixture so the tab is tested without the snapshot tables. Rejected: interpolating between instants (it would invent marks), replaying from the 5-minute stream beyond its retention (the rows are gone), and a per-strike IV surface over time (B9c if ever; the tab shows the recorded mark IV per row).

## ADR-080 · The phone pass: stacked panes, scrolling tab strips, a compact header, a chain that pans and acts on tap, tables that scroll instead of squashing, proven on a Pixel 7 profile (2026-09-12)

**Context.** Roadmap item 12. Everything shipped this week was built and tested at 1440 px, and the public trader page (ADR-075) is the first thing a stranger opens on a phone from an X or Telegram link. The workspace already stacked below 1000 px (Workspace.tsx) but three things broke a phone: the two tab strips clipped their last tabs with no way to reach them, the chain's mirrored horizontal scroll had no touch path and its row controls appeared on hover only at 18 px, and the analyse header row overflowed at 390 px.

**Decision.** (1) The tab strip (packages/ui Tabs) scrolls sideways with a hidden scrollbar and never wraps; the active underline is drawn inside the trigger (an inset shadow, no negative margin) so the scrolling strip has no vertical scroll range, and the focus ring sits inside the trigger so the strip cannot clip it; the strip's info text and the collapse buttons hide below 1000 px, where the panes are stacked anyway. (2) One shared `useMediaQuery` hook (`useNarrow`, `useCoarsePointer`) replaces the workspace's private one; an explicit viewport export (device width, no forced zoom, notch area painted). (3) The analyse header compacts below 768 px so its 412 px row never overlaps: tighter gaps; the exchange chip, the currency toggle, the theme toggle and the venue switch step back into their menus (theme sits in the settings menu; the venue switch stays a desktop control for now, GAPS #96); the logo keeps its mark only, the futures price keeps its figure but not the 24 h change, the feed status keeps its dot; the asset pills, alerts and the two menus stay. (4) The chain pans by touch: a drag on either side track moves the shared offset through the same path as the wheel once the gesture is clearly horizontal (10 px more sideways than up or down), and otherwise leaves vertical scrolling to the browser (the tracks carry touch-action: pan-y, so the browser keeps vertical scrolling and never claims a horizontal drag first; a move that is no longer cancelable is left alone). On a coarse pointer a tapped row becomes the focused row, which carries the controls, and the controls grow to 36 px targets; the trader still taps B or S to add a leg, a tap on the row alone never places anything. (5) Tables that cannot fit a phone scroll inside their own container instead of squashing: net positions, the backtest trades, the replay ladder; the paper and journal search boxes go full width; the journal stats go 2-up then 3-up; the workbench ticket's pills relax below 640 px. (6) A `phone` Playwright project (Pixel 7 profile: 412 × 839, touch, mobile viewport, the same Chrome) runs `phone.spec.ts` only and the desktop project skips it; it proves the stacked layout, the compact header, both strips reaching their last tab, the one-side chain with tap-to-act controls of finger size, a dialog inside the viewport, and no sideways page scroll on the paper tab, the journal and the public page; two captures `analyse-phone-dark/light`.

**Consequences.** The desktop is untouched above 1000 px except the shared hook and the strip's overflow rule. The adjustment workbench already stacks below 720 px; the terminal and admin tables, the column settings dialog and the chain picker stay desktop-first (GAPS #96). Rejected: a separate mobile route or component tree (two products to keep in step), a bottom tab bar (the stacked pane switch already exists and the browser chrome takes the bottom), and hiding the chain's row controls behind a long press (undiscoverable; a tap to select, then a tap to act, is what a phone trader expects).

## ADR-081 · Error tracking through the Sentry envelope protocol without an SDK, API metrics in Prometheus text, browser errors relayed through the API (2026-09-12)
**Context.** Roadmap item 27 / GAPS #72: the API logged JSON through pino and answered `/healthz`, only the gateway served Prometheus metrics, nothing caught a failure in the browser, and docs/deploy.md §6 left the error tracker as an open decision. Adding a vendor SDK meant three new dependencies with auto-instrumentation weight, before the vendor was even chosen; an `errors` table nobody watches is not tracking. Every tracker worth choosing (Sentry, self-hosted GlitchTip, Bugsink) accepts the same wire format: one DSN, `POST {base}/api/{project}/envelope/`, an `X-Sentry-Auth: Sentry sentry_version=7, sentry_client=…, sentry_key=…` header, an envelope of a header line, an item header and the event JSON, frames oldest-first (develop.sentry.dev, envelopes and the event schema, read on 12 Sep 2026).
**Decision.** (1) One in-house error sink per process (`apps/api/src/error-sink.ts`, the same file in `apps/gateway/src/error-sink.ts`): `createErrorSink({ dsn: ERROR_SINK_DSN, environment, release: RELEASE })` builds and posts envelopes with `fetch`, no SDK; every tag, extra and message is redacted by the logger's secret-key rule, a request carries only method, scrubbed path and user agent (never a body, cookie or authorization header), a user only its id; sends are fire-and-forget with a 3 s timeout, capped at 60 events a minute (the rest counted as dropped), flushed on shutdown; without a DSN every call is a no-op, so tests, a laptop and CI need nothing. (2) The capture seam is the log: the API's pino gets a `hooks.logMethod` that hands every error- and fatal-level record (child bindings included, so the request id travels) to the sink, and the gateway's logger does the same for its error level, so 500s (`onError` now logs the Error itself with method, scrubbed path, route pattern, request id and user id), job failures, feed faults and the process-level `unhandledRejection` / `uncaughtException` handlers all reach the tracker through the one line they already log, and nothing is captured twice; both process handlers flush the sink and then end the process, so Node's semantics stay (a half-done job tick never runs on). Two things the seam's callers could leak are cut at the sink: a drizzle query error quotes its bound values after `params:` (an OTP, a session token), so the message is cut there and pino redacts `err.params` / `err.query`; personal-data keys in log fields (`to`, `email`, `name`, `phone`, `mobile`, `user`) are dropped before `extra`. (3) `GET /metrics` on the API renders Prometheus text by hand like the gateway's: `hapiecoin_api_requests_total{method,route,status}`, a `hapiecoin_api_request_duration_seconds` histogram per route, `hapiecoin_api_errors_total{kind}`, the sink's sent / dropped / failed counts, jobs active, uptime and process memory; the route label is Hono's matched pattern (`/v1/strategies/:id`; `(other)` when nothing matched), never the raw path, so a URL scan cannot grow the series; `/metrics` and `/healthz` are not counted; `METRICS_TOKEN` guards the route with a bearer like the gateway (GAPS #30, compared in constant time); the gateway's `/metrics` gains the same sink-count block. (4) Browser errors go through the API: `POST /v1/client-errors` (no session, 5 reports a minute per address, a strict body of message, name, stack ≤ 4 000, path, kind) relays a `javascript` event tagged `browser:<kind>` (the body's `release` becomes the event's release, never a tag: Sentry reserves that key), so no DSN ships to the browser and the server scrubs; the web installs `window` error and unhandled-rejection listeners once (one report per message per minute, a failed report is swallowed), and `app/error.tsx` / `global-error.tsx` show "Something broke on our side. It has been reported." with the tracker's reference, Try again and Reload, and say plainly that a page error cannot change strategies or orders on the exchange.
**Consequences.** The vendor choice in docs/deploy.md is now an env value, not a code change: any Sentry-protocol server works from `ERROR_SINK_DSN`, and events carry environment, release, server name, route, request id and user id. Ops reads one `/metrics` shape from both apps. The API's unhandled-error log line now carries the stack (pino's `err` serializer) where it carried name and message before. Not built (GAPS #97): better-auth's own failures (answered inside `auth.handler` by the library's console logger, not through pino), tracing spans and timings, source maps for the browser frames (minified names), sampling, a shared server package for the duplicated sink file, alert rules and uptime pings (deploy-time), the web build's own release tag (browser events carry the API's), and a second factor or session on the client-error route (rate limit only). Tests: api `error-sink.test.ts` (DSN parsing, stack frames, redaction agreeing with the logger, envelope shape, cap, timeout and failures, the log-record mapping), `metrics.test.ts`, `observability.test.ts` (the pino hook, `/metrics` with and without the token, a 500 reaching the sink), `routes/client-errors.test.ts`; gateway `error-sink.test.ts`, `log.test.ts`, `config.test.ts`, `app.test.ts` (an error log reaching the injected sink, flush on stop, the off sink); web `lib/errors/report.test.ts`, `app/error.test.tsx`. No e2e: a deliberate crash is not a scriptable path; the rows read unit (api + gateway + web).

## ADR-082 · Home-screen app: a manifest, icons, a deny-by-default service worker with an offline page, an install entry in the settings menu, proven on Pixel 7 and iPhone 14 profiles (2026-09-13)

**Context.** Roadmap item 23 (E4 mobile, ADR-060), the PWA half; the Capacitor shell waits for store accounts. After the phone pass (ADR-080) the workspace works in a phone browser but has no manifest, no icons, no service worker and no offline state: the browser tab is the app, a lost connection shows the browser's own error page, and nothing invites the trader to install. A PWA library (next-pwa, Serwist) was the obvious route; both precache every route and chunk and answer navigations from that cache, which is the opposite of what a trading screen needs.

**Decision.** (1) `app/manifest.ts` serves the manifest (name HapieCoin, standalone, start and id `/analyse`, Obsidian dark ground as theme and background); the 192 and 512 px PNG icons are rendered once from the SVG mark and committed under `public/icons`; the layout links the manifest and sets Safari's standalone flags. (2) A hand-written `public/sw.js` is deny-by-default: it answers only same-origin GET navigations (network first, the precached `/offline` page when fetch fails; that page carries no client script, its retry is a plain link, because its own chunks may be missing from the cache; it is rendered per request like every route, so its CSP nonce and inline script match inside the one cached response) and `/_next/static/` assets (cache first, stored only when the server marks the response immutable: production chunks are content-hashed and immutable, dev chunks are not, so an edit is never served stale); every path under `/v1`, `/api` (which holds the auth API), `/__test`, Next's data and image routes, every other origin and every non-GET pass through untouched, so market data, positions, orders and sessions are never served from a cache; no page response is stored except `/offline` (pages carry the request nonce and the trader's data). The offline page is fetched fresh at install together with the same-origin stylesheets it links, so the cached pair stays consistent across deploys; the first navigation after each worker start refreshes it and trims the immutable-asset cache to the newest 200 entries; the icons are not precached (the manifest fetches them). VERSION is bumped by hand and activate drops every other cache. A unit test loads the file into a sandbox and pins the routing table. (3) The CSP gains `worker-src 'self'` and `manifest-src 'self'` (strict-dynamic does not cover workers). (4) Install: one external store keeps Chrome's deferred `beforeinstallprompt`; the settings menu shows "Install app" only while there is something to do (Ready on Chrome, iPhone on iOS; hidden once installed) and the Install dialog either shows the native prompt or the two share-menu steps (Safari and Chrome on iOS both have one); on a phone the shell shows one hint toast, once per browser, whose Install action opens a dialog the shell hosts itself, so it works on the sign-in and public pages where the settings dialogs are not mounted. No persistent banner. The manifest carries no orientation lock (the chain gains from landscape); the splash uses the body ground and the chrome the header tone; the iOS status bar is opaque black until a device check proves the safe-area handling. (5) A `phone-ios` Playwright project runs the phone spec on the iPhone 14 profile inside the same Chrome (WebKit is not installed here); the phone spec proves the manifest and icons, the menu entry, the worker's registration and precache list, that no API path is cached, the offline page on a cut connection and the live chain after Retry.

**Consequences.** Nothing about prices, orders or sessions changes; the worker cannot answer those requests at all. Desktop Chrome also fires the install event, so the menu item appears there too, which is correct. Real Safari behaviour (Add to Home Screen, standalone display, the status bar) is untested until a WebKit run or a device check (GAPS #98); no push, no background sync, no update toast when a new worker takes over. Rejected: a PWA library (precaches routes, opposite policy, new dependency); caching `/analyse` for a faster cold start (a stale page with a nonce and yesterday's chain is worse than a spinner); a persistent install banner (the settings menu plus one hint is enough, and the banner would sit where the portfolio bar lives).

## ADR-083 · A resting live limit order can be cancelled or re-priced from the card: cancel without the word, re-price as a true exchange edit with it (2026-09-13)
**Context.** GAPS #61: since ADR-044 A3 an adjustment entry can rest on the exchange as a limit; the card showed it as "pending", Sync recorded an exchange-side cancel and Retry re-sent the same limit at its stored price, but the trader could not pull the order or move its price from HapieCoin and had to go to the exchange. The venue client already had `cancelOrder` (DELETE /v2/orders); Delta also edits an open order in place (`PUT /v2/orders` with `id`, `product_id`, `limit_price`; docs.delta.exchange, read 13 Sep 2026). Two hazards decided the shape: the reconciler runs every 15 s over every pending order and could book an exchange-side cancel or a fill in the same second, and an order can fill between the click and the request.
**Decision.** (1) Cancel is risk-reducing, so it takes no typed word and no kill-switch refusal (ADR-078's line: the word is for entries; unlike the positions-exit route, which sends reduce-only orders and does check the switch, a cancel sends nothing, so the operator pausing trading must not trap a resting order); it keeps the order rate limit and ownership. `POST /v1/strategies/{id}/live/orders/{orderId}/cancel` accepts only a resting limit entry (pending, limit, not an exit, known to the venue; anything else is a 409 that says to sync first), sends the cancel, then reads the order back from the exchange and books the venue's truth: a fill that beat the click becomes a filled order with the leg's entry price, a cancel becomes `cancelled` with the trader's own reason "cancelled from HapieCoin" (distinct from the reconciler's "cancelled on the exchange"); an accepted cancel whose read-back fails is booked as cancelled; anything else is a 409 that names what happened. The leg stays open with no entry price, exactly as after an exchange-side cancel, so Retry keeps working. (2) Re-price is a true edit at the exchange: the same venue order and client order id, the new price snapped to the product's tick toward the passive side like a fresh limit, no cancel-then-replace and so no unhedged window and no second client order id. It keeps a real order live at a new price, so it needs the typed word, the kill switch applies, and the route is `POST …/orders/{orderId}/reprice` with `LiveRepriceBody { limitPrice, confirm? }`. A fill on the new price comes back booked; a refused edit is re-read from the venue first, so a fill or a cancel that beat the edit is booked before the refusal is reported ("filled before the new price reached the exchange"). The venue port gains `editOrder(creds, { orderId, productId, limitPrice }) → PlaceOrderResult` (a transport failure is the unknown outcome, as for a placement) and `VenueOrder.limitPrice`, the price the venue holds, which every read-back and Sync write onto the row when it differs, so an edit whose answer was lost never leaves the app showing a price the exchange does not hold; every booking is guarded on the row still being that pending venue order, so a reconciler pass or a retry in between is never overwritten; the test double moves the price and fills when the new limit crosses its fill price, and now refuses to cancel or edit an order that is no longer open, as the exchange does. (3) The card: the chip of a resting limit reads "resting @ price" (its `data-state` stays `pending`, so the e2e assertions on order states hold) and a row under the chips carries Re-price and Cancel order per resting order; one `RestingOrderDialog` serves both, naming the leg, the resting price, the mark and the contracts: cancel is one plain click with the sentence that nothing is placed and the leg stays on the card; re-price has the price field, the red block and the word. A cancelled entry now shows in the retry banner ("Order cancelled · Retry Cancelled Orders") and in the Retry dialog, since the executor already re-sent cancelled entries.
**Consequences.** A resting order is managed where the trader watches it, with the exchange as the source of truth on every path; the reconciler and a click cannot disagree because both book what the venue reports and a row already booked is refused rather than overwritten. Not built (GAPS #99): retry at a new price for an already-cancelled order (Retry re-sends the stored price; re-price before cancelling instead), dropping the leg after a cancel (it lingers open with no position until Retry, settlement or a square-off), cancel or re-price for a resting *exit* (exits are market and reduce-only today), whether Delta preserves `client_order_id` across an edit is unverified against the live venue (the row keeps its id either way; a mismatch would surface as an "unknown" outcome on the next sync), and a batch cancel of every resting order of a strategy. Tests: venues `trading.test.ts` HC-TR-188 (PUT body, refusal, unknown and unreadable outcomes, the fake's fill / rest / refuse rules), `trading-more.test.ts` (a filled order cannot be cancelled); api `live.test.ts` HC-TR-187 (cancel, refusals, Retry after, the fill that beat the click) and HC-TR-188 (word, tick snap, fill at the new price, the refused edit re-read); web `trading-live.test.tsx` HC-TR-187 / HC-TR-188 (chip, dialog, both outcomes, the cancelled entry's retry). No e2e: the resting path is unit-covered end to end through the mock, whose reprice fills when the new limit crosses the mark.
## ADR-084 · The Mindful pause decided on the server: a live day P&L from the recorded marks, carried in the preview, enforced as a delay on place, batch and a live add (2026-09-13)
**Context.** ADR-074 built the Mindful pause in the browser from the book's day P&L, and GAPS #90 recorded what that left open: a trader who orders through the API directly, or whose tab never priced the live legs, met no pause; an adjustment that adds a leg is a new bet and was not covered; the server had no per-day P&L (`strategy_pnl` was written by the browser only, GAPS #38). The server already valued a strategy from its recorded marks for the alerts evaluator (realised + Σ (latest mark − entry) × lots × lot size over open legs, marks no older than 20 minutes, null when a leg has none), and every server-side day key is the UTC day, the same day the browser's `todayIso` uses.
**Decision.** (1) `apps/api/src/day-pnl.ts` computes the trader's live day P&L from what the server holds: active live strategies at the marks' total minus the last `strategy_pnl` point before today, live strategies closed today at their realised figure minus the same baseline (the browser's `dayPnl` rule), unknown while any open live leg has no fresh mark; no pause is ever decided on a guess. A leader job (`DAY_PNL_MS`, default five minutes, 0 off) writes today's `strategy_pnl` point for every live strategy whose legs have fresh marks, so the baseline exists without a browser; no new table. (2) `LivePreview` gains `mindful: { day: { pnlUsd, count, closedCount, known }, pause: { seconds, thresholdUsd, basis } | null }`, computed once per request from the trader's Mindful settings and the day figure (the batch route computes it once and hands the same one to every strategy); it rides outside `reasons`, so `ok` keeps meaning "refuse" and the pause means "delay". (3) Enforcement: place, batch, a live adjustment with adds and `POST /strategies/{id}/legs` on a live strategy answer 409 `MINDFUL_PAUSE` with the seconds left while the pause applies and has not elapsed; the preview routes and `GET /v1/me/day-pnl` stamp when the trader saw it, and a refusal stamps too, so a client that skipped the preview waits the same pause once (`MindfulGate`, in memory per replica; a stamp not seen for ten minutes is an abandoned flow and restarts, a re-preview keeps a running clock, an entry that goes through consumes its stamp so the next entry on that strategy waits its own pause). Going live from paper resets the strategy's realised P&L and deletes the paper run's `strategy_pnl` points, as `/start` does, so a paper day never reads as a live figure. Trims, closes, exits and square-offs never wait (ADR-074's line); an adjustment that mixes a trim with an add waits as a whole. (4) The client prefers the server's verdict whenever its figure is known: the trade preview and the batch dialog show the server's figure (the block says "the server's figure") and fall back to this tab's fold only while the server has none; the workbench shows the same block and countdown for an add, replacing the hold button until the pause ends. The mock mirrors the preview field and the endpoint from a per-account day figure knob.
**Consequences.** The pause is a control now, not only an aid: a script that skips the preview is refused once and waits the same pause, and a second tab or a stale book cannot avoid it while the server knows the day. The three entry dialogs handle the one refusal a preview cannot foresee (the day moved between preview and place) by showing the block and the countdown from the refusal's own figures; the batch dialog waits for the server's figure before its button is live. Where the server has no fresh mark (a symbol the market-data job does not read, a quiet venue) the browser's fold still decides, so the server pause triggers no more often than the browser's, never less honestly. Not built (GAPS #100): the stamp lives in memory per replica (another replica asks for the preview again; a Redis stamp would follow the rate store's pattern); the day figure re-reads marks per live strategy on every preview (fine at a trader's scale, a per-user cached row if it shows up hot); `POST /strategies/{id}/pnl` still accepts any day from the client, so the trader's own browser (a skewed clock) or script can move the baseline the server subtracts, a self-inflicted skip on a par with turning the setting off; a partially filled leg is valued at its entry price like every open leg (Re-enter needs nothing extra: it places through the same route). Tests: api `day-pnl.test.ts` (the figure against marks, baseline, closed-today, staleness; settings; the writer; the gate; place / batch / a live add refused until the pause elapsed, a trim untouched), web `trading-live.test.tsx` HC-TR-189 / HC-TR-190 (the server's figure in the trade preview, the server's "not down" overriding the tab's fold, the workbench add's countdown), `mindful.test.ts` (serverMindful).
## ADR-085 · Open interest the venue did not send is unknown, never "0" (2026-09-13)
**Context.** GAPS #15: `Quote.oi` was required, and the venue adapter wrote `"0"` whenever the venue sent no open interest, so a strike with no figure looked like a strike nobody holds. Delta's REST tickers and verbose `v2/ticker` frames carry `oi_contracts`, but the compact `ticker` channel omits its `oi` cell on some ticks and carries neither `oi` in underlying units nor `contract_value`, so nothing can be derived there; the Delta session replaced its stored quote wholesale, so one such tick wiped the REST-seeded figure for the next subscriber's snapshot. The schema's rule for every other venue-optional field is "nullable → omitted, never null", and every web formatter already renders an absent figure as "—".
**Decision.** (1) `Quote.oi` is optional: omitted means the venue sent no open interest; a real zero stays `"0"`. (2) The venue adapter omits `oi` when the venue quote's `oiContracts` is null. (3) Both venue sessions carry the last open interest pair the venue sent forward when a newer frame has none (one helper, both figures move together so units never mix), so a snapshot shows the last known figure and the schema shows none only when none was ever sent. (4) Web: the OI bar helpers skip an absent side; the screener row's OI is null when unknown, shown as "—", sorted last, kept by a zero minimum and dropped by a positive one (an unknown figure cannot prove a minimum); the chain cell, the picker, the option details and the workbench chain already showed "—". (5) The gateway is unchanged: a delta without `oi` reads "unchanged" on the wire, which is right because the venue session never emits a quote whose figure went from known to unknown.
**Consequences.** A dash means "the venue sent nothing", a 0 means nobody holds the strike; Σ OI and the put/call ratio sum only the figures that exist. Left open (GAPS #101): the wire cannot express an open interest that went from known to unknown (never produced now; only a restart re-seeds from REST, since the periodic reload never replaces a quote newer than its own); that a compact tick omitting its oi cell means "not sent" rather than zero is Delta's documented shape and is checked on the first live compact-channel run; the per-instrument OI analytics in the terminal come from a separate ingest and were never affected. Tests: schema `market.test.ts` (omitted accepted, null refused), venues `schema-adapter.test.ts` (none sent → no field, zero stays) and `delta/market-data.test.ts` (carry-forward), gateway `diff.test.ts`, web `range.test.ts`, `screener.test.ts`, `chain.test.tsx` HC-WS-116.
## ADR-086 · A fresh authenticator code on the sensitive routes; the authenticator state in the admin table (2026-09-13)
**Context.** ADR-078 put a second factor on sign-in. GAPS #94 recorded what a signed-in session could still do unchallenged: connect or replace an exchange API key, remove one, and rewrite the settings that decide what a live order can do (the Mindful pause, the lot sizes). The session resolver carried id, email, name and role only; there was no step-up helper; Better Auth's `verifyTOTP` works on a signed-in session as a re-check (it returns the user, or 401 on a wrong code) but skips the plugin's lockout on that path, and the plugin's public barrel does not export the verifier. Admins could not see whether an account had the authenticator on.
**Decision.** (1) An account with the authenticator on confirms a sensitive change with the current code (the plugin accepts it across its ~90 s window; one observed code therefore covers the changes made in that span), sent as the `X-Second-Factor` header (allowed through CORS for the app shells): `POST /v1/credentials` (connect or replace a key), `DELETE /v1/credentials/{id}`, and `PUT /v1/settings` when the body changes `mindful` or `lotSizes` (`safetyKnobsChanged`, key-order-free); theme, density, currency and P&L basis never ask, and an account without the authenticator is never asked. (2) `requireSecondFactor` (apps/api/src/security/second-factor.ts): 403 `SECOND_FACTOR_REQUIRED` without the header, the code checked through `auth.api.verifyTOTP` with the request's own session, 403 `SECOND_FACTOR_INVALID` on a wrong one, and its own budget because the signed-in verify path has none: every attempt takes a slot and a right code hands them back, so five wrong codes in fifteen minutes lock the check for that user (429 `SECOND_FACTOR_LOCKED`, `Retry-After` = the time left). Nothing is written while refused (the check runs before any row is touched). (3) `SessionUser.twoFactorEnabled` comes from the session resolver (the plugin adds the field to the user; no cookie cache, so it is fresh per request), so the guard costs no extra read; a session user built without the flag (the jobs' synthetic users, tests) is looked up in `users`, never assumed off, so omission cannot switch the step-up off; `AppDeps.auth` is typed as the handler and the server API. (4) Web: one `SecondFactorField` (the six boxes of the sign-in forms) in the API Settings, Mindful and Lot size dialogs when `/v1/me` says the authenticator is on; the mutations carry the code in the header (`useUpdateSettings`, `useConnectExchange`, `useDisconnectExchange` take an optional `secondFactor`); a refusal is shown under the field, or as a toast with a fresh `/v1/me` when the tab did not yet know 2FA was on; the knob dialogs ask only when a value changed; the code is cleared on open and after each save, so the next change asks again. (5) `AdminUserRow.twoFactorEnabled` and a "2FA" column (on / off, in the CSV) in the admin users table. (6) The mock mirrors the header check with its fixed test code.
**Consequences.** A stolen or forgotten session on a 2FA account can no longer swap the exchange key, remove it, or switch the pause off; the same session can still read the masked key list and trade (trading has its own interlocks, ADR-078 and ADR-084). The step-up is a header, so the OpenAPI bodies stay what the schemas say and a script sends the code the same way a browser does. Left open (GAPS #102): backup codes are not accepted as a step-up (the authenticator app is the only source); the currency conversion rate is not gated (a display figure); the admin's lot-size override (`PATCH /v1/admin/users/{id}`) writes the same row under the admin guard only; a settings save from a second tab can carry a superseded knob out of its cache (GAPS #45's replace semantics) and meet a refusal it has no field for; the check bypasses the plugin's own lockout by design and relies on this budget; the code is asked on every save while 2FA is on (no grace window); the admin column shows the state only, admins cannot turn it off for a locked-out user; no e2e (the dialogs are exercised through the mock). Tests: api `security/second-factor.test.ts` (connect / delete / settings refused, allowed, locked, reset; the plain account; the admin row), web `dialogs.test.tsx` HC-SH-136 (the three dialogs), `users.test.tsx` HC-AD-129.
## ADR-087 · Trade All → Live previewed as one batch (2026-09-13)
**Context.** ADR-010 made Trade All → Live an explicit batch selector (HC-TR-089) and GAPS #4 kept the rest open: per-strategy margin checks and one combined preview. The batch route previewed each strategy inside its placement loop, so a strategy that failed its own check stopped the batch after the earlier ones were already live, and the two wallet rules in the executor's preview (the worst loss, the net premium against the available balance) looked at one strategy at a time: several strategies could each pass while the premiums they pay together exceeded the wallet. The venue has no pre-trade margin estimate (ADR-029), so "margin" here is the wallet rule plus the margin the exchange already holds, shown for information. The dialog's rows showed name, asset, open legs and paper P&L only.
**Decision.** (1) `POST /v1/strategies/live/batch/preview` runs every ticked strategy's own preview (the same `preview()` a single placement uses, the exchange wallet read once per account across the batch) and adds the batch rule: the premiums the batch pays together against the available balance of the account it trades through, per account when strategies name different keys (a strategy naming none is put on the exchange's only key, as its placement would be); premiums received are not netted against premiums paid, because the strategies go out one at a time in the order given. It answers per-strategy items (paper or skipped, ok, reasons, legs with marks, notional, net premium) and the whole (ok, the batch's own reasons, Σ notional, Σ premium, available, margin in use, limits). (2) The batch route runs that combined preview first and refuses the whole batch with 409 naming every refused strategy and the batch reasons before any order goes out (a contract change: a check refusal used to come back as 200 with the earlier strategies placed and `failed` naming the one that stopped it); the placement loop reuses the previewed plans and flips each row to live only while it is still paper, so a second batch from another tab cannot place it twice; a failure at the venue still stops the batch at that strategy with the earlier ones placed and reported, as before. (3) The dialog asks for the batch preview whenever the ticks, the exchange or the account change: each ticked row shows its check (notional and the premium it pays or receives, or the first reason in red), a batch line shows Σ notional, Σ premium, available and margin in use (in the display currency; no single wallet figure when the strategies trade through several) with the batch reasons, the rows keep their last check while a tick change re-asks, a failed check offers Check again, the Mindful block's at-risk line becomes the batch's premium, and the button stays disabled until the fresh answer says the whole batch is ok, so unticking a refused strategy is the way through. (4) The mock mirrors the endpoint and the refusal with its 4 000 USD wallet.
**Consequences.** A batch is all-or-nothing at the check: no strategy goes live while another would be refused or while the wallet cannot pay for all of them. Not built (GAPS #103): a per-strategy worst-loss figure in the batch (the dialog has no analysis result for saved strategies, so the premium rule is the batch's only wallet rule, as on a single placement without a client figure); the per-placement notional cap is not summed across the batch; a multi-account batch states the wallet rule without naming the account; the preview runs twice on a placement (once for the answer, once in the route); the mock has one wallet and no account grouping; no e2e. Tests: api `live-batch.test.ts` (each passes alone, the batch fails together, nothing placed, then both live; a refused strategy names itself; a repeat of the key; two keys apart and together, credits not netted), `live-cover.test.ts`, `accounts.test.ts` and `venue-column.test.ts` moved to the 409 contract, schema `strategies.test.ts`, web `trading-live.test.tsx` HC-TR-191.
## ADR-088 · Drafts are server rows; the Phase 2 browser hopper is retired (2026-09-13)
**Context.** GAPS #35 recorded that drafts lived in the browser (ADR-023) and that a draft saved on one device was not on another. Phase 3 (ADR-024) closed that in code: Save in the Builder creates or patches a server strategy with status draft, "My templates" lists the server drafts, Load reads the server legs back into the Builder, Activate starts the server draft, and archive / restore / delete round-trip. What remained was the one-shot import hopper for Phase 2 browser drafts (`SavedStrategy`, `drafts[]`, `draftsImported`, the local save / load / archive / delete actions and `useImportLegacyDrafts`), which no component called any more, a dialog line that still said drafts stay on the device, and a traceability row (HC-TR-043) that still described a "Refresh strategies" button that never existed.
**Decision.** (1) The hopper is retired: HapieCoin has never been deployed, so no browser holds Phase 2 drafts worth importing; the store drops the legacy state, the actions and the persisted flag, `merge` ignores the old keys, TradeFlow loses the import hook, and the store test loses the local-draft cases. (2) The save dialog says where a draft lives ("Saved to your account: open it from Templates → My templates on any device"). (3) HC-TR-043 is restated as the self-refreshing list: a TanStack query refetched after every mutation on this device and when the My templates list mounts after its 15 s stale time (the app turns focus refetch off, so a draft saved on another device appears on the next mutation or mount here); the Paper and Live panels keep their own Refresh for the exchange-side read. (4) GAPS #35 is closed with this evidence.
**Consequences.** One less code path and one less persisted key; an old browser's `drafts` / `draftsImported` keys are dropped silently at the next load. Left open (GAPS #104): the unsaved Builder working set and the per-device `draftId` pointer stay on the device by design (a draft you never saved does not travel, and another device opens an empty Builder with the draft listed under My templates rather than "continuing where you left off"); no e2e on a second device. Tests: web `store.test.ts` (meta and merge without the legacy keys), the builder / trading suites unchanged in what they prove.
## ADR-089 · Passkeys: the sign-in button, the Security section, the mock and the tests (2026-09-13)
**Context.** The server has had the passkey plugin since Phase 1 (`rpID` from the web origin, `rpName` HapieCoin, the `passkeys` table, the seven routes under the auth wildcard) and ADR-078's session hook refuses a passkey sign-in for an account with the authenticator on (TWO_FACTOR_REQUIRED).
