Rejected for now: closing the filled legs automatically, which sends more orders to an exchange that has just refused one.
**What does not change.** The manual place and batch routes still refuse to take an Autopilot trade live. A live Autopilot trade is squared off by hand like any live trade. A live trade is left out of the strategy's paper record and of the trader's paper results. The 85 built-in playbooks never trade live.
**Consequences.** Migrations 0038 (the index over live rows; `strategies.autopilot_mandate_id`) and 0039 (`strategies.autopilot_entry_complete_at`). HC-TR-299 to HC-TR-306. No screen. GAPS #183 lists what is open.

## ADR-149 · The trader's side of live Autopilot: one fact about a strategy's approval, live availability said openly, and live never looking like paper (2026-11-27)
**Context.** The mandate (ADR-147) and the engine's live entry (ADR-148) had no screen: a mandate could be signed only through the API, and nothing on the Autopilot screen told live from paper. ADR-145 decided that a trader sees nothing of an approval (who approved, when, on which paper record).
**Decision.** On `/autopilot`, in the row of a saved strategy: where a mandate could be signed, "Trade live…" opens a dialog (the exchange account, lots per trade, trades open at once, the most capital, the loss limit for the day and the week, the four statements in the server's own sentences, the typed LIVE word, the authenticator code); where the trader holds a mandate, a line shows its state in words, its terms, and Pause (one click), Resume (with the code) and End (one more question); ended mandates stay under "Past mandates" as the record of what was signed. A strip above the summary counts the mandates by state, names the ones that need the trader, and lists any mandate whose strategy has left the trader's list (a retired version, a changed audience), so a mandate can always be seen and stopped. A mandate reads LIVE only when a live entry can actually be taken: the server enters live trades and the trader has that strategy switched on; otherwise it reads "signed" with the reason, and no sentence claims paper trading for a strategy that is switched off. A mandates read that fails is said, and offers nothing: it is never read as "no mandate". The dialog offers only the accounts on the strategy's exchange. Every log line carries a PAPER or LIVE chip.
**What a trader is told about approval.** One boolean on a strategy's card, `liveOpen`: a mandate could be signed for it (it may trade live and is this trader's to use). Nothing of who approved it, when, or its record: ADR-145 stands.
**Live availability is said, not hidden.** The view carries `liveAvailable` (the operator's switch, ADR-148). Off: nothing offers live, and a mandate already signed reads "Mandate signed · live is off" with the sentence that the strategy trades on paper. Both fields are sent only when true, so every response without live is byte for byte what it was.
**Live never looks like paper.** LIVE is never green (green and red stay on profit and loss) and never amber (the primary action's): a plain bold chip; the TESTNET tag of ADR-092 sits beside every live state; the dialog's button says "Sign mandate" and the line above it says that signing places no order; the dialog shows no result, margin or profit figure; a paused mandate shows its reason whole, and one paused for an entry that did not complete says what to do (check the exchange, square the trade off on the Live tab). An account without the authenticator, or without a connected exchange account, gets that one instruction and no form.
**Consequences.** HC-TR-307 (dialog), HC-TR-308 (mandate line), HC-TR-309 (live told apart from paper), HC-TR-310 (the two fields). No migration. GAPS #184 lists what the screen does not do.

## ADR-150 · A paper strategy that sells the same expiry again after its take: "ETH monthly 12 %, 1 month out, take 35 % and sell again" (2026-11-27)
**Context.** The research thread studied the ETH monthly strangle 12 % away with its stop at double under a profit take that sells again (STRATEGY-LAB.md section 9a item 18 and `study_monthly_take.py`, on its branch `research/exits` at 40d77f3): at a take of 35 % of the premium, with a new strangle sold on the same expiry each time, +36.6 an expiry on 10 expiries, 6 positive, worst -31, 2.6 strangles an expiry, against +23.3, 3 of 10 and -60 for the stop alone held to settlement. The owner approved building it on paper on 27 Nov 2026. Until now the engine never opened a trade because a rule had fired, and never looked at why the last trade of a strategy closed: a monthly strategy was sold once per expiry and that was all.
**Decision.** One more built-in strategy, `eth-monthly-strangle-12pct-near-take-35-sell-again`, beside the five ETH monthly ones, none of them changed: 100 lots, a call and a put 12 % from the spot on the month-end expiry, first sold on the first evening that expiry is 30 to 44 days out (the range of the others; it is the first evening in practice, and a few days later if Autopilot was off), each sold option bought back at twice its price, the whole trade closed at 35 % of its premium. Two settings of a clock playbook carry what is new, and no other playbook has either:
- `sellAgain: { minDte, withinHours, maxPerExpiry }`: when the newest trade of that trader, playbook, coin and expiry is closed and it closed by its profit take, within the last `withinHours` (24), and that expiry is at least `minDte` days away (7), a new structure is sold on that SAME expiry at the spot of the pass, with the same exits, on the next engine pass and at any hour. Only a take does it: a trade that ended with both options stopped, by hand or at settlement is followed by nothing (a take that fires after ONE option was stopped is a take of the strangle as a whole, as the study counts it, and is followed by a sale). One sale for one take; an expiry is sold at most `maxPerExpiry` times (8; the study averaged 2.6), so a wrong price cannot sell on every pass; a take found later than a day (Autopilot off, an outage, the strategy switched off and on) sells nothing. Such a playbook holds one trade per EXPIRY, not one in all: with one in all, this month's re-sold strangle would keep the next month's first sale out until that month was nearer than its range, and the month would never be traded. Built-in playbooks only. If the new sale cannot be made (no strike in range, no price, a premium under the fees) the log says "Not sold again after its take" with the reason, and the next pass tries again.
- `takeAfterEntryFee`: the take's level is the share of the premium plus the fee of the entry, so the trade is that share ahead after the fee, as the study counts it. The engine's profit and loss is before fees everywhere else, and the other strategies' takes stay as they were.
**Paper only.** A built-in strategy cannot hold a mandate (ADR-147): this one never trades live. It is switched on by a trader like any other; the build switches it on for nobody.
**What it rests on, said on the card.** Ten expiries, hourly prices, the nearest strike within 3 % of the spot, a fill cost measured on daily options only; the level was chosen after seeing five. Its figures are counted per expiry (every strangle sold on one expiry together), which no other card does, so the unit reads "an expiry".
**Consequences.** HC-TR-311 (the strategy), HC-TR-312 (the rule). No migration. 86 built-in strategies. GAPS #185.

## ADR-151 · The private copy runs on one server from images GitHub builds; the local database is restored once at go-live (2026-11-26)
**Context.** Roadmap item 13. The owner wants a private copy on the internet for a few people, not a launch: plans stay off sale (ADR-140) and no live order is placed. The research is `docs/research/deployment-providers.md`; the owner's decisions of 26 and 27 Nov 2026 are recorded at the end of its section 10. The earlier plan built the images on the server, which a small server cannot be trusted to do: the web build has run the development laptop out of memory, again on 26 Nov 2026.
**Decision.** (1) **One server, one compose file** (`deploy/compose.prod.yml`): Caddy, web, api, gateway, ingest, Postgres, Redis. An Azure virtual machine in Japan East (Tokyo), `Standard_B4as_v2` (4 vCPUs, 16 GB) with a static public IPv4, created 27 Nov 2026 in resource group `rg-hapiecoin`: the owner has sponsorship credit there, and it avoided OVHcloud's identity check. OVHcloud VPS-2 was the research's pick and stays the fallback when the credit ends. The owner's subscription is offered almost no sizes in Singapore; from Microsoft's network in Tokyo every source answered and Delta's socket hosts answered in about 15 ms (two samples). (2) **GitHub builds, the server pulls.** The image job in `ci.yml` builds the four images on every merge to `main`, tags them with the commit and pushes them to GitHub's registry; `RELEASE` in the server's settings names the commit to run. The web image is built for one deployment, because the feed address is fixed inside the browser bundle: it comes from the repository variable `GATEWAY_PUBLIC_URL`, and without it the web image is not pushed. (3) **The web image keeps `next start`.** Next's `standalone` output was rejected for now: a smaller image, but it changes `next.config.ts` and how the e2e suites start the app. (4) **Private by a password gate in Caddy** (`basic_auth` over the whole app), until it is shown to break sign-in or the installed app; the alternative, a sign-up switch in the api, is not built. (5) **The visitor's address.** Requests reach the api through the web container's rewrites, so the web container has a fixed private address and is the one peer in `TRUSTED_PROXY_IPS` (both spellings: the api sees `::ffff:`); Caddy drops `CF-Connecting-IP` and `X-Real-IP` from visitors, because the api believes them from a trusted peer. (6) **Releases by hand.** Backup, set `RELEASE`, pull, up. No deploy job. (7) **The database comes from the owner's laptop, once**, as the last step before the first start (`deploy/restore.sh`); the conditions are in `docs/deploy.md` §6.3. (8) `TRADING_DISABLED` defaults to true in the compose file; it stops new live entries, not a close or a cancel on a live strategy already in the database, so `DELTA_TRADING_REST_URL` is required and never defaulted (GAPS #186 j). (9) The image job needs the checks to pass (typecheck, lint, unit tests, build, budget), not the e2e: the owner decided on 27 Nov 2026 that a stale picture must not hold back a release, after the e2e of `main` had been red on 32 pictures through several merges. On a pull request it builds the four images without pushing, so a broken Dockerfile shows before the merge; without the variable the web image is built with a placeholder address and never pushed.
**Consequences.** `apps/web/Dockerfile`, `apps/ingest/Dockerfile`, `deploy/` (compose, Caddyfile, env.example, backup.sh, restore.sh), the image job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, the runbook in `docs/deploy.md` §6 (HC-SH-144). Tried on 26 Nov 2026 with dummy settings: the api, gateway and ingest images build; the api starts in production mode from the compose file, migrates and is healthy; a dump restored into an empty database with the same row count in all 42 tables, and the api started on it. The web image built on GitHub in the image job of the pull request (27 Nov 2026, 2.28 GB). Not tried: running the web image, the gate in front of sign-in and the installed app, anything on the server (GAPS #186). A merged migration is permanent once a server has run it. The move of the gateway to Delta's new public socket host is its own change.
